# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

Please choose versions by [Semantic Versioning](http://semver.org/).

* MAJOR version when you make incompatible API changes,
* MINOR version when you add functionality in a backwards-compatible manner, and
* PATCH version when you make backwards-compatible bug fixes.

## Unreleased

- fix: **`manager-liveness.py --arm` now emits the session's `wrapper` answer-reach, so one command arms and proves the answer route.** The wrapper leg was a `curl` plus a recorder call in `commands/steps/re-arm.md`, and across days of measurement managers ran the tool call and not those two commands — 4 fresh `mcp` reaches against **1** fresh `wrapper` reach on 2026-11-13. An instruction the fleet reliably does not follow is not a gate it can pass, so the arm emits it.
  ⚠️ **Best-effort and off the arm's own path.** The emission runs in a thread joined after `arm()` returns, because `arm()` is the liveness reading and its epochs are what `observed_period` measures: an inline call lengthened every arm-to-arm gap by its own latency and moved an observed period from 2250 s to 2251 s, breaking a test that asserts exact seconds (and adding ~100 s to the suite). A missing session id, an unreachable wrapper or a non-404/409 status all leave the arm intact and write no reach — the correct signal, since the session then has not shown it can reach the route.


## v0.296.1

- fix: **the answer-reach sentinel call now passes `agent_id`, so it stops being rejected before it can record anything.** Both step files documented `answer_permission(request_id, behavior)`; the tool's schema declares `agent_id`, `request_id` and `behavior` all required, so the MCP layer refused the call above the handler — no request reached the server and no `mcp` reach was written. Measured 2026-11-12: a manager following the step literally got `agent_id and request_id are required` and wrote no leg; retrying with a sentinel `agent_id` produced the foreign-park reply and the reach.
- fix: **every documented `answer_permission` call now names `agent_id`.** The same unusable two-argument form was in `commands/steps/worker-channels.md`, `commands/goal-answer.md`, `commands/goal-loop.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md` and `agents/goal-wrangler.md`; all now carry `agent_id`, so no page teaches a call the schema rejects.
- fix: **the steps no longer claim a real park cannot write the `mcp` reach.** That was true of the retired JS `server/supervisor.mjs` (real parks settle on another arm) and is false of the Go handler the fleet runs, which records the reach **on entry**, before it decodes arguments or looks the request id up. The sentinel is still required — a manager that answers no parks writes nothing — but the reason changed.

## v0.296.0

- feat: **`blocked-row-routing.py` decides whether a `🔴 BLOCKED` row needs a manager at all, and emits the routing record when it does.** `agents/fleet-drive.md` renders an `ESCALATION` row for every blocked session and `scripts/escalation-manager.py` resolves which manager owns it — but *whether to route* was still prose. Measured 2026-11-10: 4 of 8 blocked sessions named no recipient, so those rows were escalated to nobody, while 4 that already named an owner would have been double-routed. ⚠️ **It composes two shipped scripts and re-derives neither:** `closer-gate-lint.py`'s `owner_named()` owns "does this closer name an owner" — including the two-haystack split where `⏰ Ends:` sits on its own line — and `escalation-manager.py`'s `resolve()` owns "which manager", including the four-tier chain. A second copy of either is the drift `docs/fleet-surface.md` forbids. ⚠️ **`waiting on someone` is not an owner** — it names a wait, not a recipient — so it routes. ⚠️ **An unresolvable manager exits 2 rather than printing nothing**: silence would read as "an owner was found", the exact failure this ends. Change set: `scripts/blocked-row-routing.py`, `scripts/tests/test_blocked_row_routing.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.295.3



- fix: **the keeper's `restart-worker` budget is raised from 120 s to 400 s, because role resolution alone costs 170.6 s.** `restart-worker.py` resolves the session's role through `fleet-board.vault_index()`, which walks every vault's topic/goal/task pages and reads each task's frontmatter — measured 2026-11-12 at 170.6 s, on its own, before the kill or the spawn. At the old 120 s budget every restart reported `outcome UNKNOWN` and recovered nothing, and because the hang sits BEFORE the kill it aborted harmlessly, so no failure was visible. The new `RESTART_WORKER_TIMEOUT` constant is shared by both call sites — the answer-gated fresh restart and the `--absent` resume — since both pay the same index cost and a literal left behind on either is a live defect. Operator ruling 2026-11-12: raise the budget above the index cost rather than optimise the index here. Tests: `test_the_restart_budget_exceeds_the_measured_index_cost`. Change set: `scripts/fleet-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_keeper.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.295.2

- fix: **`stuck-idle.py` resolves a resumed session's transcript instead of giving up.** `resolve_transcript()` returned `None` whenever the glob matched more than one file, and a resumed session has one transcript per project dir it ran in — so every resumed headless worker read `unverifiable` for ever, the direction that never resumes anything. Found by exercising the deployed `v0.293.0` probe against a real session, where it rendered `transcript=none` on a session with two copies. Newest mtime is the only choice that answers the question the probe asks (*is this transcript moving*), and the same session then read `unchanged_rounds=2` with `in_flight=AskUserQuestion` → `not-stuck`, which is the probe correctly refusing to call a mid-call worker idle. Change set: `scripts/stuck-idle.py`, `scripts/tests/test_stuck_idle.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.295.1

- fix: **the parked row's page-less limb names the TRACKED set, so it stops claiming no page records a session a page does record.** `covered` is built from `out_rows` — the round's tracked set — so a session recorded on a page outside that set entered `unrecorded` too, and its limb read `(no page records this session)` for a session a page under `25 Tasks/` demonstrably records. A reader chasing that limb would create a page that already exists, and `unrecorded` became untrustworthy in exactly the direction it exists to answer. The limb now reads `(no tracked page records this session)`, and `resolution.unrecorded`, its row marker and the reader contract say the same. Measured 2026-11-12T09:02+01:00: **18 of 33** claiming rows named a session a page under `25 Tasks/` records (an earlier probe read 6 of 32 twenty minutes before, so the population grows with the fleet). ⚠️ **The fix relabels; it does not exclude.** Dropping these sessions from `unrecorded` would make a park on a page outside the tracked set invisible again — the regression the unrecorded path exists to prevent — so the row stays and only the claim is corrected. Change set: `scripts/round-readings.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py`, `agents/round-readings-reader.md`, `docs/handback-contract.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.295.0

- fix: **`restart-worker.py` gains `--allow-busy-lapse`, so the answer-gated `restart fresh` can recover a lapsed manager that is still busy.** The `busy-target` guard refuses any target whose registry status is not `idle`, and a lapsed manager is usually still busy — the 4.4 h stall on 2026-11-10 was a live manager answering messages with its context exhausted and writing no digest, which is exactly the shape the guard refuses. Measured 2026-11-12: 30 of 30 direct attempts refused `busy-target` because the Fleet Manager runs fleet rounds continuously and never presents `idle`. Operator ruling 2026-11-12: on the lapse card the operator's answer IS the judgement that separates a lapse from a slow round, so a blanket refusal overrides a decision the human has already made. The flag is confined by an argument check to `--reason lapse` AND `--fresh-prompt`, so it can never widen a code-staleness restart or a plain resume; every other refusal (held, headless, role-undetermined, ambiguous-pid) still applies, and the lift prints a line naming itself so the kill is never silent about why it was permitted. `fleet-keeper.py` passes it only from `restart_fresh_session`. Change set: `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/fleet-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_keeper.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.294.0

- feat: **`operator-ask-card.py` answers whether a parked session's operator ask has a live card.** ⚠️ **The instrument only — nothing calls it yet**, so the fleet round is unchanged by this PR and a follow-up wires it in. The `producer_id`-to-open-card join itself already exists (`server/open-cards.mjs`, `notify-gate.py`, `who-needs-me.py`); what is new here is the **closer classification** and the **cited-id rule**. 5 of 18 parked sessions measured 2026-11-10 had **no card at all**, so nothing could ever answer them while the operator believed the fleet was handling it. ⚠️ **The join is the SESSION, not an id parsed out of the closer:** a `pick` closer usually names no id, and its mirror card sits in the store under an id the closer never mentions — so a checker that reads the text and calls NO-CARD when it finds no id flags every mirror card as missing. The store item's `producer_id` is the asking session, and that is the key used. ⚠️ **Three cases, three rules:** a waiting-verb closer with no card for the session is NO-CARD; an id-less `pick` whose mirror card is in the store is CARD; a closer naming an id the store does not hold is NO-CARD **even when the session has another card open**, because a live card for the session does not make a dead cited id answerable. ⚠️ **An unreadable store is UNKNOWN, never NO-CARD** — NO-CARD accuses the operator of being unable to answer, and a failed read cannot support that. A `later (on …)` closer is NOT a waiting ask: it waits on a named event, so reporting it would accuse a correctly parked session. Change set: `scripts/operator-ask-card.py`, `scripts/tests/test_operator_ask_card.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/operator-ask-card/`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.293.1

- fix: **`closer-gate-lint` accepts a `🔴 BLOCKED` line over a `parked-wait` closer — `👤 You: later (on <trigger>): …`.** That is the form in which the operator has deferred to a named trigger, and `docs/closer-classes.md` calls it `parked-wait`; it names an owner exactly as `pick` does, and the Async State Closer treats the verb as a legal closer. `OWNER_RE` listed only the four operator verbs, so a blocked line over one was refused as `ownerless-blocked`: a false positive on a legal shape, firing on live sessions. One arm added, `|^\s*later\s*\(on\b`. ⚠️ **The false positive is specifically the PROSE-trigger `parked-wait` closer.** Recorded shapes whose trigger is a 32-hex card id (`later (on card b07ee746…): re-run the same approve-run command`) already passed, because the first arm matches hex; the records the old regex actually refused carry prose triggers — `later (on the \`CHANGES_REQUESTED\` gate shipping)`, `later (on either blocker landing)`, `later (on accept edits granted)`, `later (on a free interactive worker slot)`, `later (on \`sun.hm.benjamin-borbe.de\` reachable)` — six records over five distinct closers. ⚠️ **A bare `later`, or the phrase mid-sentence, still names no owner** — the negative controls pin that, so the arm cannot be widened into disabling the check. Change set: `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.293.0

- feat: restart a stuck headless worker from the fleet round. `scripts/stuck-idle.py` reads the three conjuncts — transcript unchanged for two rounds, no tool call in flight, no declared wait — and `scripts/stuck-heal.py` gains `--kind idle-no-wait` on its existing ladder, so the remedy stays one fresh process on the SAME session id and never becomes a second resume mechanism. `agents/fleet-drive.md` step 5c assigns the new `stuck` verdict to a headless `revive` row and renders a `RESUMES` line carrying `stuck-heal.py`'s action; `commands/steps/drive.md` executes it through `spawn_agent(resume=…)`. ⚠️ The gap this closes: `reap-idle-headless.py` closes an idle headless worker's process at 15 minutes, and nothing resumed it — `close-idle-tab.py` refuses a headless row `not-tab`, so step 5b could only mark it `unverifiable`, and the task kept open boxes with no live owner. The two-round count is carried in from the drive ledger's `transcript_mtime`, never re-derived in prose. ⚠️ An unreadable task file reads `unknown`, never `no wait`, and a pending tool call is cleared only by its own `tool_use_id`.

- feat: recover a dead spawner's parked prompt (E13). `adopt-orphans.py` already gives an exited manager's live workers an owner, but a claim routes only their NEXT gate — the prompt a worker is parked on right now was held by the spawner's own server process, so it has no responder at all and the worker waits out the 15-minute auto-deny before continuing on a denial nobody was asked about. The script now reads each adopted worker's registry `status` and prints `  parked: <sid8> -- its prompt died with the spawner; resume it`; `commands/steps/adopt.md` acts on that line with `spawn_agent(resume=…)`, the same resume path the stuck ladder uses. ⚠️ **Headless only (`entrypoint: sdk-cli`)** — a TAB worker parked on its own tab is waiting on a person looking at that tab, so its transport outlived the spawner and a resume would put a second process on a live conversation. ⚠️ The records are read through `read_registry_entries`, not `read_registry`'s normalised `{pid, status, name, alive}` view, which drops `entrypoint` and would make the population read empty while looking correct. `waiting` is the registry's own word for parked (`manager-attention-watch.py`'s `registry:waiting`); `busy` is mid-turn and `idle` is between turns, so neither is reported.


## v0.292.1

- docs: **the resume's `cwd` rule now carries an orphan branch, so path B can be used for the case it exists for.** The rule named one source — the session registry (`~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json`, field `cwd`) — and refused a session whose record carries no `cwd`. ⚠️ **Those two requirements cannot both hold for an orphan, and the collision is structural rather than incidental:** a registry entry is **deleted when its session exits** (this repo's `CLAUDE.md` registry table states it for the `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` row; `scripts/who-needs-me.py` and `scripts/session-exit-record.py` restate it), and the auto-resume gate's own liveness clause reads *no live id on any id* (`agents/goal-drive.md`, step 3) — so the source the recipe names is absent **by construction** exactly when the recipe is needed. Measured **2026-10-10** on this host: **38 of 38** sampled registry records carried a live pid (no stale residue to fall back on), and **3162 of 3204** top-level session transcripts had no live registry entry. The rule now resolves from the first of **two** sources — the registry, then the session's transcript (`find ~/.claude/projects -name "<session-id>.jsonl"`, its **first** entry carrying a `cwd`) — and the refusal is corrected to guard a **cwd-less record** rather than a missing one: a record that exists without a `cwd` is still refused, while a session with **no record** selects the transcript. ⚠️ **The transcript's first `cwd`, never its last and never the project-directory name, and both alternatives are rejected on measurement rather than taste:** the first cwd-carrying entry equals the registry's `cwd` for **38 of 40** live sessions (one exception a symlink alias — `Personal` → `private-personal` — the other a genuine drift, the transcript recording the launch directory), while the **last** entry differs for **21 of 39**, because it records where the session had `cd`'d to — several of those landing outside every configured vault that item 8 refuses; the project-directory name is a lossy re-encoding (`-` is both its separator and a legal path character) that can carry a symlink alias. ⚠️ **Three surfaces that restated the rule now point at its single home, and one of them was restating a source that could not work at all:** the path-B recipe above it, `commands/open.md` § 3.1, and `agents/goal-drive.md` step 3's `To resume` hand-over — that last one being the actor the auto-resume gate itself runs in, whose rows are orphans *by the gate's own precondition*, so it was the site where the missing field would actually have been read. § 3.1 read the value from `session-liveness.py --list --json`, which lists **live sessions only** — structurally empty for the resume branch it sits in. ⚠️ **Every pointer cites it as *"resume decisions item 3"*, never a bare `item 3`.** § Spawn a worker holds **two** numbered lists, and a bare `item N` in this document means the **readiness ladder**, whose item 3 is an unrelated check — so a pointer written the obvious way lands the reader on the wrong item and breaks the consolidation it exists to make. The vault-side copy (`65 Runbooks/Supervisor - Install and Operate.md` § Step 4) lives outside this repo and is not pinnable from here. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `commands/open.md`, `agents/goal-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.292.0

- feat: **the flagged-open leg records what it opened PER KIND, and the round's legs verdict becomes one copyable string.** A single `flagged-open: unactioned: <n> operator-flagged` cannot say which kind the operator's flag reached: a round that opened three topics and a round that opened nothing printed the same line, so a flag could be silently unhonoured and the digest still read clean. `fleet-legs.py` gains `--per-kind "tasks=<o>/<u>/<e> goals=… topics=…"`, writing `flagged-open: tasks=<o>/<u>/<e> goals=<o>/<u>/<e> topics=<o>/<u>/<e> <marker>` — three numbers per kind, `opened + declined == eligible`, refused at the writer **and** at the reader when the arithmetic does not hold, so a mis-derived count is a failed write rather than a plausible line. ⚠️ **The guard reconciles independently for goals and topics only** — there `<o>` is the invocations and both `<u>` and `<e>` are counted from the selector's output; for tasks `<e>` is `<o> + <u>` by construction and the discriminator is that `<o>` is the batch's own footer. The per-kind line **supersedes** the single count rather than joining it: both cover `operator-flagged`, and two lines for one key is the duplicate `--check` refuses. It also gains `--status`, which prints the round's verdict as one string — `legs: OK — …`, `legs: INCOMPLETE — <reason>`, `legs: UNKNOWN — <reason>` — and exits 0 / 1 / 2, because a render told to "render it as INCOMPLETE" is a sentence a model is asked to remember and a softened verdict is how a failed round reads as a clean one. `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md` now names an `INCOMPLETE` round as a **failed** round whose verdict must reach the round's closer, while still never braking the arm (`fleet-keeper.py` would turn a refused arm into a crash loop). Change set: `scripts/fleet-legs.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_legs.py`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md`, `agents/fleet-flagged-open-actor.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **`claim-dead` is routed to the Fleet Manager too — the third sibling of `unowned` and `dead-manager`.** When a live manager recorded a claim on a pane and then died, the claim block emitted to **every** manager: nobody can act on the pane, and the old rule handed it to all of them. That is the same defect the other two classes had, reached through a third door. ⚠️ **It did not appear in either SC1 window measured on 2026-11-11**, and that is the argument for routing it now rather than leaving it to fire on some later hour and fail the same criterion the other two already failed — the three classes now share one limb, so a fourth cannot be added to one and forgotten in another. `CLAIM_DEAD` joins `UNOWNED` and `DEAD_MANAGER` as a routed reason (`claim-dead-fleet`), landing in the same `dropped(fleet-routed)` bucket; a **live** claim still returns `claimed` and this watcher's own claim still returns `own-claim`, because the routed limb runs below that block. The two operator docs that still described the old broadcast — `commands/steps/round-tail.md` and `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md` — now name all three classes and the renamed bucket, and `counts_line`'s `dropped(fleet-routed)` bucket gained its first test, pinning all three classes plus the fact that a routed **emit** (the Fleet Manager receiving the gate) is not counted as a drop. Change set: `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **the tail beat's marker now lives in its own `<slug>.tail` file, so a session still running a pre-upgrade copy of `manager-liveness.py` can no longer erase it.** The marker was a third column on the `<slug>.arms` ring, and a pre-upgrade writer rebuilds that ring from two-tuples — `(prior + [(time.time(), interval)])[-ARMS_KEEP:]` then `f"{epoch:.0f} {iv}\n"` (`0.263.0:839-843`) — so every arm it took dropped the column from every prior line. Measured 2026-11-10 on `goal--git-ai-sync-runs-as-one-go-binary`: 90 minutes of continuous ticking with a real two-beat gap pattern still left exactly one marked arm; six of 123 rings carried a marker, each exactly one. Consequence: `observed_period()`'s round-to-round exclusion was structurally unreachable during a mixed-version fleet, and the two-consecutive-rounds probe shipped in v0.264.1 could never fire. `arm()` now writes two columns and records the tail epoch in `<slug>.tail`; `read_arm_pairs()` reads the marker from that store and still honours a legacy third column, because dropping it would turn every pre-move tail beat into a head and manufacture the false STALE the marker exists to remove — and the first arm after the move carries each legacy column marker into the store in the same write that strips the column. The store keeps only epochs still present in the ring, so it stays bounded by `ARMS_KEEP` — and those epochs survive the two-tuple rebuild, which is what makes the marker outlive it. Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **the fleet digest reports a session's transcript age, and `stalled` needs both clocks.** The digest's `<age>` column rendered the **task file's** mtime age, not the transcript's: measured 2026-10-23 on round `20:09:07Z`, all five `stalled` rows matched their task-file mtime to the minute (56m/59m/37m/40m/41m) while four of them had written their transcripts minutes before the round — so a session in a long turn, which edits no task page, was rendered as 37–59 minutes dead. `scripts/who-needs-me.py` gains `--ages <ids…>` → `<sid8>\t<seconds>\t<human>\t<quiet|active|ambiguous|unknown>`, reading the same memoized `session_transcript_age()` the board's `Inactive` column uses and formatting through `fleet-sessions.py`'s `human_age()` rather than a second formatter; an ambiguous 8-char prefix — or an argument that is not an id fragment at all, which would otherwise be globbed as a pattern — reports `ambiguous`, and a transcript read that raised reports `unknown`, never `quiet` in either case, because an unresolvable or unreadable id read as quiet would render a live session as stalled. `STALL_TRANSCRIPT_SECONDS` (30 min — two sweeps at the 15-min cadence, sharing its value with `manager-predispatch.py`'s `STUCK_SECONDS` and nothing else) is the transcript half of the class, so `stalled` now requires the task mtime unchanged for ≥2 sweeps **and** a quiet transcript; `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` states the call in step 4, the both-clocks condition in the classification table, and the rendered clock in the payload row; that body's growth (43 118 → 43 658 B) is baselined in `scripts/budget-baseline.json` rather than funded by cutting prose, since the added clause is the new flag's only documentation and the file carries no redundant passage to trade for it. `docs/sweep-output.md` records the two clocks and which one is rendered.

## v0.291.0

- fix: **an expired `⏰ Ends:` declaration now re-drives its `parked-wait` row, instead of being suppressed by the class that was correct only while the wait was live.** The two paths were each right alone and neither knew the other: `scripts/declared-wait.py` already rates a stale declaration `expired` ("not re-rendered for `MAX_AGE_S` and naming no future clock") and the sweep already acts on it — an expired declaration is not a wait, so the row falls through to `parked` and becomes a drive candidate — but the drive leg then rated the row `unverifiable` because its closer class is `parked-wait`, and sent nothing. Measured 2026-11-11 on round `2026-11-11T21-29-05Z`: session `64a0e8f1` sat `idle · 3h · parked`, its newest `Stop` `ts` 2 h 53 m stale with no future clock, and `.drive` line 8 read `unverifiable` with `.drive` line 1040 recording `closer class parked-wait (unchanged)`. `closer-verdict.py` gains a SECOND input: `--session <id or 8-char prefix>` resolves the session's declaration through `declared-wait.py` and widens `parked-wait` to `revive` when it is `expired`. ⚠️ **The widening is confined, and the static map is resolved first** — only `parked-wait` + `expired` changes the answer; `ask`, `none` and `routine` keep their verdicts even on an expired declaration, and a live, absent, unreadable or prefix-ambiguous declaration keeps `unverifiable` (the safe direction, and the same rule `declared-wait.py` states for a record with no readable timestamp). So `test_only_routine_revives` still pins exactly one reviving class, and the dynamic widening is pinned separately. ⚠️ **The fixture is the recorded row and a SECOND one carries a different observable** — a branch keyed on the recorded `make precommit` string passes the first pair and fails the second, which is why both exist. Change set: `scripts/closer-verdict.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_verdict.py`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `docs/closer-classes.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.290.3

- fix: **the fleet wake refuses a subject that is date-gated asleep, so a sleeping manager is no longer nudged every round.** `pane-wake-guard.py` skipped only a declared machine wait (`🟡 WAITING` / `⏰ Ends:`), and a sleeping subject's pane shows an empty composer with no `👤 You:` ask — indistinguishable from a lapse — so `pane-wake.py` typed the wake into it. Measured 2026-11-12 on v0.288.0: a goal manager reading `SLEEPING until 2026-11-12T01:22:59+01:00 (3467s remaining)` returned `verdict: "wake"` with the full nudge text on a dry run. The guard gains `subject_state`, a value the caller reads and passes in (`verdict()` stays pure), and returns `skip:sleeping` before the screen is considered; `pane-wake.py` resolves the session's subject from `~/.claude/state/worker-manager/<session-id>.json` and reads `manager-liveness.py --state` once per wake, reusing that value in `send()`'s re-check. `scripts/manager-wake.py` — the sweep-gate wake, which already holds the subject and tier — passes the same state, so both wake paths refuse a sleeper. ⚠️ **A failed state read is `None`, never `"OK"`** — it falls through to the screen verdict, so a broken `manager-liveness` cannot silently stop every fleet wake. The triage's verdict→bucket mapping gains `skip:sleeping` → EXTERNAL (a sleeper waits on its own clock); without it every sleeping manager would render as `STUCK` and the run's `STUCK == 0` green condition would go permanently red. Change set: `scripts/pane-wake-guard.py`, `scripts/pane-wake.py`, `scripts/manager-wake.py`, `scripts/tests/test_pane_wake_guard.py`, `scripts/tests/test_pane_wake.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_wake.py`, `commands/steps/fleet-triage-verify.md`, `commands/fleet-triage.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.290.2

- fix: **the nudge gate joins a `.sent` record to its drafted `TO:` recipients on the normalised name, so a decorated record entry no longer reads as a lost send.** Measured 2026-11-11 on round `2026-11-11T20-56-50Z`: the round's `.sent` carried `⚙ Serve the git-rest Protocols vault-api Clients Still Need` while the payload's `TO:` line carried the same name **undecorated**, and `artifact_failures()`'s raw `name in drafted` intersection reported `nudges: drafted 6 · sent 5 · missing 1 · unexpected sent 1`, rc=1 — a lost send *and* an over-send manufactured out of one correctly-sent nudge, which the round then "repaired" by rewriting its own record five seconds later. Both sides now strip a leading `^[^\w(]+` run and casefold, mirroring `fleet-board.py`'s `_norm_session_name`; the `drafted` list stays raw and first-seen-deduped, so two genuinely distinct recipients are not collapsed into one denominator. ⚠️ The reproduction is a **constructed** fixture, not the round's own `.sent` — that file was overwritten at 21:20:46Z and already read `missing 0` against the unfixed gate, so it could never have served as a "before". Change set: `scripts/check-drive-nudges-sent.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_drive_nudges_sent.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.290.1

- fix: **`next-focus-answer.py` unwraps `shlex.quote`'s apostrophe escape when the shell has already stripped the outer quoting.** Two payload shapes reach the store and they need different handling: the whole payload still quoted (one `shlex` token, so `shlex.split` returns it unquoted), and the outer quoting removed by the shell while the `'"'"'` escape survives **literally**. The second shape splits into many tokens, so the single-token branch never fired and the escape stayed in the row name — measured 2026-11-12 on store item `1127462d`, whose payload begins `A Worker'"'"'s Gate …` and splits into 14 tokens; the row was refused even though its page exists, so the operator's `add it to the plan` answer was dropped. `unquote_payload()` now falls back to unwrapping `'"'"'`, the only form `shlex.quote` emits, so the substitution is exact rather than a guess. The test builds the escape into both the name and the path, exactly as the live payload carries it. Change set: `scripts/next-focus-answer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_next_focus_answer.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the task lookup uses ripgrep when it exists, so the terminal-task guard is no longer silently inert.** `grep -rl` over the 11 375-file `private-personal/25 Tasks` measured **19.6 s** on 2026-11-12 — past `_grep_task`'s 15 s timeout — and a timeout is indistinguishable from "no task claims this session". On the close side that is the false `task-unresolved` the `find_task` docstring records; on the resume side `task is None` reads as "no anchor, allow the resume", so the `task-terminal` guard returned 0 for a session whose task was plainly `completed` (session `2e540d65`). `rg -l -F` over the same directory: **0.7 s**, and the guard then refuses in 1.8 s. Falls back to grep when ripgrep is absent. Change set: `scripts/close-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- test: **`poll --answered-at`'s opt-in default is pinned by a test, and `docs/restart-worker.md` § `--absent` now states the unresolvable-launcher outcomes accurately.** The flag's contract is that the default output stays byte-identical for every existing consumer (`pod-attention.py`, `answered-attribution.py`, the hooks) — verified by hand against the live store, but not pinned, so a later change could break them silently. Four tests now hold it at two layers: the function-level pair fails if the flag is ignored or made the default inside `cmd_poll`, and a CLI-level case pins argparse's default and the `answered_at=args.answered_at` wiring, which the function-level pair cannot see. Separately, § `--absent` said *"An unresolvable vault or launcher is an `❌ error:` operational failure, never a refusal"* — true for a launcher unresolvable **for one session** (exit 1), but silent on the `environment-fault` / exit 3 row the exit-code table has declared since `e981c855`. ⚠️ **Exit 3 is not reachable on this path at all:** `vault_configs()` returns `None` when `vault-cli` is missing, before any launcher is resolved, so a broken caller environment reports the exit-1 `❌ error:` naming the vault rather than the missing binary. The paragraph now says that, and names exit 3 as reachable on the normal restart path where the cwd comes from the registry entry. Change set: `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py`, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **The idle-reaper tick's summary line reconciles its counts against the lines it wrote, and names the trigger.** It printed `candidates=<n> closed=<n> refused=<n>`, a second tally that could drift from the per-session lines. The accounting now derives from those rows (`summary_line()`), so `closed <n> past the 15-min trigger` cannot disagree with the `outcome=closed` lines in the same tick. **Both close counts are derived, not literal:** each row carries a `trigger`, and a close is *past the 15-min trigger* when its row carries `TRIGGER_IDLE` and *with no wait* when it does not — so a future close path that skips `IDLE_FLOOR` moves the second count instead of silently redefining the first. Today the second count is 0 because every close this tick can perform is the 15-minute one, which is a measured fact rather than a hardcoded constant. `refused <n>` is restored (the first draft of this line dropped it), and each per-session line now prints its `trigger=` so the counts are checkable against it. `scripts/idle-reaper-tick.py`, `scripts/tests/test_idle_reaper_tick.py`.

## v0.290.0

- feat: add a session-independent `UNCLAIMABLE` digest section to `fleet-sweep-reader`. A task whose `target_vault` is empty or absent is claimed by no controller — the non-owning one drops it as cross-vault traffic and the legacy-default one cannot recreate a file already at the title path — so no pod is scheduled and nothing reports it, which is why the row cannot hang off the session roster. `scripts/unclaimable-tasks.py` scans the agent runtime vaults and prints recent rows, a historical count and an undated bucket; since `agent-task-controller` v0.26.12 the create path refuses the empty field outright, so a **recent** hit means a producer still omits it and its PR is stalling silently. ⚠️ The window is `created_date` else `job_started_at`, never file mtime — measured 2026-11-11, an mtime window read 9,624 "recent" when the true number was 0, because mtime is the clone date on most of these files.

## v0.289.0

- fix: **the frozen-tree predicate's second conjunct actually reads the ledger.** `OPEN_ITEMS_DIR` was derived as `os.path.join(STATE_DIR, "open-items")`, and `STATE_DIR` is `~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch` — this script's own store, which has no `open-items` directory and never did. So `open_asked_of_you()` returned `[]` on **every** call and the predicate silently collapsed to mtime-only, which is precisely the failure the gate exists to prevent: an mtime-only predicate answers FROZEN on a tree that still carries an unanswered operator question. It failed silently because a missing ledger is a legitimate empty answer, so nothing distinguished "the operator owes nothing" from "I looked in the wrong directory". The path is now its own constant at the ledger's real home (`~/.claude/state/open-items`, overridable via `SUPERVISOR_OPEN_ITEMS_DIR`), and `LedgerPathDefault` pins the default — the earlier tests passed only because `setUp` overrode the attribute with a tempdir, which is why the defect shipped. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **a gated tick still prints the table, under a marker that says so.** `manager-receipt.py --render-cached` replays the cached `~/.claude/state/manager-receipts/<slug>.sweep` payload with a **fresh timestamped marker** for the current tick. The operator's 2026-09-15 (*"I like this table every time"*) and 2026-09-20 (*"moves to a FILE, not to stdout"*) rulings require the table on every tick, including one where the gate suppressed the reader — but replaying the payload verbatim prints a header claiming a time this tick did not run, and that makes a dead loop and a gated one look identical, which is the ambiguity the marker exists to remove. The payload's own first line is therefore **replaced, never kept**, and the replacement marker names the gate explicitly rather than claiming goal or slot counts this tick never read. A missing payload exits 1 rather than printing an empty table, because an empty render reads as "the table printed and had no rows". `commands/steps/topic-sweep.md` now calls it instead of describing the render in prose. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `commands/steps/topic-sweep.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **a round can now record a leg the frozen-tree predicate suppressed, and both step files gate on it.** `manager-receipt.py` gains `--gate` (with `gated_path`/`read_gated`/`gate()`): it records that the predicate decided a step was **never owed this tick**, and `_skips()` excuses that step on the same round-scoped window as a paid one, with the same non-finite tamper guard. ⚠️ **`.gated` is a different claim from `.abandoned` and is kept in its own file** — an abandonment pays a leg that *was* owed, a gate records that the leg *was not* owed, and collapsing the two would make "skipped by design" indistinguishable from "skipped a leg it owed", which is exactly the distinction the round's receipt exists to draw. ⚠️ `--gate` is deliberately **not** fleet-scoped, unlike `--abandon`: the predicate applies to every tier, and the goal/topic tier — where `--abandon` refuses because no debt file is written — is where it is most needed. `commands/steps/round-readings.md` step 1 and `commands/steps/topic-sweep.md` now evaluate `manager-predispatch.py --predicate` at the round's head: exit 0 records the gate and dispatches nothing, exit 1 dispatches as written. Both state that gating on the mtime half alone is wrong (the second conjunct stops the gate hiding an unpublished `asked-of-you`), and the sweep one repeats that **the table still prints** from the cached payload with a fresh marker — the gate touches the leg, never the render. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `commands/steps/round-readings.md`, `commands/steps/topic-sweep.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **the frozen-tree predicate is implemented, and it answers both conjuncts.** `manager-predispatch.py --predicate` evaluates the runbook's rule — *"no tracked file's mtime has moved for >= 2 cadence intervals (30 min at the 15-min default), and the tick's content has already left the machine"* — which had no implementation anywhere, so nothing could gate on it. The mtime conjunct reads the tracked set `resolve_tracked()` already resolves. The second conjunct had no instrument at all, so it is read here as: this session's ledger carries no **OPEN** `asked-of-you` (`~/.claude/state/open-items/<session-id>.json`, kind `asked-of-you`, state `open`). ⚠️ **The two conjuncts are different claims and neither stands in for the other** — an mtime-only predicate answers FROZEN on a tree that still carries an unanswered operator question, which is exactly the failure `Manager Session.md:488` names: *"never arm over an unpublished `asked-of-you`"*. ⚠️ An unreadable or absent ledger is `[]`, never fail-closed: a session that never used the ledger asked the operator nothing, and failing closed there would freeze the gate forever on every such session. ⚠️ This is **not** the digest `--check` computes — that one hashes content and liveness and deliberately excludes raw mtime, and `round-quiet.md` states the two are *"a different predicate, not a second spelling of this one"*; neither may be read as evidence for the other. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.288.0

- fix: **a closer that declares a wait on a pull request must name the repository, and the Stop-hook lint now refuses one that does not.** A `⏰ Ends:` naming a PR is the shape the round's re-probe exists to answer — but the probe resolves `owner/repo#N` and a `github.com/owner/repo/pull/N` URL, so a bare `PR #N` leaves it a number with no repo, renders `unprobeable`, and the session never learns its wait cleared. Measured 2026-11-11 against the live attention store: **36 of 324 declarations named a PR and only 4 carried a repo** — and 3 of those 4 were already `MERGED` while still rendering as parked, which is the defect in its own right. ⚠️ **Both bare shapes are real and they are not the same string**: the store carries `PR #516` *and* `PR 738`, so a matcher built to `PR #N` alone passes a declaration that is just as unprobeable. ⚠️ **`repo#N` without an owner is refused too** — the owner would have to be guessed, and guessing it lands the probe on another org's identically numbered PR. ⚠️ **A slot naming BOTH a bare and a qualified PR is still refused.** The obvious presence-based rule ("pass if any qualified reference appears") is wrong and was caught on review: `wait-reprobe.py` calls `observable-reprobe.observables()` with **no repo**, and that function only merges a bare `PR #N` into a qualified one when a repo resolves — so `PR #516 on bborbe/claude-supervisor#516` yields two observables, one `repo: None`, and `verdict()` ANDs it to `unprobeable`. The matcher now blocks any bare match that no qualified span covers. The check is independent of the session-bound one, not a branch of it: `PR #12` matches the `external-probe` durable class, so it passed every existing check while staying unprobeable. Fail-open, like every other path here. ⚠️ **Fixture `36-ends-pr-watch-compliant.jsonl` was REQUALIFIED rather than flipped** to the negative case — it is the suite's only positive PR-wait fixture, so it now carries `bborbe/claude-supervisor#12` and keeps proving a compliant closer passes, while five new fixtures cover the bare hash form, the bare no-hash form, the ownerless `repo#N`, and both qualified shapes. A second test asserts the positive half directly, because a lint that rejects every PR-bearing closer would satisfy the negative test alone and refuse every compliant closer on the machine. Deliberately **not** covered: a bare `#N` with no `PR` word — `#` also introduces issue numbers and headings, and a false block on a compliant closer costs more than the widening saves. Change set: `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_drive_state.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/{36-ends-pr-watch-compliant,46-ends-pr-bare-hash,47-ends-pr-bare-nohash,48-ends-pr-repo-only,49-ends-pr-qualified,50-ends-pr-url}.jsonl`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.287.6

- fix: **every close refusal names the session id, and the resume guard's token is the first line again.** A live round on v0.287.5 produced four close-refusal lines of which two could not be joined to any close line: `task-unresolved` already carried the id (`no page carries <id>`), but `task-not-terminal` and the page verdicts named only a task or page path. The refusal site now prefixes the id when the detail does not already carry it. Separately, `restart-worker.py`'s terminal-task guard had been placed after the header print, so its `task-terminal` token was the sixth line rather than the first — every other refusal in that script prints its token first, and callers branch on the first line. Measured 2026-11-11. Change set: `scripts/close-worker.py`, `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.287.5

- fix: **both halves of a close/refusal pair now name the full session id, so the round can join them.** `close-worker.py`'s header printed `session <sid[:8]>` and `commands/steps/drive.md` prints that header as the round's close record; on the other side, `restart-worker.py`'s `task-terminal` refusal named no session id at all (only a task basename and status), and its own `--absent` and normal-path headers printed `sid[:8]`. Neither line could be joined to the other, so a refusal read as an unrelated event rather than the sequel to a close. All four sites now carry the full id. Measured 2026-11-11 on session `2e540d65`: closed at 07:18:34Z, refused at 07:33:55Z. Tests pin the full id on the close header, the refusal detail, and both resume headers. Change set: `scripts/close-worker.py`, `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **the flagged-open count says whether the batch ran.** `flagged-open: unactioned: <n> operator-flagged` carried two different facts with no way to tell them apart — the leg ran `/supervisor:open --flagged` and left N rows, or the leg never invoked the batch and N rows are waiting — and the second is the read-only failure `commands/steps/flagged-open.md` exists to remove. Observed 2026-11-11: a session read the line as evidence the leg had executed and reported it as such. `--count` on `operator-flagged` now requires `--invoked` or `--not-invoked` and renders `(batch invoked)` / `(batch not invoked)`; the marker is refused on every other key, the same way `--floor` / `--exact` are confined to `dead-workers`; and `--check` rejects a digest carrying a bare `operator-flagged` count or a marker on a key with no batch, so the invariant holds on the read path and not only on the write. Change set: `scripts/fleet-legs.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_legs.py`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `agents/fleet-flagged-open-actor.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.287.4

- fix: **the `dead-manager` class is routed to the Fleet Manager too, because it is the same defect reached through a different hop.** `spawner_verdict` emitted when the pane's spawner IS a manager that is no longer live, on the reasoning that "a dead manager cannot act on its own gate, so nothing duplicates this wake" — right about duplication, wrong about cost: it hands the gate to **every** manager. Measured 2026-11-11 in SC1's after-window on a goal manager's ledger: after the `unowned` class was routed, **this branch was the only foreign wake left** — 11 records of a single orphaned worker pane (`088ab282`, spawned 2026-11-07, ledger row still `running`, spawner `837b471a` not live). `route_to_fleet()` now takes the reason as an argument and appends `-fleet`, so `unowned` → `unowned-fleet` and `dead-manager` → `dead-manager-fleet`: the routed row still says WHICH class it was, while the counts line buckets them together as `dropped(fleet-routed)` — one field answering "how much left this feed for the Fleet Manager" rather than "which hop found it", with the specific reason kept on the row for `--explain`, `--json` and the ledger. The fail-open limb is per class: with no live Fleet Manager each reason keeps its own spelling, so a routed row is never confused with an un-routed one. Change set: `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.287.3

- fix: **an operator's answer on a Stop-hook MIRROR card now reaches the session that asked.** A mirror is a gate the EVENT LOG holds and the STORE never carried — the class `who-needs-me.py`'s `board_missing_gates()` returns — so `attention-answer.py`'s `fetch_item()` 404'd on its id and **that exception escaped `cmd_answer` before any `TARGET:` line was printed**. The operator's approve reached nobody and the session kept waiting. `cmd_answer` now treats a 404 as the mirror case: `mirror_gate()` **folds** the event log — an `open` line (`type: "open"`) with no `close` anywhere (`closed_by`) — and resolves the id to the record's own `session_id`, the same key `producer_id` carries for a store item, written on every item `open_item()` mints. The answer is delivered straight to that session with no store write, because there is no store record to answer. ⚠️ **Two refusals, both safety rather than polish:** a mirror whose card has already **closed** is refused — the store path has a compare-and-set for that and the mirror path has none, so the fold is its only guard — and a **`permission`** card is refused outright, because a relay cannot release another session's parked gate and that would be permission laundering; only `kind == "question"` routes. ⚠️ **Only a 404 falls back, and only for an id that is genuinely a mirror card**: any other store error still fails loudly, an id in neither place reports `FAILED`, and a 404 for an id that is not a mirror keeps the documented `FAILED:` signal an older store's missing `/attempt` endpoint relies on. The answer marker is written **on this branch** only when the producing session is actually deliverable, so a mirror card nobody could receive is never attributed an answer. Change set: `scripts/attention-answer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_answer.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.287.2

- fix: **an unowned gate is routed to the live Fleet Manager instead of waking every manager, and the feed arm finally keeps a per-decision ledger.** `unowned` used to emit to every manager on the rule that "unresolvable is never foreign" — right in principle, because an unowned gate might be the reader's own, and wrong for the producer class this defect is named for: a `Monitor` producer (`sweep-gate`, `fleet-keeper`, `drive-escalate`) has no spawner and no session id, which are exactly the two inputs that produce `unowned` (`:789`, `:816`), so no manager can ever be its owner and every manager inherits all of its traffic. Measured 2026-11-11 on one goal manager: **more than ten foreign wakes in roughly two hours**, each a full model round, which kept the REPL busy and starved the round cadence itself — `manager-liveness.py --state` read `STALE age=11643s limit=2497s`, no round in 3 h 14 min against a 15-minute cadence. `route_unowned()` now sends the class to exactly one destination and pins four answers: a goal manager **drops** it (named `unowned-fleet`, so `--explain` and the counts line say where it went), the Fleet Manager **emits** it, a run with no `--self` emits it (a diagnostic probe that printed nothing would read as "this pane is fine"), and — the safety property — **no live Fleet Manager falls open to every manager**, because an unresolvable destination is not a licence to silence a gate. `fleet_manager()` reads the registration `scripts/fleet-register.py` writes (the fleet tier manages no subject page, so `owner.py`'s page resolution cannot answer this), newest `resolved_at` first, and **skips a registration whose session is not live**: routing the class to a dead session would take the gate out of every goal manager's feed and deliver it nowhere, which is strictly worse than the flood. The new branch only re-routes an `unowned` emit — a resolved `peer-manager` drop is a fact and is never re-routed, the same asymmetry as "a claim may only ADD a drop". `gate-owner-filter.py` also now appends one record per **decision** to `~/.claude/state/manager-attention-watch/<session-id>/feed-emissions.jsonl`, carrying `ts`, `decision`, `reason`, `item_id`, `pane`, `session_id`, `spawner`, and the gate's **resolved** `task`/`goal`. The feed arm had no history at all: `gate-feed/<sid>.json` is a pruned current-state map (`emit_decisions` drops every entry whose pane left the kept set), so "how many foreign gates did this manager's feed carry in the last hour" was unanswerable. ⚠️ **`ts` is load-bearing**: the ledger lives as long as the manager session, so without it every count is a whole-file count and a `0` could come from before the fix it is meant to certify. The task/goal join reads `session-scope.py`'s existing reverse index (`Scope.sessions` is already `{session_id: [Entry]}` over every task page in every configured vault) rather than walking the vault a second time, and a session stamped on two pages or none records `null`/`null` — unattributed, never silently attributed. The counts line gains `dropped(unowned-fleet)` as a tenth field. ⚠️ **The routed limb sits BELOW the claim block, deliberately**: an earlier cut returned it above hop 3b and the claims, which silently broke adoption — a manager that had adopted a spawnerless pane (`own-claim`) lost the gate the moment a Fleet Manager was live, and a live peer manager's own gate was relabelled from `peer-manager-own` to `unowned-fleet`. The file's rule is that a claim may only ADD a drop, so a new drop must not be inserted ahead of one. ⚠️ **The ledger is bounded** — `trim_emissions()` keeps the newest lines once the file passes 5 MB, because an append-only file that lives as long as the manager session measured ~25 k records/day (~6 MB/day) with no rotation, unlike its pruned sibling `gate-feed/<sid>.json`. ⚠️ **The suite now points `GATE_EMISSIONS_DIR` and `SUPERVISOR_WORKER_MANAGER_DIR` at a tempdir before importing the module** — without it every subprocess test passing `--self` appended to the operator's live state tree, and every `evaluate_panes` read the real fleet registrations. `log_emissions` also catches `TypeError`/`ValueError` (a malformed store record raises before the write, and the call is on the wake path), and the poll writes the ledger AFTER the `LIVENESS` line so the heartbeat always lands first. Change set: `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.287.1

- docs: **`session-liveness.py`'s docstring names the one class of live session the `mcp-timer` poster never re-stamps, and pins its verdict as UNKNOWN.** The stamp is posted by `claude-wrapper`'s heartbeat poster (`pkg/heartbeat.go`) every 30 s for every session the session registry lists with a live pid, and it deliberately skips any session id the spawn ledger records terminal (`done`/`error`) so a finished worker does not read live. A session id reused by a later resume inherits that terminal row — a resume writes no new ledger row — so the poster never posts for it again and its row freezes at the last POST while its process runs. Measured 2026-11-11: `1c05c414` registry-alive on pid 81794 with its endpoint row frozen at `2026-11-09T22:09:51Z`, its ledger row `done` since `2026-11-07T08:49:12Z`. The verdict for the class is UNKNOWN, never ABSENT, and the registry read in `_absent_licensed` is what keeps it there. Docstring only — no behaviour change; the verdict fix itself shipped in v0.275.0. Change set: `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.287.0

- feat: **the goal manager's `ScheduleWakeup` cadence is removed, so the detached sweep gate is its sole wake source.** `commands/goal-loop.md` step 3 no longer arms a wakeup and its `allowed-tools` no longer grant `ScheduleWakeup`/`CronCreate`; `commands/steps/round-tail.md` step 7 no longer re-calls it on a no-change tick and keeps only the three `Monitor` arms; `commands/goal-drive.md` drops the cadence from its round map and `--interval` note; `commands/steps/round-quiet.md` routes a goal-tier sleep to the gate. ⚠️ **A `Monitor` is bounded at 30 min**, so with the cadence gone the feed arms are re-armed only at the tail of a gate-woken round and die between gate wakes — the stated consequence, not a defect. ⚠️ **A gate-driven goal manager now reads `OK`, not `STALE`:** `scripts/manager-liveness.py --state` falls back to the gate's own per-subject heartbeat (`~/.claude/state/sweep-gate-loop/<vault>/<base>.heartbeat`) when a goal's `.cadence` ages past its limit, so `topic-keepalive.py` does not restart a healthy manager whose only wake source is the gate. Scoped to `goal--`; topic and fleet still arm their own cadence. Change set: `commands/goal-loop.md`, `commands/goal-drive.md`, `commands/topic-drive.md`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `commands/steps/round-quiet.md`, `commands/steps/round-liveness.md`, `docs/subject-resolution.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.286.0

- feat: **`KNOWN_SPAWN_KEYS` and both cap resolvers follow the wrapper's Soft/Hard rename, and a new check derives the key set from the Go source so the copy cannot go stale again.** The authoritative list is `axisKeys` in `bborbe/claude-wrapper` `pkg/spawn_axes.go` — the program that resolves and validates these caps. The copy here existed only for the unknown-key warning, and it had already drifted: it was missing `maxSessions`, `maxHeadless`, `maxInteractive`, `maxTopicManagers` and `maxGoalManagers`, so a config using any of them was told the key was ignored while the wrapper honoured it. `scripts/check-spawn-keys.py` now derives the expected set from the Go source (14 axis keys from `axisKeys`, 8 pre-rename spellings from `renamedSpawnKeys`, plus `mode` and `maxStartsPerMinute`) and fails `make precommit` on a mismatch; it skips with a printed notice where no `claude-wrapper` checkout exists, which is CI, so the comparison is authoritative locally rather than everywhere. **`resolveMaxConcurrent` and `resolveMaxManagers` read both spellings, current first** — the wrapper still parses the pre-rename names, so a server reading only the new ones would fall back to the built-in defaults on a config the wrapper honours (90 in the file, 20 enforced, nothing red). The eight pre-rename names stay in `KNOWN_SPAWN_KEYS` for the same reason: warning "ignored" about a key that works is the false-warning failure the `cluster` note already records. Docs, `commands/` and `agents/` move to the new names, and `check-worker-target.py`'s anchor follows. **No cap value changed.** ⚠️ Merge order: the wrapper release must be on its `master` before this one's `make precommit` is green, because the check reads that checkout. Two script consumers of the old names are corrected too, and both were gates that the rename would otherwise have disabled silently: `scripts/topic-spawn.py`'s fleet-manager ceiling read only `spawn.maxManagers`, so an operator following the new docs would have set `maxManagersSoft` and the ceiling would have stopped binding; `scripts/who-needs-me.py`'s cap-refusal regex matched only the old spellings, so a relayed worker-cap refusal fell to `unknown` in the operator's view. Change set: `server/spawn-mode.mjs`, `server/spawn-mode.test.mjs`, `server/manager-cap.test.mjs`, `scripts/topic-spawn.py`, `scripts/who-needs-me.py`, `scripts/tests/{test_topic_spawn,test_who_needs_me,test_worker_target,test_check_worker_target,test_check_spawn_keys}.py`, `scripts/check-spawn-keys.py`, `scripts/check-worker-target.py`, `Makefile`, `README.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `commands/steps/*.md`, `commands/topic-spawn.md`, `agents/fleet-open-actor.md`, `server/{config-reload,manager-sessions,start-rate,worker-sessions}.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.285.0

- feat: **the round now emits a row for a live session that no page records, so a park on one can no longer read as a clean fleet.** `compute()` built `rows` from resolved task files and took each row's id set from the page, so a session stamped on no page entered no row and no `parked` row could be emitted for it however parked it was. Measured 2026-11-11: session `1c05c414` sat `waiting` on the fleet while the round rendered `parked=0`. Live registry sessions in the round's vault that no row covers now carry a row of their own — keyed `unrecorded:<sid8>`, marked `unrecorded: true` / `resolved: false`, and classified by the same `in_flight()` / `liveness()` / `external_wait()` / `stuck_verdict()` calls as every other row, so a parked one reads `in_flight: parked` and counts under `parked=`. ⚠️ **The id set is not re-derived:** `row["sessions"]` is still `manager-predispatch.session_id_set`'s output (the one home for "which fields are a session id"), and the new block only READS it to learn which sessions a page already accounts for — nothing is added to any row, so a manager id arriving through `metrics_sessions` stays where it was instead of being re-owned into a second row. ⚠️ **Scoped to the round's own vault** (`cwd` under `vault`, via `_under()`): a sibling vault's fleet is that vault's round to report, and sweeping every cwd in would drown the one park the row exists to show. ⚠️ **A record the pid check disproved is skipped** (`alive is False`), so a stale registry entry cannot fabricate a park; `alive: None` is "cannot tell" and is kept. The reader's row-count invariant widens to `resolved_rows + len(unresolved) + len(unrecorded)`, and the hand-back gains `unrecorded=<n>` (`check-handback-keys.py` requires it) so an unrecorded park reaches the caller rather than only the payload. Tests: `test_a_parked_session_on_no_page_surfaces_as_a_parked_row`, `test_an_unrecorded_session_that_is_not_parked_fabricates_no_park` (the negative control — the path is a discriminator, not a catch-all), `test_a_manager_id_in_metrics_sessions_stays_covered_and_is_not_re_owned`, `test_a_session_outside_this_vault_is_not_swept_in`, `test_a_record_the_pid_check_disproved_is_not_a_park`, `test_the_row_count_invariant_includes_unrecorded`, `test_an_absent_registry_adds_no_unrecorded_rows`. Change set: `scripts/round-readings.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py`, `agents/round-readings-reader.md`, `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`, `docs/handback-contract.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.284.0

- feat: **the round now joins declared waits to the observable probe, so a session whose PR already merged stops rendering as parked.** `declared-wait.py` said which sessions declare a `⏰ Ends:` wait and what the text says; `observable-reprobe.py` answered whether one declaration's PR had cleared; nothing connected them, so the two facts sat one call apart and the row stayed parked forever. New `scripts/wait-reprobe.py render` reads the declaring set and prints a `REPROBED` block — one row per session with `resumed` / `still-blocked` / `unprobeable`, plus a count line — rendered rather than left to prose for the reason `drive-escalate.py` records. It **renders only**; resuming stays the drive leg's decision. ⚠️ **`unprobeable` is never a clearance**, here as in the module it delegates to. **Measured on the live store, and the honest shape of it:** `17 declared: 0 resumed · 0 still blocked · 17 unprobeable · 307 expired (not waits) · of 324 rows`. Across repeated runs the store yields **4 resolvable declarations** — `bborbe/teamvault#2`, `bborbe/nuke#698`, `bborbe/nuke#1187`, `bborbe/sun#13`, **all genuinely `MERGED` while still rendering as parked** — but ⚠️ **they are overwhelmingly `expired`**, and on the final run **none of the 17 live `declared` waits resolved at all**. So today this join catches the expired end of the defect (a row still rendering as parked after its wait ended) and not yet the live end; the live end needs the declaration-writing convention below, and the count is expected to move as sessions re-park. ⚠️ **Only a declaration that NAMES ITS REPO is probed, and there is deliberately no resolver.** The obvious one — transcript `cwd` → `git remote get-url origin` — returns the repo the session *lives in*; every PR-bearing declaration in the store comes from a vault-hosted session, and the vault repo has a merged PR #1, so a bare `PR #1` would render `resumed` off a repository the wait never mentioned. A resolver that manufactures a false clearance is worse than none. ⚠️ **The verdict counts are taken over `declared` rows only** — folding an `expired` declaration's result into "N still blocked" would report a blocked session that declares no wait, which is the misreading the per-row `wait` state exists to prevent. Change set: `scripts/wait-reprobe.py`, `scripts/tests/test_wait_reprobe.py`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.283.2

## v0.283.1

- fix: **`restart-worker.py` spawns through the resolved wezterm binary, not a bare `wezterm` name — the keeper's `restart fresh` killed the Fleet Manager and could not start the replacement.** Under launchd the keeper's `PATH` is `/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`, which holds no wezterm, so `["wezterm", "cli", "spawn", …]` raised `FileNotFoundError` after the pid was already signalled. Measured 2026-11-11: lapse card `f83d0cd0…` at 11:22:02Z, `fresh restart … FAILED — exit 1` at 11:23:02Z, manager ABSENT by 11:24Z, recovered only by the separate `--absent` path at 11:38Z — 14 minutes of an unsupervised fleet from a path that was destructive without being restorative. The `--absent` path never had the defect because it already called `find_wezterm()`; the normal path now does too, **before the kill**, alongside the launcher and cwd guards. Also: `fleet-keeper.py` reported `lines[0]` of restart-worker's stdout — always the `🔄 restart-worker …` header — as the failure reason, which is why the log hid the cause; it now reports the last `❌` line. Change set: `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/fleet-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_keeper.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.283.0

- feat: **the decision-park leg counts its resumes per wait kind.** `decision-park-resume.py` printed one `decision-resumed=` total, which cannot say which wait cleared nor reconcile against the `--record` store kind by kind; the summary and the keep-alive actor's hand-back now carry `decision-resumed-a=` (approval landed) and `decision-resumed-d=` (`blocked_by` completed) instead, and `check-handback-keys.py` requires both. To stay inside the agent's 30 KB body budget, the `<output_format>` sentence saying `park-nudge:` never truncates is dropped — the next sentence already states that `crash-loop:` and `cannot-tell:` are the only lines that truncate, and `commands/steps/keepalive.md` carries the same rule. Test: `test_resumes_are_counted_per_wait_kind`. Change set: `scripts/decision-park-resume.py`, `scripts/tests/test_decision_park_resume.py`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive-decision-parks.md`, `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.282.1

- fix: **`decision-park-resume.py` kind (d) fires only when the last `blocked_by` dependency completed after the worker's last turn.** It read status alone, and a completed dependency stays completed, so a worker resumed on it was re-resumed every time the reaper closed it again — close → resume → idle → close with nothing consuming the wait, which the resume cap's 30-minute window does not bound against a 15-minute reap cycle. Kind (d) now uses the same ordering rule as kind (a): the latest dependency `completed_date` must be later than the session's last transcript message, so the resumed worker's first turn consumes the wait. A dependency with no `completed_date` reads `unread` (reported, never resumed); measured 2026-11-11, 2133 of 2388 recently touched completed tasks carry one. Tests: `test_a_resumed_worker_that_speaks_consumes_its_wait` (fails on v0.281.0), `test_a_dependency_completed_before_the_last_turn_does_not_fire`, `test_a_dependency_with_no_completed_date_is_unread`, and the SC2 four-round guard. Change set: `scripts/decision-park-resume.py`, `scripts/tests/test_decision_park_resume.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.282.0

- feat: **a drive round now reports how many nudges it drafted against how many it sent, so a send leg that never ran can no longer read as a complete round.** Measured 2026-11-11 on `2026-11-11T08-51-29Z`: the payload drafted 5 distinct recipients and the round recorded no send at all, while `Sent: 0` rendered exactly like `Sent: 5`. `scripts/check-drive-nudges-sent.py` prints `nudges: drafted <N> · sent <M> · missing <N-M>` and exits non-zero on a deficit: `N` is a parse over `REAPS`, `NUDGES` and `UNVERIFIABLE NUDGES` deduplicated by recipient (the unverifiable block is a transcription instruction, so one session can be named twice — the measured round is 6 lines over 5 recipients), and `M` is the round's own action record. ⚠️ **`missing` means "the round never touched this line", not "this line was not messaged"** — a `REAPS` line whose target is a finished headless row is closed through `close-worker.py` and deliberately not sent, and a line whose peer moved to `busy` is skipped and reported; the record carries the action (`<recipient>`, `closed`, `skipped`) so `sent` stays the count of messages that left while neither a close nor a reported skip is flagged as a miss. ⚠️ **The record is per-round (`~/.claude/state/fleet-handback/<round>.sent`), not `/tmp/drive-sent.txt`** — a shared path plus a truncate leaves a skipped send leg holding the PREVIOUS round's ids, which is exactly the failure this gate exists to catch; a round with no record reports `missing <N>` rather than inheriting a clean bill. ⚠️ **`UNVERIFIABLE NUDGES` joins the send order** at one message per distinct recipient, so a counted line is a line the send step is told to send and no session is nudged twice. ⚠️ **It does not prove delivery** — a send step that writes a recipient without calling `SendMessage` defeats it; the measured failure is a send leg that did not run, and that limit is named in the task's `# Out of Scope`. Change set: `scripts/check-drive-nudges-sent.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_drive_nudges_sent.py`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.281.0

- feat: **a `⏰ Ends:` observable is now probeable, so a `🔴 BLOCKED` row whose PR already merged can be re-driven instead of rendering as blocked forever.** `scripts/declared-wait.py` answers *whether* a session declares a wait and how stale the declaration is; nothing asked whether the thing it names still exists, so of 8 blocked sessions measured 2026-11-10, **2 had already cleared and were never re-checked**. New `scripts/observable-reprobe.py` is the callable seam — the drive leg is handed a prompt, not a board, so prose naming a probe is a mechanism nobody can run (the same lesson `scripts/escalation-manager.py` records). It parses a PR out of the declaration (`https://github.com/o/r/pull/N`, `o/r#N`, or a bare `PR #N` with `--repo`), probes it through `gh`, and prints `<verdict>\t<reason>`: `resumed` on `MERGED` or `CLOSED` (kept distinct, because landing abandoned work is a different next move), `still-blocked` otherwise **quoting the PR's own state, review and merge status**, and `unprobeable` on anything unreadable. ⚠️ **A declaration naming more than one PR clears only when EVERY one has** — probing the first match alone would report `resumed` off a merged PR while a second is still open, the same false positive by another route. That holds for the shape the fleet actually writes, not only the fully-prefixed one: **every `#<n>` in the declaration is collected, wherever it sits**, so *"PR #119 … or nuke #939"* and *"nuke #939 … PR #119"* both collect both and reach the same verdict. ⚠️ **Over-collection is deliberately preferred to under-collection** — `verdict()` ANDs, so an extra observable can only add a `still-blocked` or an `unprobeable` and can never produce a clearance, whereas a matcher that recognises only some of the references clears on the rest. Two earlier drafts gated on a `PR` prefix, then on a `PR` token appearing before the number; both were false-clearance bugs and both were removed. A collected number that turns out not to be a PR costs a false negative — the row stays blocked — which is the direction this module always prefers. ⚠️ **The same rule binds the dedup**: two references are one observable only when their RESOLVED targets match, never when their numbers merely coincide, so `PR #536` and `bborbe/nuke#536` are both probed and a merged one cannot clear the other. ⚠️ **`unprobeable` is never a clearance** — a failed `gh` call, a missing repo or an unrecognised observable all leave the row standing, mirroring `declared-wait.py`'s failed-read rule; folding any of them into `resumed` would resume a session on no evidence. Verified against the live API: `#786` (merged) → `resumed … MERGED`, `#999999` → `unprobeable … probe failed`, a clock-only `⏰ Ends:` → `unprobeable`, and an open PR → `still-blocked … <state> · review <decision> · merge <status>`. Change set: `scripts/observable-reprobe.py`, `scripts/tests/test_observable_reprobe.py`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.280.0

- feat: **`closer-gate-lint` refuses a paneless session's closer that asks the operator for Shift+Tab or a pane-only slash command.** A headless worker has nobody at its keyboard, so `you run: Shift+Tab → accept edits` or `you run: /reload-plugins in the Fleet Manager tab` minted a card nobody could act on. Measured 2026-11-05..11: 131 Shift+Tab and 279 `/reload-plugins` closer lines in 7 days. The check fires only when `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=sdk-cli`, which is every headless worker and no tab across 42 live sessions on 2026-11-11. ⚠️ Not `WEZTERM_PANE`: it is inherited from the spawner and was measured unset in a real tab. The recorded `e4fd1919` tab closer still passes, and so does a paneless closer naming a full store card id, which is the `approve-run.py` route the stderr prescribes. A path that merely contains a command name (`/exit-codes.md`, `~/exit`, `./rename-notes.md`) is not an ask: the slash arm's terminator is `(?![\w./-])` and its lookbehind excludes `~` and `.`, not `\b`, which holds between `t` and `-`. The stderr names the routes: an `approve-run.py` card for one command, the manager for a mode switch or a restart. Bounded by the existing loop guard (fail-open, one re-block). Fixtures 41–45; the whole existing fixture table is re-run under the paneless environment and only the three pane-only fixtures change verdict. `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/41..45`, `docs/closer-classes.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.279.0

- feat: **`restart-worker.py` refuses to resume a session whose anchored task is already finished — an eighth refusal, `task-terminal`.** Every resume leg started from "the process is gone", never from "the work is done", so a reaped worker whose task read `completed` was handed a spawn slot again and the close was undone: measured 2026-11-11, session `2e540d65` was reaped at 07:18:34Z with a `completed` task and its id was live again at 07:33:55Z. The guard reads the task's `status` and `defer_date` from disk **at resume time**, not from a census or digest taken earlier in the round, so a task that flips between the candidate enumeration and the spawn is refused rather than started. The terminal set and the task lookup are `close-worker.py`'s (`TERMINAL`, `find_task`, `task_verdict`), reached through `load_sibling` — not a second copy, because a close side and a resume side that disagree about "finished" drift until the resume restarts exactly what the close refused to reap. Wired into both resume paths this script owns, before the dry-run report and before `os.kill`: the normal restart, and `--absent` — the only resume it performs for a headless worker, and the path the incident took. A handed-off task, and a session with no task anchor (a manager), still resume: refusing those would stop every manager resume. ⚠️ **The headless `spawn_agent(resume=…)` path in `bborbe/claude-wrapper` is NOT covered by this change** — it is a separate task on that repo, and it is the path that fires in production. Change set: `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.278.0

- feat: **the fleet round closes a finished or deferred tab row through `close-worker.py` instead of only drafting the reap message.** `commands/steps/drive.md` closed a finished headless row and, for a tab row, sent the reap message and stopped — so a worker whose task was terminal sat holding its pane and its spawn-cap slot until an operator clicked. The caller now runs the same close for a `FREEABLE TABS` row classed `finished` or `deferred <date>`: `close-worker.py` is the script `/supervisor:close` wraps, so the busy / gate / worktree / hold / terminal refusals are unchanged and the round gains no new authority. ⚠️ **A refusal is reported as its token and never retried as a message** — all eight tokens the script emits (`busy-target`, `gate-held`, `dirty-worktree`, `held-session`, `task-not-terminal`, `task-unresolved`, `unknown-session-id`, `ambiguous-pid`) leave the row for the next round. ⚠️ **`long-wait <age>` is never closed by this arm** — it is a parked worker holding a live wait, and closing it would destroy the wait. Change set: `commands/steps/drive.md`, `commands/steps/reap.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `scripts/tests/test_close_worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.277.0

- feat: **every `blocked` `ESCALATION` row now carries the manager it belongs to, so a blocked session whose closer named no owner still reaches someone.** The round resolved a blocked row's jump link but never its owner: `agents/fleet-drive.md`'s `ESCALATION` format gains a `manager <resolved|fleet>` field, resolved by a new `scripts/escalation-manager.py <task title>`, which is the one implementation of `docs/fleet-surface.md` § *Which manager owns a subject*: it reuses `fleet-board.py`'s grouping (`build_grouping` → `manager_on_chain`) rather than re-deriving the tiers, prints `fleet` for tier 4's fallback, and **exits non-zero when the registry cannot be read**. ⚠️ A failed read renders `UNKNOWN`, never `fleet` — `fleet` claims the Fleet Manager owns a row it may not own, which is the `A Wait Needs a Performer` defect the field exists to close. Three rounds of prose naming the resolver functions failed before this because the drive leg is handed a prompt, not a board; a script is the thing a reader can actually run. `commands/steps/drive.md` carries the field through unchanged and is forbidden from re-resolving it, so two tiers cannot end up owning one subject. `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`.

- fix: **a sweep-gate wake may pass a declared machine wait.** `pane-wake-guard.py` refused every pane whose last turn declared a machine wait (`🟡 WAITING` / `⏰ Ends:`), and a goal manager's resting state IS a machine wait — the loop cadence is the machine it waits on — so the gate could never wake the sessions it exists to wake, and L1's measured ceiling would be unrealisable in practice. New `--allow-machine-wait` (passed by `manager-wake.py`) lets a sweep-gate wake through that one skip; an operator ask still blocks, because that check runs after the machine-wait branch. Measured 2026-11-11: `SKIP skip:machine-wait pane=273 session=5b29b81a` on a manager whose goal had an actionable change. `scripts/pane-wake-guard.py`, `scripts/manager-wake.py`, `scripts/tests/test_pane_wake_guard.py`.

## v0.276.1

- fix: **the digest renders one `CLOSERS` row per live session instead of a count summary.** The 43-line payload cap applied to the whole render, so the reader collapsed `CLOSERS` to `5 ask, 33 routine/none` and the 33 captured `routine`/`none` rows never reached `agents/fleet-drive.md` step 4a — which reads the rendered digest and fail-closes a missing row to `unverifiable`. A captured closer therefore looked absent, no interactive-close candidate reached `revive`, and `0 closed` was the symptom (measured `2026-11-11T07-16-47Z`: 38 rows captured for 38 sessions, 5 rendered).
