The cap is now a fixed 40-line body plus one `CLOSERS` row per live session — bounded by fleet size, not candidate count — and the section is explicitly exempt from the fixed allowance. Change set: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/steps/sweep.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.276.0

- feat: **the keep-alive leg resumes a decision-parked worker whose wait cleared.** It resumed only clock-bound waits — `park-event.py due` reads `defer_date` watches — and nothing read the two waits that write no `defer_date`: an approval that landed after the worker was closed, and a `blocked_by` set that went terminal. A worker reaped at 15 minutes idle therefore stayed closed with no reader to bring it back. The decision is `scripts/decision-park-resume.py`, not prose: six review rounds found six different real defects in the prose version, so it became a script with one named test per defect in `scripts/tests/test_decision_park_resume.py`, driving the real parsers, the real store and the canonical frontmatter readers. ⚠️ **Kind (d) requires a non-empty `blocked_by` whose every member is `completed`** — "every name is terminal" is vacuously true of an empty set, and `hold` is an active block rather than a finished dependency. ⚠️ **The resume bound is the script's own counter, written by its `--record` and read by its `spent`** — recording through `worker-keepalive.py` writes a different store the bound never sees. ⚠️ **An answered attention card is deliberately not a kind here**: it already ships as [[An Answered Card Resumes the Session Waiting On It]]. The hand-back gains `decision-candidates` / `decision-resumed` / `decision-unread`. Change set: `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive-decision-parks.md`, `scripts/decision-park-resume.py`, `scripts/tests/test_decision_park_resume.py`, `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.275.0

- feat: **`manager-wake.py` wakes a subject's manager pane on change only — the pane-wake half of the sweep-gate wake wiring.** Resolves the subject's manager session from `~/.claude/state/worker-manager/` (subject + tier, newest `resolved_at`), its pane via the `ps`/tty join over `wezterm cli list`, gates with `pane-wake-guard.py`, and types one `/supervisor:<tier>-drive --interval <N>m` prompt via `wezterm cli send-text` only when the guard returns `wake`/`wake:suggestion`. `skip:*` types nothing; the submit is CONFIRMED (the composer must clear or the session start working) before `WOKEN` is reported, and an unconfirmed submit is `UNVERIFIED`, never a false success; every decision (woken, unverified, skipped, no manager, no pane) exits 0 so a quiet tick never reads as FAILED. Operator pick: pane wake over headless resume (attention card 1803ce3c). `scripts/manager-wake.py`.

- fix: **the task-link strip unlinks only a session that is positively `ABSENT`, so a live session keeps its task link.** `strip_task_links()` selected on `status` alone, so `--strip-task-links` removed `claude_session_id` / `metrics_sessions` from every non-terminal task whether or not its session was running — measured 2026-11-10, when one run unlinked 30 live sessions across `private-personal` and `seibert-brogrammers`, invisible afterwards to keep-alive, nudge, close and resume. Every session id a task carries — `claude_session_id` **and** each `metrics_sessions` entry, since the strip removes that whole block and it can name a different, live session — is now probed through `session-liveness.py --check` (exit-code contract, corroborated against the `ABSENT` word on stderr so an uncaught exception exiting 1 is not read as a verdict, memoized, bounded by a timeout) **and** corroborated against the identity registry's pid, because `--check` alone answered `ABSENT` for a session whose pid was running. The link is stripped only when every id is dead; `LIVE`, `UNKNOWN`, `AMBIGUOUS`, `MALFORMED`, a registry-backed process and a link carrying no id all keep it, and the run prints a `kept` line beside the candidate count. The verdicts are snapshotted **before** `stop_sessions()`, so the dry-run and the real run decide from one fleet state instead of a live fleet versus a post-kill one, and an unreadable or absent registry answers "alive" rather than reading as "nothing is live". `scripts/fleet-reset.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_reset.py`, `commands/fleet-reset.md`, `README.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`.

- feat: **The fleet round now reports how many live sessions are executing an old plugin body — the reading no in-body rule can take.** `scripts/plugin-version-census.py` already existed and already carried the two traps (the marker count is not the session count; liveness comes from `session-liveness.py`), but **nothing in `commands/` or `agents/` referenced it** — it had never been wired into a round. Measured 2026-11-10: `installed 0.273.0 · 44 of 49 live sessions stale`, spread across **eight** versions, with the in-body staleness rules shipped in every one of them. That measurement is the argument: a rule living inside a step body cannot detect that its own body is stale, because the session holding an old body is exactly the one that never prints the new rule. Three such rules were written for this and each failed differently (content hash never fired; a version-line trigger was circular; a fleet-tier version line was circular for the same reason). `commands/steps/readings.md` gains the reading as the seventh — `plugin-stale: <N> of <M> live (<V> versions · installed <X>)` — so the fleet round reports the spread from a session that can see it. ⚠️ **The round reports the number and names the lever; it never pulls it.** `--reload` types `/reload-plugins` into every stale session whose pane resolves — production-touching on live sessions, several mid-turn — so running it stays the operator's call. ⚠️ **`unknown` is never `stale`**: a headless or cluster worker may hold no local `.in_use` marker, and calling it stale would name a fix for a session the lever cannot reach. `commands/steps/readings.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`.
- fix: **`session-liveness.py --check` no longer answers ABSENT for a session whose process is running: the verdict path now consults the identity registry on a stale `live: false` row.** `_verdict_for` returned ABSENT for any non-cluster row with `live: false`, and `_check` routed an HTTP-200 row straight there without ever reading the registry — the module states it itself at `_identity()` (*"the verdict path (`--check`) never touches the registry"*), while the registry-aware `_absent_licensed` was reachable only on HTTP **404**. A session whose `mcp-timer` heartbeat lapsed therefore read as dead while alive. Measured 2026-11-10: `--check 4295ddac` answered ABSENT (exit 1) for hours while `ps -p 26515` showed `claude` running, and that id's endpoint row read `live: False, source: mcp-timer, state: idle` — a **stale row**, not a missing one, so the coverage-gap explanation was the wrong mechanism. ABSENT is the one verdict that authorises a resume, so the defect manufactured duplicate workers on live tasks. The not-live branch now routes through `_absent_licensed`, which gains an `endpoint_state` parameter so the message names the stale row instead of claiming the endpoint "holds no row", reusing the same `alive is not False` predicate the no-row path already pays: registry-live answers UNKNOWN, registry-dead keeps ABSENT, so the gate **narrows** the verdict rather than erasing it. The existing not-live case is split into both halves — the dead-registry control keeps ABSENT (its pid is now planted beyond the OS range so the registry genuinely reads it dead, where the old fixture only passed because `plant()` writes no `procStart`) and a new regression case pins UNKNOWN for a registry-live row and fails on the pre-fix code (`1 != 2`). `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`.
## v0.274.2

- fix: **a row whose task carries a future `defer_date` is no longer classified `nudge`.** `fleet-board.py`'s `unblocks_for` suppressed the nudge only for a session that DECLARED a wait (`⏰ Ends:`, via `declared-wait.py`), and a `defer_date` is a task-file field that reader never sees — so a worker correctly parked on the clock reached the nudge test, passed it, and carried an empty `details` (that map is populated only for `needs-input` and `operator-keystroke` rows). Measured 2026-11-10 on a live board: 2 of 6 `nudge` rows carried a future `defer_date`. `build_task_index` now carries `defer_date` in `meta`, and `unblocks_for` parses it through `scripts/defer-date.py` — the tree's single reader — so only `deferred` suppresses the nudge: a `defer_date` that has passed leaves the row `nudge` again (the defer parks work, it does not mute it), an absent one is unaffected, and an unparseable one falls through to the normal rule and is named in `details` as `defer_date invalid: …` rather than silently parking a row forever. `unblocks_for` takes an optional `now` so the deferred and expired cases are testable without waiting.
- fix: **`build_task_index` now exposes the stamped task's `defer_date` alongside `status`, `phase` and `open_boxes`.** Raw and unquoted — parsing stays in `defer-date.py`, because a second parser here is the drift that reader was extracted to remove.

## v0.274.1

- fix: **the owner test reads `⏰ Ends:` from the whole message, and the store probe is gated to operator-verb closers.** Review findings on #778: the owner arm searched only the `👤 You:` body, so a `🔴 BLOCKED` closer that correctly declared its observable on its own `⏰ Ends:` line was refused — the exact shape the check exists to accept. `absent_card_handles` now runs only on `approve:` / `review:` / `you run:` / `pick` closers (any 32-hex token in a prose closer is a citation, not an unanswerable gate), dedupes its handles and caps the probe count, so a pathological body cannot outlive the hook's own timeout. `card_in_store` gains direct tests for 200 / 404 / other-HTTP-error / connection-error / the `off` switch. `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`.
- feat: **the Stop-time closer lint refuses the two shapes that make an operator gate unreachable.** A closer naming a full 32-hex card id the attention store does not hold now blocks (`absent-card`): the old handle check refused only a handle `poll` cannot *address*, so a well-formed id that resolved to nothing passed and the operator had nothing to answer. A closer carrying a `🔴 BLOCKED` state line whose `👤 You:` body names no owner now blocks (`ownerless-blocked`) — an owner being a card id, a manager/supervisor role, a re-checkable `⏰ Ends:` observable, **or an operator-directed verb** (`approve:` / `review:` / `you run:` / `pick`) — that last arm is required by `fixtures/e4fd1919-transcript.jsonl`, a recorded real closer whose `👤 You:` is `you run: switch this tab to accept edits (Shift+Tab)`; `waiting on someone` is none of them. ⚠️ Card-less `pick` closers are deliberately NOT blocked (operator decision 2026-11-10): the closer-mirror mint became opt-in on 2026-10-17 because mirrors were 58.3% of board volume, and blocking every card-less pick would reverse that. Both new checks fail open — an unaskable store returns `None` and never blocks. `reap-closer.py` gains `last_closer_scan` (one scan, plus the message text the owner test needs) and `last_closer_and_ends` now delegates to it, so there is still exactly one scanner. `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/reap-closer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/39-ownerless-blocked.jsonl`, `40-blocked-with-manager-owner.jsonl`.
- fix: **A task under `# Explicitly Deferred` is never proposed back.** `parse_weekly` reads `### N.` blocks carrying a `**Task:** [[name]]` line, and the deferred section is authored as plain `- ` bullets, so nothing in it could ever enter `planned_keys` — every row parked there stayed eligible for a proposal card, and a card is an ask. `parse_deferred` reads the section from the same weekly note the picked sections resolve, bounded by its `---`/heading terminator (an end-of-file reader swallows `# Planning Notes` and suppresses real proposals while looking correct), takes every wikilink on a bullet (one bullet may carry two), and reports the count in `notes` so an exclusion cannot read the same as a section that was never found. `scripts/next-focus.py`, `scripts/tests/test_next_focus.py`.
- fix: **the worker crash-loop cap counts close→resume cycles, not resumes inside a 30-minute window.** `scripts/worker-keepalive.py` now resets the resume count only on **progress** — an open box ticked, a new `# Progress` entry, or a new commit on the task's `repo:` branch — so a loop that closes and resumes every ~40 minutes with no progress stops at the 4th resume instead of never tripping. It posts ONE attention card on `crash-loop` (`dedup_key` `crash-loop:<slug>`), which the script did not do before. The store gains a `progress` marker beside `resumes`; a legacy `{resumes: [...]}` store has no baseline, so its first probe falls back to the retired 30-minute window and the next `--record-resume` migrates it. The manager tier (`topic-keepalive.py`) keeps its 30-minute window — the shared helper is untouched. `scripts/tests/test_worker_keepalive.py` covers the no-progress cycles spaced beyond the old window (refused + one card) and the progress reset.

## v0.274.0

- feat: **A stopped worker stays stopped — `/supervisor:stop` now stamps the anchored task, and both automatic paths that could resurrect it honour the stamp.** The crash-vs-stop distinction existed for managers only: a manager's `<slug>.stopped` marker keeps the keepalive from reading a stand-down as a lapse, while a **task** had no tier at all. A worker that ran `/supervisor:stop` either wrote nothing or fell through step 2b's `(none recorded)` fallback onto its task's **goal**, standing down a goal manager's loop it did not own. Measured 2026-11-10: an approved, due, dead-session row the operator had no interest in was reopened by the open leg. The marker is now `stopped: true` + `stop_date` on the task page, written through `vault-cli task set` by the new `scripts/task-standdown.py` — a **page field, not the state file**, because a task stop must leave `~/.claude/state/sweep-gate/*.stopped` byte-identical and because the state dir is per-user, so a slug would let two same-titled tasks in different vaults share one marker. Two consumers read it: `open-queue.py`'s `guards()` returns `stopped` (ahead of every derived guard, so a stopped-and-deferred row reports the stop), and `worker-keepalive.py` jumps with *"a stop is not a death"* — the restart half, without which a stopped worker whose process died was resumed anyway. `commands/stop.md` step 2b now discriminates the tier **per session** — a `worker-manager/<sid>.json` registration means manager, otherwise the worker limb runs and its own verdict decides (`stopped` → it was a worker; `no-task` → not a worker, so the manager limb runs) — so a worker writes the page field and a manager keeps its subject marker unchanged. ⚠️ The first version used the probe's gate-loop line as the second limb, which is a **machine-wide** process match: a launchd-hosted manager whose registration was missing read no gate process, took the worker limb, and kept its loop armed. The by-name form (`/supervisor:stop "<task>"`) writes the same page field on top of its `task--<base>.stopped` record — that file is a record no reader consumes, so without it a task stopped by name was still reopened and resumed. `agents/goal-drive.md`'s auto-resume gate gains a **`not stopped`** clause: `worker-keepalive.py` returns `jump` for a row a goal owns, so that gate is the only auto-resume path for a goal-owned stopped worker — without the clause the goal tier resurrected exactly the population the feature claims. `--clear` is the operator's only clear verb. `scripts/task-standdown.py`, `scripts/open-queue.py`, `scripts/worker-keepalive.py`, `commands/stop.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `agents/goal-drive.md`.

## v0.273.1

- fix: **The headless-reap close rule, the name→session-id match and the gate's own docstring now agree with the shipped behaviour.** `commands/steps/rules.md` said a manager closes a finished worker *only through `/supervisor:close`*, contradicting the fleet round's drive step, which closes a finished headless row itself through `close-worker.py`; it now names both routes as the one script. `commands/steps/drive.md` resolves a `REAPS` roster name after stripping a leading decoration run and casefolding, exact equality, never a substring — the join `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` owns — so a `⚙ `-prefixed or re-cased name still matches and a partial one cannot. `scripts/check-headless-reap-close.py`'s docstring counted five assertions while listing six.

## v0.273.0

- fix: **A `waiting` session now reads `parked`, and `stuck_verdict()` consumes it, so a permission-parked worker is no longer stamped `frozen`.** `in_flight()`'s `status == "waiting"` branch returned `("quiet", …)`, and `stuck_verdict()` took no `in_flight` argument at all — so one row emitted `in_flight: quiet` **and** `stuck: frozen` from the same read, and a worker suspended on a permission gate (whose tool call writes nothing to the transcript) was indistinguishable from a dead one. Measured 2026-10-29: two classifiers keyed on that signal in one night, two false STUCK rows, and two process kills — one of which took a session that was working. `in_flight()` now returns `parked`, and `stuck_verdict()` gained an `in_flight_verdict` parameter that short-circuits to `none` **after** the `feed == "gated"` check and **before** the mtime check: a veto, not a fourth carrier, because transcript staleness is exactly what a suspended tool call produces. ⚠️ **`scripts/manager-receipt.py`'s `in_flight` vocabulary set is the gate that makes this shippable** — `readings_shape()` rejects any row outside `{"in-flight", "quiet", "unread"}`, so `parked` had to land in the validator and the document together or the receipt would refuse the new value, which is a regression rather than a fix. ⚠️ **The mapping is asserted in five places, not the two the defect names**, and the sweep found three the plan had not listed: `agents/goal-drive.md` limb 1, `agents/goal-sweep-reader.md` input 11 (twice), `commands/goal-status.md`, and the vault's `Manager Session` runbook (twice). A `grep` for the old mapping now returns empty across `agents/`, `commands/` and `skills/`. Three of those files sit on the byte ratchet at their exact recorded size, so every edit was funded by trimming redundancy in the same file and `check-budget.py --tighten` re-seeded the baseline. Pinned by `TheParkLimb` in `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py` (4 cases): `waiting` → `in_flight: parked` **and** `stuck: none` · an `idle` session with a static transcript still reads `quiet` **and** `frozen` (the negative control, so the new limb cannot swallow the real case) · a released gate reads `frozen` again · and reverting the one line makes the first case fail, which is the positive control that proves the fixture can fire. Change set: `scripts/round-readings.py`, `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py`, `agents/goal-drive.md`, `agents/goal-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/goal-status.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **a worker that exits having written nothing is reported as a failure instead of reading as an idle one.** The drive leg revived `parked` candidates and reaped `finished` rows, and neither could tell a worker that never wrote from one that wrote and stopped: `finished` is decided by the task's own boxes, which a task completed before the worker spawned satisfies without the worker having done anything. Measured 2026-11-07 — a fleet manager's worker hit a plan gate, the 15-minute timeout denied it, and it exited with its task file untouched; the round had no row for it, and a nudge cannot reach a worker that never started. New step 1a compares the task page's mtime against **`spawned_at` on the session's spawn-ledger row** — never `claude_session_started`, a vault-ui Start-button flag the payload profiler records as `nonempty 0` on every task and which the orphan seed already refuses, and never `metrics_sessions[].started_at`, which a headless spawn never binds because `vault-cli task work-on` is what writes it. The same reachability gap applied to the candidate set: step 1's rows come from `CLASSIFICATION`, and `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md:122` states a headless worker is *"invisible to `ListAgents` by construction"*, so step 1a also takes every ledger row absent from `CLASSIFICATION` that reads `mode: headless`. Both reads use commands that run on this fleet's macOS host: the mtime through `os.path.getmtime`, not GNU-only `date -r`, and the ledger through a new `spawn-ledger.py --json` CLI, because the module run bare printed nothing and exited 0. The ledger row must record an exit — **`ended_at` set**, not `status`, which reads `running` on 824 of 1,075 rows against 26 live sessions — so a still-running worker is never reported as a dead one, and a 15-minute grace window covers a worker spawned moments ago. Both joins are named: ledger row to `CLASSIFICATION` by the first 8 characters of `session_id`, and ledger row to its task page through the page's own `claude_session_id` / `metrics_sessions` id set, because the wire row carries no task reference. `wrote nothing since spawn` joins the drive leg's verdict set, its ledger entries and both count lines. mtime at or before `spawned_at` past that window with an `execution` task and open boxes reports a failure and drafts no nudge; mtime after it keeps the ordinary disposition, which is the positive control that stops every unexplained row reading as a dead worker. The class is worded as evidence rather than proof, because a task page's mtime moves when anyone writes it. `scripts/check-wrote-nothing.py` pins every property in its `REQUIRED` list — sixteen markers, the authoritative count — and `scripts/tests/test_check_wrote_nothing.py` proves each one is separately load-bearing.

## v0.272.0

- feat: open leg skips a row whose last session ended on a wait a new session cannot clear — `waiting-external` (newest Stop carries `⏰ Ends:`) and `waiting-operator` (newest Stop is an unanswered question card) — read from the row's whole session-id set in `<sid>.events.jsonl`, so slots stop going to workers that park on their first turn
- feat: `fleet-keeper.py`'s lapse card for a live Fleet Manager offers `restart fresh` — on the operator's answer only, once per card, it runs `restart-worker.py <sid> --reason lapse --fresh-prompt /supervisor:fleet-loop`, which stops the stalled manager and starts a NEW session (fresh context) instead of resuming the exhausted one (operator ruling 2026-11-10). `restart-worker.py` gains `--fresh-prompt` (allowlisted). The keeper's no-kill pin is narrowed to exactly this executor and the 2026-11-06 resume.

## v0.271.0

- feat: **A tab worker nudged and still idle the next round is closed, and stays resumable.** The drive leg nudged an idle worker once and then suppressed every later nudge, so the row lingered until the 12 h `FREEABLE TABS` threshold and an operator click — the lifecycle page's gap 1. `agents/fleet-drive.md` step 5b now closes such a row instead: a `revive` candidate whose previous ledger entry carries `nudge: drafted` and whose bucket, open-box count and blocker state are all unchanged is closed through the new `scripts/close-idle-tab.py`, and the round renders a `CLOSED` line. A refusal never falls back to a nudge — `gate-held` / `held-session` / `task-terminal` / `task-unresolved` escalate, the transient tokens retry next round. `close-idle-tab.py` is a separate verb rather than a flag on `close-worker.py` for the reason `reap-idle-headless.py` is: `close-worker.py`'s task gate refuses `task-not-terminal` and is right to, since it closes *finished* sessions, while this one closes a *parked* one and keeps the task `in_progress` with its `claude_session_id` so `/supervisor:open --task` resumes it. Every guard is kept (`held-session`, `busy-target`, `gate-held`, `dirty-worktree`, `watcher-alive`, `not-idle-long-enough`, `not-tab`) and the pane kill and pane→session join are imported from `close-worker.py`, never copied. `drive-ledger.py` gains the `closed` verdict. `scripts/tests/test_close_idle_tab.py` covers every refusal in both directions.

## v0.270.0

- feat: **a `⏰ Ends:` declaration now expires, so a wait that can never fire stops suppressing the nudge.** `scripts/declared-wait.py` reads the `Stop` record's own `ts`. A declaration older than an hour (`SUPERVISOR_WAIT_MAX_AGE_S`) that names no future clock (an ISO date or datetime, such as a `defer_date`) is now `expired`. `fleet-board.py`, `manager-predispatch.py` and the fleet sweep's `parked-on-watcher` row all stop treating an expired declaration as a wait, and the session is re-driven. If the wait is still real, the session re-parks. A declaration now carries two clocks: `last_ts` is the newest render and drives the 1 h re-check, while `ts` is the start of the trailing run naming the same observable (digits ignored). So an hourly re-park no longer resets `drive-escalate.py`'s 24 h card, and that script is unchanged. Measured 2026-11-10: 16 of 27 idle 🟡 sessions would never wake.
- feat: **`declared-wait.py --verdicts [--json]` prints each declaration with its verdict, age and `mechanism`.** `mechanism` is `session-bound` (`Monitor`, `ScheduleWakeup`, `CronCreate`, a background shell, task or sub-agent) or `external`. A session-bound wake dies with its process, so it is flagged for headless workers.

## v0.269.0

- feat: **A headless worker idle for 15 minutes is now closed with no session running — `launchd/com.bborbe.idle-reaper.plist` drives `scripts/idle-reaper-tick.py` every 900 s.** The operator's rule (2026-11-07, restated 2026-11-10) is that an idle headless worker exits after 15 minutes; `reap-idle-headless.py` was the verb and nothing called it. Each tick enumerates candidates through the reaper's own `candidate_session_ids()` (so the tick and `--all` cannot disagree on the population), runs the reaper once per candidate, and routes exactly one refusal onward — `task-terminal` goes to `close-worker.py`, the finished-task verb, because `close-worker.py` takes one id and cannot enumerate. Every other refusal is final for the tick and logged with its token; a refusal exits 0, an unexpected verb exit exits 1. ⚠️ Deliberately **not** on `com.bborbe.fleet-keeper`, which is report-only by operator decision (2026-11-01, card efc771fb) and pinned against every kill path by `test_fleet_keeper.py`. `RunAtLoad` is false, so installing it signals nothing until the first interval. Installing it is an operator step. Also repairs a CHANGELOG fold: a stray `## Unreleased` below `## v0.268.2` held two bullets whose merges (`dc27080d`, `15e31e4e`) shipped in `v0.268.3`; both now sit under `## v0.268.3`.

## v0.268.3

- docs: **the open leg now documents the render vocabulary it actually emits, and pins the third batch/`select()` divergence.** `commands/steps/open.md` stated `<why>` was one of `flagged` / `priority <n>` / `no priority` and that a name is its line minus that suffix; `render()` emits `flagged · priority <n>` / `flagged · no priority`, so the enum named strings the code no longer produces and a suffix strip would leave the rank fragment on the name — the re-invoke guard now takes the name between the `<n>. ` prefix and the LAST ` — ` separator. The same file's skip-reason list gains `not flagged`, the gate's own reason, and `open-queue.py`'s module title, feature bullet and argparse description no longer read rank-first. The quoted-flag divergence — `is_flagged()` strips quotes and admits `flag: "true"`, while `flagged-rows.py`'s task predicate reads a YAML string and refuses it — is named as the third divergence and pinned in both directions, and a mixed flagged/unflagged fixture covers the `target=None` branch where `capacity` and `not flagged` coexist. `commands/steps/open.md`, `scripts/open-queue.py`, `scripts/tests/test_open_queue.py`.
- feat: **A wait that never resolves is carded after 24 h, so a session parked on a dead observable stops waiting silently.** `Session Lifecycle.md:43` and its edge case E5 (`:95`) require one card to the operator when a declared wait's observable has not changed for 24 h; nothing implemented it, and `scripts/declared-wait.py`'s boolean suppresses every other reader — `fleet-board.py` withholds `nudge`, `manager-predispatch.py` returns `LIVENESS_PARKED` — with nothing to expire it, so a wake that never fires waits forever. The new `scripts/drive-escalate.py` measures the declaration the record already carries: the newest `Stop`'s `ts` and its `⏰ Ends:` text, so **no live probe is needed** and a re-declaration resets the clock on its own. It posts exactly one attention card per stall, deduped on that pair, so a second round at the same observable posts nothing. State lives in `~/.claude/state/fleet-drive/waits.json` rather than in the drive ledger — `drive-ledger.py merge` replaces a session's **whole** entry via `canonical()`, so a row that is `waiting` this round and a `parked` candidate the next would drop the dedup key and re-card the same stall every time it changed class. `declared-wait.py` gains `wait_record()` and `--detail-json` so the `⏰ Ends:` read keeps one home; `agents/fleet-drive.md` gains step 0c and a `STALE WAITS` payload section. Closing the parked process stays out of scope — that is its own task. `scripts/drive-escalate.py`, `scripts/declared-wait.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_escalate.py`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`.
- feat: **`reap-idle-headless.py` gains `--all`, so a clock can call it without enumerating first.** The verb took `session_ids` as `nargs="+"` — required positionals — which is why it had no trigger: a `LaunchAgent` cannot call it bare, and nothing in the plugin enumerated session ids for it (`claude-session-census.py` pushes counts, not ids). `--all` reads `~/.claude/sessions/*.json` and selects every record that is `entrypoint: sdk-cli` **and** `status: idle` — exactly the population the `not-headless` and `busy-target` guards would refuse anyway, so an unfiltered sweep would print one refusal per tab worker per tick and bury the refusals that carry information. The other nine guards still run per candidate. It decides *which* sessions to consider, never *when* to run: the caller still owns the clock. Ids given on the command line are still honoured and are merged with the enumerated set, deduplicated; no ids and no `--all` is a usage error (exit 2), while `--all` over an empty fleet exits 0 with `nothing to do` rather than reading as a usage error. Measured 2026-11-10 on the live registry: 49 alive headless sessions, 35 reading `idle`. `scripts/reap-idle-headless.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reap_idle_headless.py`.

## v0.268.2

- fix: **`commands/goal-loop.md` no longer says the `mcp` reach comes "from the server", which read as "no emitter needed".** It needs an explicit sentinel `answer_permission` call — answering real parks does not write it, because they settle on a different arm of the handler. That wording is what produced the measured 0-`mcp`-reach state the emitter fix corrected.

## v0.268.1

- fix: **the fleet round's open leg admits only FLAGGED rows — `select()` gates on the flag instead of ranking on it.** `open-queue.py` treated `flag: true` as an ordering hint and `approved_at` as the gate, so `select()` admitted ANY eligible approved row up to the worker target; with `bborbe/vault-cli#367` having removed the approved-row admission cap, nothing bounded the open set at all (2793 approved rows measured 2026-11-10). The operator's requirement, verbatim: *"we only want auto open for flagged items"*. `select()` now filters first — an approved, eligible, un-flagged row is never selected at any position and is reported with reason `not flagged` — and `rank_key` orders only the admitted rows (priority, then oldest approval; its flag component is gone because it is constant). Provenance is still not read (`flag_set_by` is a note, never a gate). The other three auto-open limbs needed no change and are recorded as already gated: goals (`topic-spawn.py` `goal-unflagged`), topics (`topic-unflagged`), and crashed-session resume, which is correctly liveness-only so a crash never silently drops work. Tests: the one-flagged/three-unflagged fixture, a flag-removal regression case, and an agreement table against `flagged-rows.py`'s `eligible()` with the two deliberate divergences (`phase: todo`, provenance) named rather than claimed as agreement. `scripts/open-queue.py`, `scripts/tests/test_open_queue.py`, `agents/fleet-open-actor.md`, `commands/steps/readings.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`.
- fix: **A finished headless worker is closed by the fleet round instead of being messaged.** The reap leg drafted a message telling the worker to run `/vault-cli:session-close` + `/exit`, which cannot work for an `entrypoint: sdk-cli` worker: vault-cli's `session-self-exit.py` closes only the session's own WezTerm pane, proven by tty match, and a headless worker has no controlling tty, so it always answers `SELF-EXIT: stay`. Measured 2026-11-10: session `3702c7ae` sat idle 45 minutes after its task read `completed`/`done`/zero boxes with its PR merged, while `close-worker.py --dry-run` answered `✅ would close pid 5229 (entrypoint sdk-cli, no pane)` — the mechanism worked and nothing invoked it. `commands/steps/drive.md` now resolves each `REAPS` line's roster name through `session-liveness.py --list --json`, runs `close-worker.py <sid> --dry-run`, and on a `(entrypoint sdk-cli, no pane)` row closes it for real and sends nothing — a tab row keeps the reap message, and any refusal token is reported with the guards intact. ⚠️ **The read is in the command, not the drive agent:** `agents/fleet-drive.md`'s `allowed-tools` grants no `Bash(python3:*)` (`docs/blocked-row-reread.md` § Who runs the probe), so the leg only points at the caller. `agents/goal-drive.md` step 1 owns the same tier read itself (it holds `python3`) and its output contract gains a `Closed (headless)` bucket. `commands/fleet-drive.md`'s INLINE rule becomes *close via `close-worker.py` only*; `commands/steps/reap.md` carries the carve-out. New `scripts/check-headless-reap-close.py` (with its test) gates the prose, and the existing `test_close_worker.py` exercises the headless close and every refusal.

## v0.268.0

- feat: **`attention-ask.py poll` prints `ANSWERED_CLIENT:` beside `ANSWERED_BY:`**, rendering the store-recorded `answered_client` — `user_agent`, `remote_addr` and `forwarded_for` — so "who answered this card" is one command away instead of a grep of the controller's 182 MB stderr log. `answered_by` names the surface; the client is what separates the operator's browser from a script. `forwarded_for` renders in its own parenthetical because the header is caller-set, never in place of the peer address. An item that recorded no client prints no line.

## v0.267.1

- fix: **the topic tier can now answer parks, so its `mcp` reach can actually be written.** `commands/topic-loop.md` and `commands/topic-drive.md` granted only `list_agents` and `agent_status` from the supervisor, so the sentinel `answer_permission` call added to `commands/steps/round-liveness.md` would have been refused before reaching the server — the same 0-`mcp`-reach failure this change exists to fix, left unfixed for one of its two tiers. A topic manager may open a worker (`docs/session-tiers.md`), and a worker it opened parks with it, so the grant closes a pre-existing gap as well.
- fix: **the answer-reach `mcp` leg now has an emitter, so the reach record can actually carry both routes.** `commands/steps/round-liveness.md` and `commands/steps/re-arm.md` said the leg "needs nothing" because the server writes it on `answer_permission`'s foreign-park reply — true of the server, false of the caller: a manager only ever calls with a real park id, which IS in the server's `pending` map, so the settle path runs and the foreign-park arm never does. Measured 2026-11-10: **18 `wrapper` reaches and 0 `mcp` reaches**. Both steps now spell out the sentinel call, which settles nothing and is the only writer of that leg.

## v0.267.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:fleet-reset` clears the fleet's remembered state, and — behind a separate opt-in — the task-file links that make a keep-alive leg resume what the reset retired.** The fleet remembered itself in five stores (`~/.claude/sessions/*.json`, `state/worker-manager/*.json`, `state/open-items/*.json`, `state/session-holds.json`, `state/fleet-snapshot.json`) with no verb to clear them, and `/supervisor:reset` cannot be repurposed because its contract is *never deletes*. `scripts/fleet-reset.py` stops every session **before** removing its registry entry (SIGTERM, `--grace`, then SIGKILL; a pid that survives keeps its entry and the run exits 3), clears the five stores under one `flock` with atomic unlinks, and prints each store's `before → after` count. Three gates, each tested in both directions: `--confirm fleet-reset` is the only thing that authorises deletion (no token → exit 2 and nothing changes); `--dry-run` prints the full plan and moves no mtime under any store; and `--strip-task-links` is a SEPARATE flag that removes `claude_session_id` / `metrics_sessions` from non-terminal task files only, so a state-only run leaves every task file byte-identical. This session's own pid and its ancestors are never signalled or unregistered. `scripts/tests/test_fleet_reset.py` covers all of it.

## v0.266.0

- fix: **The stood-down closer skips `task--` slugs, so a worker's stand-down is never consumed as a close.** `/supervisor:stop "<task>"` writes `task--<base>.stopped`, and the closer's `LIVE` test passes on that row *by construction* — the ownership guard that wrote the marker required the caller to be exactly the session the page records — so the leg would hand the page over and close the session 60 minutes later, turning a deliberate stop into a close that `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Session end forbids. The leg closes managers, never workers. `commands/steps/keepalive-stood-down.md`.

- feat: **`/supervisor:stop` accepts a name, so a topic, goal or task can be stood down by name from the session that owns it.** The command was session-scoped by design and took no argument, which meant a stand-down could not be *named* — the subject was inferred from this session's registration, and an unregistered session had nothing to name at all. The by-name form resolves the name to its subject page the way `/supervisor:open` does (never guessing on ambiguity), **refuses** with `not-owned: <subject> is not this session's subject` unless this session owns it, and writes the subject's `<branch>--<base>.stopped` marker. The ownership test is a new caller-anchored resolution in `scripts/owner.py` (`--session` + `--subject` + `--vault`): the page's `claude_session_id` must equal the caller. `of_session()` could not serve here — it answers *"is the caller live and is the subject's loop armed"*, which a live foreign worker passes, so the guard would have been inert. `cannot-tell` refuses alongside `not-owner`, because an unreadable probe licenses nothing. `task` joins `goal`/`topic`/`fleet` in both `BRANCHES` and both folder tables, so `--branch task` resolves a task page instead of refusing `EXIT_UNRESOLVED`. The bare form is unchanged. `commands/stop.md`, `scripts/owner.py`, `scripts/manager-liveness.py`.

## v0.265.6

- fix: **the fleet-wide cap counts a resumed worker, so the keep-alive leg can no longer overrun it.** `server/worker-sessions.mjs` and `scripts/worker-sessions.py` skipped every ledger record carrying `resumed_from`, so a resumed worker was live, consumed exactly the load the cap exists to bound, and was invisible to it — measured 2026-11-10, **22 counted against 78 live worker sessions**, and **31 against 99** earlier the same day; every session the counter missed was a resume, and the keep-alive leg had resumed 34 dead workers in a single round. ⚠️ **The exclusion was written down deliberately, and that is what made it a defect rather than an accident** — it was codified on 2026-11-07 when the liveness channel widened (before that it held by construction, because the registry could not see a headless worker at all), with the rationale that counting resumes "would leave a sweep that revived two dead workers unable to start any new one". That inverts the cap: a sweep that revives two dead workers has **added two to the fleet**, and must be refused at the ceiling like any other addition. `role: manager` is now the only exclusion. ⚠️ **The crash-loop cap on the resume path is a separate control and is unchanged** — it bounds how many resumes a round may perform, and is not a reason to remove a live session from this census. `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 5 is corrected with it, including its path-B clause, which claimed a ledger-less resume **is** counted when the counter iterates ledger records and therefore cannot count it; the remaining asymmetry (path B, `restart-worker.py --absent` and the operator's own spawn are uncapped) is stated in the direction that is actually true. Tests flipped in both twins — the exclusion case, the populations split and the resumed-manager case — with the manager-exclusion control kept beside each so a flipped test cannot pass on a counter that returns 0 for everything.

## v0.265.5

- fix: **`worker-keepalive.py` no longer offers to resume a session whose recorded id is the string `null`.** `scalar()` returns the literal text after the colon, so `claude_session_id: null` arrived as four non-empty characters — and `session-liveness.py`'s `is_session_id` admits any lowercase prefix by design (it must accept the 12-char recovery prefix its own `AMBIGUOUS` verdict hands back), so the probe answered `ABSENT` and `worker-keepalive.py` printed `resume` for a session that was never named. Measured 2026-11-08 against the row *Name the Cause If an A2A Worker Pod Is Replaced Mid-Turn Again*, whose page carries `claude_session_id: null`. `topic-keepalive.py` gains `session_id()`, which folds YAML's spellings of "nothing" (`""`, `[]`, `null`, `~`, case-insensitively — the set `goals-declaration.py` already uses for "declares nothing") to `""` at the one place the id is read; `worker-keepalive.py` uses it at both call sites (the task page and the goal pages it collects) and `topic-spawn.py` mirrors it, per the same deliberate duplication as `flagged`. The fold itself lives in `owner.py`'s `page_session_id` — the single reader of a page's session id — and `topic-keepalive.py`'s `session_id` delegates to it, so a second implementation cannot drift. The fold is deliberately **not** inside `scalar()`: that helper reads every key on the page, and `defer_date: null` must keep reaching `defer_state()` as the unreadable value that parks a row rather than as "no defer". Tests: the three shapes (absent, empty, literal `null`) assert exit 1, `cannot-tell` on line 1, and zero calls to `session_verdict` — the state-independent form, since an output-only check would pass on a day the registry read failed — plus an agreement test so the two `session_id` copies cannot split.

- fix: **`park-event.py release` and `register` accept the slug that `due` and `list` print, so the documented `--event "<key>"` form works with the token a caller actually copies.** The two subcommands disagreed about what "the key" was: watch files are keyed on `slugify(description)`, and `slugify` appends `sha256(input)[:8]` — so a printed slug cannot be inverted back to a path — while `due` and `list` print `{slug} · event: {description}` first. `resolve_event_key()` now resolves either form to the stored description, scanning the records on a path miss, and it runs **before** `locked()`: `lock_path` is `slugify(key).lock`, so resolving afterwards would put a slug and its description on two different lock files and let their read-modify-write cycles interleave. The unknown-key refusal now names the keys that exist (`no watch for event key 'x' (known: a metric)`), because a bare "no watch" reads as "nothing is parked" rather than "you passed the wrong identifier". Change set: `scripts/park-event.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_event.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.265.4

- fix: **The stood-down close is a numbered step, not a footnote.** v0.261.0 shipped the close as a step file plus a pointer, and the pointer was a trailing clause inside `section 4`'s digest-count paragraph — no imperative, no heading, no place in the sequence. Section 1 sees a `STOPPED` manager and correctly declines to restart it, and nothing then acted on it, so the leg closed nothing in any round. It became a footnote because `check-budget` refused the actor at its 30720 body budget: the procedure moved to a step file and the pointer was cut to a noun phrase to fit the remaining characters, so *"the reference exists"* was read as *"the reference is followed"*. Now `## 4b. Stood-down close` with an imperative, paid for by tightening four verbose clauses in the same file; `commands/steps/keepalive.md`'s procedure count corrected from five to six. `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`.

## v0.265.3

- fix: **`check-open-manager-resolution` now pins the launcher invocation on both manager create arms, so a rewrite that drops `--default-source manager-default` fails the build instead of silently putting a manager on the `-claude` sibling.** `manager-launcher.py` falls to its sibling rung when `--default-source` is omitted or `vault`, and `resolveManagerLauncher` (`server/spawn-cwd.mjs:266`) does the same when `managerDefault` is absent — which is how a goal manager came up on `claude-opus-5-5` on 2026-11-10 while every configured rung said `cc-private-deepseek`. The three code arms already carry guards (`server/spawn-cwd.test.mjs:280`, `test_flagged_rows.py:426-433`, `test_restart_worker.py:355,429,453`); the two `commands/open.md` prose arms carried none. `LAUNCHER_MARKERS` pins the **whole invocation** per branch, not the bare flag — Step 2C also mentions the flag in prose ("tells it the value came from config"), so a substring marker passes the very mutation it exists to catch; measured, the first version of this guard did. `test_missing_launcher_flag_fails` is the negative control. Change set: `scripts/check-open-manager-resolution.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_open_manager_resolution.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.265.2

- fix: **the proposal payload's row name is resolved from its own file path, so a task name containing ` — ` is no longer lost.** The payload is `<name> — <why> — <file>:<line>`, and **no split direction works**: `source.file` is `25 Tasks/<name>.md`, so a name carrying ` — ` puts the separator in the path too — 65 files in `25 Tasks/` do. `split(" — ")[0]` truncated such a name (4 of the 33 unresolvable blocks on the first live day), a right-split lands inside the path instead, and since v0.265.1's resolve check those rows are *refused*, silently dropping the operator's `add it to the plan` answer. `_name_from_payload()` now resolves in three descending steps: the payload's **`.md:<line>` tail** — the whole payload ends with `<name>.md:<line>`, so the stem of everything before it is the name, exact even when the name (and therefore the path) carries the separator — then the **longest** leading ` — `-joined prefix that resolves to a page (longest-first, so a row genuinely named `Foo` is not captured by an existing `Foo — bar`), then the first segment unchanged, which `add_to_plan` refuses. Alert rows are the exception: their name is a raw `prometheus-alerts.sh` line that can never be a page, so they split from the right against their fixed tail rather than being oracle-resolved into some unrelated task. Change set: `scripts/next-focus-answer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_next_focus_answer.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.265.1

- fix: **`next-focus-answer.py` no longer writes plan rows that can never resolve, and reads a shell-quoted payload correctly.** Measured 2026-11-10 on the first live day: 110 `### N. 📌` blocks written, **33 naming no page** — 22 carrying a leading stray quote (`📌 '190 of 193 Sweep Snapshots…`), 4 carrying the full `'"'"'` escape (`📌 'A Headless Worker'"'"'s Vault Write…`), and one named `proposed for this week plan (see next-focus reading)`, which is the card's *context*, not its name. A block whose `**Task:** [[name]]` resolves to no page is inert — `plan_candidates` drops it with `no page in tasks/goals/topics`, the row never enters `planned_keys`, and it re-proposes while the note reads as planned. Verified, not inferred: `190 of 193 Sweep Snapshots…`, `A Cited Board Card Id…` and `A Headless Worker's Vault Write…` were all still under `proposed` in the same run that carried their mangled blocks. Two causes, both fixed: the consumer read the store's `shlex.quote` form raw, so the quote and the `'"'"'` escape survived into the name (the 77 rows whose names needed no quoting were written correctly, which is why the defect hid); and there was no resolve check, so a payload that is not `name — why — file:line` at all still got a block. `unquote_payload()` now unquotes a single-`shlex`-token payload and returns anything else verbatim, and `add_to_plan` refuses — as a per-card `note:`, never an abort — when the name resolves to no page. Only a **permanent** refusal (the name can never be a page) is recorded, as `action: "refused"` in the consumption log, so `pending` drains — without it an unresolvable name re-rendered the same note every tick forever (measured on three consecutive live ticks, `pending=1` each). A **transient** refusal (an unreadable note, a fresh week with no `### N.` blocks yet) is deliberately NOT recorded, because that would silently drop the operator's `add it to the plan` answer when the next tick could succeed. Neither writes a dismissal record — that would suppress the row if its page is created later. `_page_path` delegates to `Board.find`, so the check is the same predicate the reader resolves with, containment guard included. Change set: `scripts/next-focus-answer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_next_focus_answer.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.265.0

- feat: **A manager whose subject has reached a terminal state is now reaped, so an idle manager stops outliving the work it was started for.** The reap contract (`agents/fleet-drive.md` step 1b) reads three facts from a **task** file — `status`, `phase`, the open-box count — and a manager has no task file: its subject is a goal or topic page, and the only record of the manager on it is the page's session id. So a manager bound to a finished subject matched nothing and was never closed. Measured 2026-10-30: `Codex Drives a Permanently Running Claude Session` read `status: completed` / `phase: done` carrying a manager session id, and that session was `LIVE` with its loop `STOPPED` — idle in that state for 31 minutes before anyone looked. Nothing was wrong with the session; the **binding** was stale. New `scripts/subject-reap.py` is the missing join: it scans every configured vault's `goals_dir` and `topics_dir`, keeps the pages whose `status` is `completed` or `aborted`, reads the manager recorded on each (`claude_session_id`, falling back to the retired `manager_session_id` that `commands/open.md` § Step 2B/2C still reads), and probes that id with `session-liveness.py --check`. ⚠️ **Terminality decides candidacy; liveness decides what to do with the candidate** — `manager_session_id` is a lead, never a verdict. Only `LIVE` is reapable; `ABSENT` and `MALFORMED` are **silence** (the manager already ended, or the value could not have named one), and `UNKNOWN`/`AMBIGUOUS` are **reported and never acted on**, the same fail-closed split the rest of the plugin uses. A `hold` subject is deliberately not terminal: it is paused, not finished. ⚠️ **The reap is non-authorising by construction** — `reap-line.py` gains a `--subject` form that names the three reads it was decided from (the subject's `status`, the `manager_session_id`, the liveness verdict) and emits the same evidence hand-over the task reap emits; nothing kills a process and nothing writes session state, and the session ends by its own `/supervisor:close` or `/exit`. ⚠️ **The reap line carries the ROSTER NAME, not the session id** — `SendMessage` takes the exact name the live roster reports, and a manager's name is not derivable from its page (page title, subject title and session name are three different strings), so the scan reads `session-liveness.py --list --json` once and puts the name in the line; a line whose slot still holds an id is UNaddressable and the step says to report it and send nothing. The leg posts one `ack` attention card per reaped subject (dedup key `manager-subject-reap:<vault>:<subject>` — the vault is in the key because goal and topic titles are not namespaced across vaults, so a title-only key would let one vault's card suppress the other's reap) and asks the operator nothing — an `ack` reports a condition; a `message` card here would manufacture a gate the leg has no question for. Placement: router row **16b** in `commands/fleet-drive.md`, immediately after the open leg, with `commands/steps/manager-reap.md` as its home and `commands/steps/reap.md` still a pointer to the canonical contract. ⚠️ **`commands/fleet-drive.md` is a wakeup target under the strict 10 240-byte tick cap and sat 3 bytes under it**, so the row was paid for by tightening prose in the same file rather than by raising the cap — the tick cap has no baseline exemption by design. `scripts/check-acting-legs.py` learns the new row: 16b acts (it posts the card and hands over every reap line), so it joins the delegate rule's carve-out. ⚠️ **Bounded and fail-closed per page:** each probe carries a 30 s timeout (`unknown`, never a verdict), an unreadable or undecodable page is reported as `UNKNOWN` rather than silently dropped (it could be the terminal subject carrying a live manager), and a `--vault-root` with neither a goals nor a topics directory is REFUSED instead of rendering `terminal=0`. Pinned by `scripts/tests/test_subject_reap.py` (15 cases) against a fixture vault: terminal + LIVE reaps (naming subject, session and all three reads) · an **open** subject with the same LIVE manager is never scanned (the positive control) · four fixtures — live / none / malformed / `ABSENT` — produce exactly one reap and three silences · an unreachable store is `UNKNOWN`, never a reap · and an AST read of the script proves no `signal` import, no kill call and no file opened for writing. Change set: `scripts/subject-reap.py`, `scripts/reap-line.py`, `scripts/tests/test_subject_reap.py`, `commands/steps/manager-reap.md`, `commands/steps/reap.md`, `commands/steps/open.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `scripts/check-acting-legs.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.264.7

- fix: **`close-worker.py` resolves a session's task in any configured vault, not only those whose directory is named `25 Tasks` or `24 Tasks`.** The resolution path carried a hardcoded `TASK_DIRS = ("25 Tasks", "24 Tasks")`, so a vault whose `vault-cli` config declares `tasks_dir: tasks` (`seibert-agent`, `private-agent`) was dropped from both the cwd walk-up and the whole-vault scan — a finished worker anchored there read `task-unresolved` forever and could never be reaped. `local_task_dirs` and `task_dirs_for` now take the directory from each vault's declared `tasks_dir` (read once per process from `vault-cli config list --output json`, overridable with `SUPERVISOR_VAULT_CONFIG` for drills), and the goal/topic lookup keeps its explicit tuples. A vault with `tasks_dir: null` contributes nothing. ⚠️ **The refusal now names its cause:** the no-page case prints `no page carries <id>`, while a session whose own vault is not among the directories searched prints `searched <dirs>; <vault> not among them` — the second was previously indistinguishable from the first and sent every reader after a missing page that existed.

## v0.264.6

- fix: **A round re-reads a step body whose file content changed, so a shipped leg is no longer skipped after a plugin release.** The router rule re-read a step only when its text was *not* in context — and after `/reload-plugins` it still is, because the session holds the body it read at session start with the paths pinned to the version directory current then. Measured 2026-11-05: six rounds on `0.240.0` skipped the keepalive leg for 80 minutes; the Fleet Manager's last `Read` of `commands/steps/keepalive.md` was the `0.238.5` body, which carries no park-nudge procedure. The three routers (`commands/goal-drive.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/fleet-triage.md`) now also re-read on a **content** change: `shasum -a 256` the step file, compare it against the hash recorded when the body was read (`mkdir -p ~/.claude/state/step-body-hashes`, then appended to `<session-id>.txt`), and re-read on a mismatch — printing `read under <hash>` beside `now <hash>` so the refresh is visible in the round's own output rather than only in the re-read. A **missing record counts as stale**: the case this exists for is a body read before the rule did, so "no record" must refresh rather than pass. ⚠️ A version compare was rejected on measurement, not taste: 2026-11-08 20:48, four rounds ran against `0.251.3` step files while five releases shipped, and all four step files the round reads were byte-identical — a version trigger fires on every release (a blanket re-read by another name) while still missing a step file edited in place.

## v0.264.5

- test: **A test now fails when a chain tier has no keeper.** `docs/session-tiers.md` § Keep-alive declares itself the single home of the keep-alive contract, and its `### The chain` table was prose: a row whose *Mechanism* cell named a script that had been renamed, split or deleted still read as covered and nothing failed. New `scripts/tests/test_session_tiers_chain_keepers.py` parses the table and asserts the **7 named tier rows**, that **every** backticked `scripts/` / `commands/` / `agents/` artifact in each row resolves, and that the one keeperless row is the background sub-agent's **deliberate** `no keeper, by decision 2026-11-08` pair rather than an omission. The row count is an independent literal, so a parser that silently drops a row cannot also supply the number it is graded against. ⚠️ **Artifacts live in *Mechanism*, not *Kept alive by*** — that column names an actor (`the operator`, `the fleet round`), so reading it as the artifact carrier fails six of seven rows. ⚠️ **Every artifact in a cell is checked, not the first** — the worker row names three, and a first-token-only implementation stays green while `scripts/worker-keepalive.py` is dead. ⚠️ The extractor is anchored on the `scripts/` / `commands/` / `agents/` prefixes, because the *Guard* cells carry non-path backticks (`STOPPED`, `jump`, `crash-loop`, `worker--<slug>`, an unprefixed `worker-keepalive.py`). ⚠️ The mutation controls are scoped to the chain subsection: `| **Fleet manager**` also opens a row of the tier-summary table in `### The four tiers`, so an unscoped row search mutates the wrong table and the deletion control reads 7 rows — green for the wrong reason. Red-then-green pinned against the real doc: deleting a row reads `6 != 7`, and renaming the worker row's **second** artifact fails naming the tier and the path while `agents/goal-drive.md` in the same cell stays valid.

## v0.264.4

- docs: **`server/supervisor.mjs` is marked DEPRECATED as the live MCP surface, because it is not the supervisor tool any session binds.** The live tool is the Go `claude-wrapper` daemon at `http://127.0.0.1:18090/supervisor/mcp` — every `cc-*` launcher loads `~/.claude/mcp-private-personal.json` with `--strict-mcp-config`, and this plugin ships no `.mcp.json` of its own. Nothing on the operator's machine starts the JS server: it is reachable only from the drill scripts and from `scripts/mcp-start.py`, the plugin's own launcher for a deployment that wires an `.mcp.json`. Measured 2026-11-09: a headless-spawn model fix was written, tested and then discarded here, because the live path was `bborbe/claude-wrapper` `pkg/roster_mcp.go`. A banner now sits at the top of the file, `CLAUDE.md` § Where a worker runs names the real surface, and the Layout table no longer calls it "the MCP server". ⚠️ A prose banner is not a control — the operator's machine also carries a `PreToolUse` deny hook on this path, and that hook is what actually stops the edit.

## v0.264.3

- fix: **A pane delivery no longer takes the operator's focus.** `sendToPane` (`server/tab.mjs`) ran `activate-pane` before every `send-text`, and nothing restored the previously focused pane — so an operator typing while the fleet pushed had their half-written line land in the worker's composer, where the following Enter submitted it as an instruction. The activation existed because a 2026-09-14 measurement showed `send-text` "doing nothing" until `activate-tab` ran first; **that measurement does not reproduce.** Against a raw-mode target (`stty -icanon -echo`) `send-text --pane-id` delivers 6/6 bytes with `wezterm cli list-clients` reporting the same `focused_pane_id` before and after (measured 2026-11-09, wezterm `20260716-195552-76b606ec`). ⚠️ The original 0-byte readings were a **canonical-mode artefact**: a line-buffered tty echoes the text into `get-text` while withholding it from the process until a newline, so the screen showed a delivery that never happened — the same reason `get-text` is not a delivery receipt. Change set: `server/tab.mjs` (activation removed, header note 1 corrected), `server/tab.test.mjs` (asserts the absence of activation rather than its order), `server/supervisor.mjs` (`send_agent_message`'s description no longer advertises focus theft), `scripts/send-delivery-drill.py`, `scripts/pane-wake.py`, `scripts/plugin-version-census.py`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`. `scripts/jump.py` keeps its activation — taking you to the pane is what it is for.

## v0.264.2

- docs: **which implementation owns a session-ledger row's terminal verdict is now written down.** `docs/ledger-ownership.md` states the rule — the process that holds the child's stdin is the only writer of that child's `status` / `result` / `ended_at` — names the live implementation (the Go daemon `bborbe/claude-wrapper`, which every vault's MCP entry points at), lists the four writes it makes across a row's life, and marks the Node terminal patch in `server/agent-loop.mjs` as the **Node-only path, dead on this fleet**: `pgrep -f supervisor.mjs` returns nothing and the daemon owns the port those configs name. The loop is retained as the reference semantics the Go port was measured against and is still correct for a Node-hosted deployment, but nothing may add a second writer for a Go-spawned worker — whichever PATCH landed last would silently become the row's verdict, including turning a finished worker back into a `running` one. The file's own header carries the same warning, so a reader of the patch cannot miss it.

## v0.264.1

- fix: **A manager round writes a second liveness beat at its tail, so a round that outruns the staleness limit no longer reaches its own end already reading `STALE`.** Row 0a (`commands/steps/round-liveness.md`) arms at the head by design — a by-hand round that stops at the pre-dispatch gate must still leave a beat — and nothing armed at the tail, so every session checking `manager-liveness.py --state` mid-round was told a live, working manager had lapsed. `commands/steps/round-tail.md` gains **step 9**, the tick's last write, re-running row 0a's `--state` allow-list block with `--beat tail`: only `OK` / `STALE` / `UNARMED` / `INVALID` arm, `STOPPED` / `SLEEPING` / `WRONG-BRANCH` / empty do not, `--resume` is never passed, and it runs after step 8's receipt check so a skipped leg stays visible. ⚠️ **The second beat is only safe because `observed_period()` now measures ROUND-to-round.** It is a p90 capped at 2.5x the median, so a two-beat ring — gaps alternating `D` and `I−D` — collapses the median to the smaller gap and puts the cap *below* the round-to-round gap it must cover. Measured on a 900 s interval with 120 s rounds: median 120, cap 300, p90 780 → period 300, limit 480, against a 900 s gap, i.e. a false `STALE` on every quiet round — the exact failure the tail beat exists to remove. `parse_arm_lines()` therefore carries an `is_tail` marker (a third column, `tail`, written only by `--arm --beat tail`; a legacy two-column line reads as a head), `arm()` preserves it across the ring rebuild, and `observed_period()` builds its spans from **head beats only**. `PERIOD_CAP` and `SLACK` are untouched. Pinned by `test_observed_period_ignores_intra_round_tail_beats` — which fails at 300 != 900 against the previous code — plus two controls: a ring with no tail beats is unchanged, and a legacy two-column line still reads as a head. ⚠️ **`scripts/budget-baseline.json` records `commands/steps/round-tail.md: 31618`, and the reason is not convenience.** That file carried 30717 bytes against a 30720 body budget — three bytes of headroom — so the rule could not be added without a raise. The alternative placement, in `commands/steps/round-liveness.md` (read at row 0a, at the head), was written and rejected: a mandatory tail action stated only at the head has to survive a whole round in the reader's memory while the file it follows at the tail says nothing, which is the "a round that skips a step reads as complete" failure this beat exists to prevent. A separate step file plus a router row was also tried; `commands/goal-drive.md` (10105) and `commands/topic-drive.md` (10238) sit at their strict 10240 tick cap and could not take the row. ⚠️ **The baseline entry is a ratchet, not an allowance** — `check-budget.py` fails if the file grows past it. ⚠️ **It is a liveness RECORD, not the `ScheduleWakeup` arm** — step 7's *"do not re-arm here"* is the wakeup, and step 7 now says which one it means. ⚠️ **The cost is stated in the step rather than implied:** two beats per round split each gap into `D` and `I−D`, so the `.arms` population becomes `[D, I−D, …]` and `observed_period()`'s p90 rises toward `max(D, I−D)`. The limit therefore grows with the round — the false `STALE` is removed by re-measuring the cadence, not by shortening the gap, and a round longer than the limit still reads `STALE` during it. A mid-round beat would shorten the gap instead; that is a different change. The fleet tier's own tail (`commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md`) has a separate receipt contract and is not covered here.

## v0.264.0

- fix: **the ownership-filtered feed no longer wakes a manager on attention cards that are already closed or answered, and a store-read failure is now a different line.** `GET /api/1.0/attention` returns OPEN items only, so `headless_items` answered "no record" both for a card that is no longer open and for a read that raised; both rendered `session=unresolved` and both woke the arm. Measured 2026-11-05 → 11-07 on one goal manager: 3 of 3, then 7 of 10, then **18 of 18** wake rows dead or foreign, and the arm was stopped rather than re-armed. New `store_states()` derives a per-key state — `open`, `not-open` (a good read, and the card is not on it) or `unread` (the read itself failed) — and `evaluate_panes()` drops `not-open` rows as `dropped(not-open)` before any ownership hop, counted on every `LIVENESS` line. A headless wake line now carries **exactly one of three tokens**: `session=<id>`, `session=unattributed` (open gate whose record carries no `producer_id` — a candidate to confirm, never foreign) or `store=unreachable` (fail-open, unchanged). ⚠️ **`session=unresolved` is retired** — it was the collision itself. `headless_items_checked()` names a failed read; `headless_items()` keeps its `[]`-as-unread contract for `round-readings.py`. Change set: `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.263.1

- fix: **A retry is now marked as a retry, so one episode stops counting as one firing per poll.** `--check` re-emits an owed lapse on EVERY poll by design — that re-emission IS the retry, and `manager-liveness-notify-tick.sh` depends on it to survive a failed transport — so a consumer counting emitted lines reported the failure rate once per poll instead of once per episode. Measured 2026-11-08 over `2026-11-07T20:58:41Z → 2026-11-08T02:43:41Z`: **165 firings for ONE `fleet--fleet` crossing**, against 227 `rc=10` ledger rows in the window. The first emission of a crossing is now the firing; every later one carries a trailing `retry=1`, driven by `pending[s].emits` (carried through `read_pending`'s normalising reader, so a rewrite cannot reset the count — it is deliberately NOT `attempts`, which counts failed deliveries). ⚠️ `retry=1` is **trailing, never a prefix**: the tick matches `^(STALE|INVALID|RECOVERED-PENDING) ` on line start and reads the subject from field 2, so a `RETRY STALE …` spelling would satisfy every test here and silently drop every retry from delivery. ⚠️ The exit code is unchanged — `--check` still exits 10 on every poll while a lapse is owed, because that is what gates the tick's whole stale block; a consumer counting ledger ROWS must key on the marker instead of on `rc=10`. A genuine second crossing after the ack still emits as a new firing. Pinned by `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`: the first emission carries no marker, the second does, the line still parses positionally, and a post-ack re-lapse is a new firing.

## v0.263.0

- feat: **A manager's answer to a worker's question is now recorded where the worker's own hook can read it.** The local attention event log records the session that ASKED and the event that cleared the gate, never the party that answered, so a question a manager settled was indistinguishable from one the operator answered — which made "worker questions that no manager answered" uncountable. `server/answer-marker.mjs` writes `<worker-session>.answer.json` (atomic, pid-suffixed temp + rename, **epoch-seconds** `ts` — milliseconds read as future-dated to the reader and are silently rejected), and two producers call it: the **wrapper-answer path** in `server/supervisor.mjs` (`answerWrapperPark` — the only path that settles a headless park since the wrapper migration left `pending` unpopulated) when the parked tool is an `AskUserQuestion`, and `scripts/attention-answer.py` for `/supervisor:attention-next`, which `commands/attention-next.md` defines as relaying the **operator's** words — so that arm writes kind `operator`, not `manager`. ⚠️ The marker is keyed on the worker's **session id** (`agent.sessionId`), never the in-process roster label (`agent.id`, `agent_<n>`) the attention state dir is not keyed by. ⚠️ **The reader is the operator-local `~/.claude/hooks/attention-log.py`**, which stamps `answerer_session` / `answerer_kind` onto the close line; neither half does anything alone — a marker nobody reads, or a reader whose file never exists — so they ship as a pair. A `permission` verdict deliberately writes no marker: `permissions.jsonl` already attributes it, and a second attribution with no reader would be the two-counters defect this repo refuses elsewhere. Best-effort by design — a failed write returns `false` and never throws, because trading a missing attribution for a stranded worker is the wrong way round. Pinned by `server/answer-marker.test.mjs`: the hook's kind vocabulary, the seconds-not-milliseconds magnitude, the operator answer's empty session surviving as `""` rather than being dropped, and a non-throwing failure when the state directory cannot be created.

## v0.262.0

- fix: **`manager-liveness.py --arm` no longer erases a deliberate stand-down, and the cadence reset moves to a file of its own.** An arm runs at EVERY round — it is the liveness *reading*, not a decision about the subject — and it used to delete `<slug>.stopped`. Measured 2026-11-07: the Several Clusters Manager stood down at 12:13, took its 12:51 round's reading, the marker vanished, the Fleet Manager read `STALE` and restarted the session at 13:14; the rules already warn *"a `STALE` manager reading is arithmetic, not proof of a lapsed loop"*, and this made the warning unenforceable. The clearing was **documented as deliberate** — a re-arm after a stand-down must start a NEW cadence sample, or the outage gap counts as cadence and the adaptive stale limit never recovers — so the fix decouples the two facts instead of dropping the reset: `--stop` now also writes `<slug>.reset`, and `--arm` consumes that epoch to set `prior = []` while leaving `<slug>.stopped` alone. Consumed, not merely read, so the second arm after a stop keeps its history and `observed_period` still returns. A stood-down subject resumes only through the new **`--arm --resume`**, passed by the three loop-start arms (`commands/goal-loop.md` step 3, `commands/topic-loop.md` step 3, and `commands/fleet-loop.md` § Arm procedure — the fleet tier has no separate loop-start, so its arm procedure carries the resume and the round's `commands/steps/re-arm.md` stays plain), which keeps the documented `Restart: /supervisor:goal-loop "<subject>"` working — a plain arm, which every round takes, must never pass it. Without the fleet-tier arm a `/supervisor:stop`ped fleet manager would be stranded `STOPPED` for ever: `fleet-keeper.py` leaves a stopped fleet alone by design and `answer-path-check.py` refuses every headless spawn on an unarmed loop. `commands/fleet-loop.md` therefore grows past its budget ratchet, which is raised by hand in `scripts/budget-baseline.json` (4402 → 4903) — the sanctioned remedy for a deliberate growth. ⚠️ **The same defect sat one surface out, in migration:** `migrate_legacy` deleted a pre-split `<base>.stopped`, so the first arm after the upgrade erased a legacy stand-down even once `arm()` stopped touching the marker. A legacy stand-down is now **renamed** onto its prefixed path and never removed, while `cadence`/`arms`/`reset` are still retired and `reset` joins `LEGACY_EXTS`. Docs that promised *"the next `--arm` clears it"* are corrected: `docs/subject-resolution.md` § stand-down, `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Session end, `commands/steps/round-liveness.md` ¶ 17, `commands/steps/topic-resolve.md` step 4c, `commands/steps/fleet-triage-act.md`, `commands/steps/fleet-triage-state.md`, `commands/stop.md` step 2b. Pinned by `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py` — a re-arm preserves `STOPPED`, `--resume` clears it, two plain arms do not, the history resets once and only once, `observed_period` returns after four further arms, and the pre-split probe migrates to `STOPPED` with no `STALE` from `--check`.

## v0.261.0

- feat: **Fleet keep-alive closes a stood-down manager past its bound.** `/supervisor:stop` keeps
  its session by design and nothing closed it, so a stood-down manager held a fleet slot, a
  full context and a keeper-census row for a round that no longer ran.
  `manager-liveness.py --stood-down-past <MINUTES>` selects them — 60 min, 4× the round's own
  15 min cadence — and the keep-alive leg then joins each row to session liveness, hands the
  page over, proves the handover through `close-worker.py`'s own verdict, and closes. A
  refusal is reported as refused, never as closed. Measured 2026-11-09: two live stood-down
  managers at 9.3 h and 15.0 h, against 83 markers of which only those 2 were LIVE — the
  marker outlives the session it describes, which is why the join is load-bearing.

## v0.260.7

- fix: **`manager-liveness.py` no longer sets a limit below the interval a manager just lengthened to.** `--arm` now writes `<epoch> <interval>` per line (the single-column format still reads), and `observed_period()` applies a conditional floor while the 12-arm window still holds a gap armed under a shorter interval: the period becomes `max(declared, min(p90, 2.5 x max(median, declared)))`. Measured 2026-11-08 on `goal--automatic-weekly-ai-knowledge-sharing-task-in-seibert-personal`: `.cadence` 3600, window still holding ~960 s gaps, limit 2877 s against a real cadence of ~3757 s — every healthy tick would have read `STALE` until the old gaps aged out. The floor is conditional twice over, and both conditions are load-bearing. It needs a **rise**, taken two ways because the recorded one alone cannot see the motivating case: a **recorded** shorter-interval gap catches a transition on its first tick, and a window whose **p75 gap reaches half the declared interval** covers the legacy single-column rings — all 118 `.arms` files on this machine are still single-column, and on the measured histories that p75 is 3642 s for the right-skewed subject against a declared 3600, but 994 s for the declared-3600/actually-900 subject. And a completed gap at the declared interval under half of it **disproves** the new cadence and switches the floor off — without which `goal--deploy-and-merge-gates-become-standing-allow-rules`, which declares 3600 and ticks at ~900, would have gone from a 1740 s limit to 3780 s and had its lapse hidden for an hour. `arm()` rebuilds the ring from (epoch, interval) pairs rather than from the epoch-only `read_arms()`, which would have stripped every earlier interval and left the floor unable to fire on a live subject; `manager-receipt.py`'s `rearm_shape()` reads through the shared `parse_arm_lines()` instead of `fh.read().split()`, which read the interval column as an epoch. `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`.

## v0.260.6

- fix: **the closer gate's `clock` durable-observable class accepts a full ISO datetime, and `reap-closer.py`'s dead `last_ends()` export is gone.** The class compiled as `\bdefer_date\b|\b\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}\b`, and a trailing `\b` cannot match between two word characters — it failed at both ends of the datetime, between `9` and `T` and between the final digit and a `Z` suffix — so `⏰ Ends: 2026-11-09T15:30:00+01:00`, which `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` § Async State Closer names as a legal clock ("a clock (`defer_date` / timestamp)"), matched nothing and the closer was blocked: a false positive in the blocking direction, and the only way past it was to weaken the closer. The datetime arm is now optional and shape-bounded — `\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}(?:[Tt ]\d{2}(?::\d{2}(?::\d{2}(?:\.\d+)?)?)?(?:[Zz]|[+-]\d{2}:?\d{2})?)?` — and ends in `(?!\w)` rather than `\b`, so `2026-11-09T15:30Z`, `2026-11-09T15:30:00z`, `2026-11-09t15:30:00`, `2026-11-09T15` and `2026-11-09-15:30` clear while `2026-11-9` and `2026-11-09123` still block. ⚠️ The terminator must NOT include `-`: `\b` matched between `9` and `-`, so a `(?![\w-])` spelling newly blocked `2026-11-09-15:30` and `2026-11-09-rollout`, both of which the pre-PR regex cleared — a narrowing that blocks a compliant closer is the very false positive this class exists to remove.
