Keyed on `producer_id`, not `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, because `manager-attention-watch.py` posts on behalf of a parked worker: the caller is a manager and the card must read `session`. The page source matches the `claude_session_id:` **frontmatter field**, never a bare substring: a page that merely lists a worker's id in `metrics_sessions` or a Progress line must not label that worker `manager`, which is the inverse of the property this module protects. Change set: `scripts/session-role.py`, `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_role.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.251.5

- fix: **`test_permission_answer_poll` stops inheriting the host's worker mode, so `make precommit` is no longer red inside a headless supervised session.** `permission-answer-poll.py` reads `SUPERVISOR_WORKER_MODE` at the top of `main()` — the composition root, and the right place for it — so the suite driving `main()` answered for the *host* rather than for the code. Inside a headless worker the ambient value is `headless`, the three cases that expect the polling path took the early exit instead, and `test_plugin_version_census` went red beside them as a cascade: the failing poll cases leave `self.m.time.sleep` patched on the real `time` module (`test_permission_answer_poll.py:357`) and the census reload leg sleeps. Measured 2026-11-08 in one process: **74 tests, 7 red** with the ambient environment, **74 green** with `SUPERVISOR_WORKER_MODE` unset; the one-variable bisect — unset only it → green, keep only it → red — names it. The fix is a new `scripts/tests/worker_mode_isolation.py`, one assignment in one module imported for its side effect, the idiom `start_cache_isolation.py` and `fleet_sessions_isolation.py` already establish. It pins `interactive`, the documented default (`SPAWN_MODES` in `server/spawn-mode.mjs`, whose fallback returns `{ mode: 'interactive' }`) — which is also why CI never saw it. The two cases that need the other value keep injecting it themselves. Production is untouched. Change set: `scripts/tests/worker_mode_isolation.py`, `scripts/tests/test_permission_answer_poll.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.251.4

- chore: restate the `do-not-merge` gate message to name its bypass actors — the Admin repository role and Integration `3907226`, both `bypass_mode: always` in the `master-protection` ruleset — instead of asserting an unqualified block

## v0.251.3

- fix: **the drive leg's `CLOSERS` source now covers headless sessions, so a pane-less row is classifiable instead of permanently `unverifiable`.** `who-needs-me.py --section last-closers` built its population from the attention store's own records, so a session that raised no gate had no row at all — and step 4a fail-closes a candidate with no row, which made every headless worker unverifiable on every round. Measured 2026-11-07: **237 of 242** drive candidates carried no pane, **232** landed `unverifiable`, and only 14 reached the closer probe. The population is now the **heartbeat store** (`session-liveness.py --list`, union with the attention store, prefix-deduped in both directions) — the registry cannot see an in-process SDK headless worker at all — and the class is read from the **transcript**, which needs no pane; a pane-less row renders `hst` and the header prints `Last closers (<N>, headless=<H>)`. `agents/fleet-drive.md` carries the count as `headless=<H>` on the payload header and the counts line, and `docs/closer-classes.md` § The channel is pane-free records the source and the `hst` meaning.

## v0.251.2

- fix: a `claude-wrapper` restart no longer kills a streaming headless worker. The event stream
  reconnects instead of failing: every SSE frame's `id:` is tracked and sent back as
  `Last-Event-ID`, so the retry resumes at exactly the event the client stopped at, with
  exponential backoff (100 ms → 5 s, 24 attempts, 2 min cap) sized to ride out a daemon restart.
  A refused connection is treated as a restart rather than a failure. A `409` is never retried —
  it means the wrapper no longer retains that resume point, and serving a different one would
  leave an invisible hole in the transcript, so it raises `StreamGapError` instead. The backoff
  budget is **per outage, not per stream** — a parked or long-running worker whose stream has
  been open for hours still reconnects, because the clock starts when a connection fails and
  resets only on real progress (a new event id), so a wrapper stuck re-sending one event cannot
  loop forever. Every gap class — `StreamLaggedError`, `StreamGapError`, `StreamExhaustedError`
  (retry budget spent) and `StreamSessionGoneError` (a `404`/`410` mid-stream) — carries a `gap`
  marker, and `runAgentLoop` settles `error` on that marker rather than on a list of class
  names, so a captured mid-turn `result` can never settle a holed transcript `done`. The
  end-of-stream confirmation read is guarded too: a failed read there is a gap, not an
  unconfirmed `done`. A connection that carried frames but no `id:` fails loudly instead of
  reconnecting blind and duplicating the transcript — checked per connection, so a successor
  that starts serving frames without ids is caught as well. Previously undici's `TypeError: terminated` reached `runAgentLoop`'s catch and the
  worker settled `error` while its child was still generating.

## v0.251.1

- fix: **`/supervisor:open`'s RESUME branch picks its launcher by running code, not by reading prose.** § 3.1 exec'd `"<claude_script>" --resume` and left the value to a sentence in the same file (*"apply the rule yourself: `vault-cli task get … launcher`"*), so whichever session ran the resume chose the launcher and nothing checked the result. Measured 2026-11-07: two `role: agent` rows with no `launcher:` were resumed as `cc-private-claude --resume … /color cyan` because their resumer ran on Opus. New `scripts/resolve-launcher.py` prints `<launcher>\t<source>\t<role>\t<chip>` from `restart-worker.py`'s `resolve_launcher` — the same rule the restart path uses, so the two cannot disagree — and the call site now uses both fields. `resolve_launcher` also gains the `role: human` arm (`_human_launcher`), a Python port of `applyHumanLauncherRule`: the `-claude` sibling ahead of a configured `manager_launcher`, then the manager chain, with a task's own `launcher:` still winning. The chip follows the ROLE through the published role map rather than defaulting to the caller's colour. ⚠️ **A manager resume is detected first** (`restart-worker.py` `resume_role` → `session_subject`): a manager session carries no task page, so a claimant-only lookup would have read it as a worker and resumed it on the worker default — the same defect one layer down. It reads the goal/topic page's `claude_session_id`, never the live session registry: the registry is deleted when a session exits, and a manager only reaches the resume path once it has exited, so a registry-based lookup answers "not a manager" exactly where the resume happens. The chip then follows `manager` → `orange`; a manager with no goal or topic page (the root) still reads as `agent` for the chip, since the page is the only source that survives an exited session. `_human_launcher`'s last rung is the vault's `claude_script`, never the worker default — matrix Rule 4 and `applyHumanLauncherRule` both end there, and returning the worker resolution would put a human row on `-deepseek` in a vault that configures `worker_launcher` but has no `-claude` sibling and no `manager_launcher`. `session_subject` refuses two pages claiming one session id, the same guard `session_claimant` carries. ⚠️ **Both values are shape-checked before they reach the spawn**: the launcher against the same plain-path rule `resolve_launcher` applies (the `SUPERVISOR_LAUNCHER` arm skips it), and the chip against the eight colours `/color` accepts — a corrupt role map carrying shell text would otherwise be re-parsed by the `bash -lc` string that re-emits it. ⚠️ **The emitted `role` is narrowed to the three known values** — it is author-controlled frontmatter, and the output is TAB-DELIMITED and split with `cut -f4` at the call site, so a tab in a `role:` would shift every field after it and hand that text to `$CHIP`. `session_subject` matches a page's whole id set (`claude_session_id` plus `metrics_sessions`), the same rule `session_claimant` already carried. `commands/open.md` § 3.1's precedence prose is deleted and replaced by a pointer to `docs/restart-worker.md`; the body grew 78389 → 79230 bytes and its `scripts/budget-baseline.json` entry was raised deliberately. Change set: `scripts/resolve-launcher.py`, `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_resolve_launcher.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `commands/open.md`, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `docs/role-mode-matrix.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **every open PR whose branch is behind master is now rebased in a scratch worktree and handed to the operator as one push line — never merged, never pushed by the agent.** Measured 2026-11-06 in `bborbe/claude-supervisor`: 8 of 12 open PRs DIRTY, 4 of them APPROVED, and four merges that evening re-broke the set four times, because each release cut renames `## Unreleased` and re-conflicts every other open branch. `scripts/rebase-agent.py` sweeps an explicit two-repo set (`claude-supervisor`, `vault-cli`) — not "every repo", since the conflict class exists only where a release cut renames a heading that open branches also edit — and for each candidate creates a **detached** worktree at a temp path (no branch ref exists to damage, the main worktree is never entered), rebases onto `origin/master`, and either emits a push line mapping a throwaway `refs/rebase-agent/pr/<n>` onto the live branch under `--force-with-lease` or a failure report. Every value in that line is shell-quoted, since the operator pastes it into a shell and the branch name comes from the PR; a PR whose head lives in a fork is refused rather than handed a line that would push to the wrong remote. ⚠️ **The mechanism is the trap, not the conflict**: `git merge origin/master` writes a merge commit, and measured on `#652` that state was APPROVED, `MERGEABLE`/`CLEAN`, and would have merged cleanly while permanently failing the merge train's linearity probe — so the linearity gate is the negative control, and `#664` and `#653` each came out linear from a branch that carried a merge commit. A conflict in any path other than `CHANGELOG.md` is refused and named (a genuine content conflict is a worker's); a `CHANGELOG.md`-only conflict is resolved deterministically from the branch's own added bullets, and refused when the branch does more than add bullets. Seven gates block rather than warn — `git log --merges` empty, the `^[-+]## ` heading guard, the `^--[^-]` dropped-bullet probe, and the `awk` placement assertion as the **authority** (an empty heading-grep is ambiguous and is never read as a pass), plus `grep -c '^## Unreleased$'` printing 1 because a duplicate heading reads as one to the heading probes, the park size limit, and a no-op guard. `origin/master` is fetched before every sweep, since the rebase and the behind-count both read the local remote-tracking ref. Exercised on the live queue 2026-11-07: 6 push lines, 4 refusals, including `vault-cli#354` refused on a real content conflict in `scripts/session-self-exit.py` and `#660` refused after a **clean** rebase silently lost `v0.250.2`/`v0.250.1`. Poll trigger is `launchd/com.bborbe.rebase-agent.plist` at 900 s, since "detected within one poll interval with no human starting the run" cannot be met by a slash command. Rule: `docs/rebase-agent.md`; tests: `scripts/tests/test_rebase_agent.py` (14 cases, each end-to-end case on a throwaway repo). Prod-pin PRs are refused before any rebase — that approval is the operator's.

## v0.251.0

- feat: **a date-gated manager subject sleeps to its earliest `defer_date` instead of standing down.** `manager-quiet.py` gained a third verdict: when every quiet row is quiet *for a future `defer_date`* and none is runnable, it prints `sleep-until` with the earliest instant instead of `stood-down`. Standing such a subject down put a human in the loop every morning — measured 2026-11-07, the Several Clusters Manager was re-armed by hand twice in one day, fired one round, and stood itself down again. `manager-liveness.py` gained a matching fifth state: `--sleep --until <ISO>` writes `<slug>.sleeping`, `--state` reads `SLEEPING` while the instant is future (never `STALE`, which the deliberately frozen cadence would otherwise produce), a **passed** instant falls through to the normal arithmetic so a manager that failed to wake still reads `STALE`, and `--arm` clears the marker. `round-quiet.md` arms the wakeup at that instant instead of calling `/supervisor:stop`, and every liveness consumer was taught the new state — `topic-keepalive.py`, `crashed-managers.py`, `fleet-keeper.py`, `propose-managers.py` (a sleeping manager owns its subject, so it must suppress a manager proposal), `round-liveness.md` (arming clears `.sleeping`, so a sleeping subject must not be armed), `docs/sweep-output.md` (a `sleeping` disposition ranking with `stood-down`), `drive-share.py`, the two topic-sweep readers and `keepalive.md`.

## v0.250.7

- fix: **the park nudge's duration is measured per watching episode, and suppression is keyed on the episode itself.** `watching_min` was published as the span from the session's *first* `🟡 WAITING` closer ever, so it never dropped and the suppression ledger's re-arm clause was dead: a second park in the same session was either suppressed for its whole duration, or nudged instantly with a lifetime figure while its panel read *"you have sat `watching` 300 min"*. `session-activity.py` (vault-local) now restarts the span at the first `🟡 WAITING` closer of the current episode and clears it on any panel naming another state. The reading also publishes that episode's timestamp as **`watching_since`**, and the ledger keys suppression on it — the duration comparison alone is not enough, because the round cadence is 15 minutes and rounds are routinely skipped, so a reading can land after one park ended and the next began with the duration never falling. A reading that carries no `watching_since` keeps the old duration fallback. Also: a `watching` row whose duration could not be measured is counted in the round's `<m> watching` tally (matching the reading's own `counts.watching`) and keeps its ledger entry rather than losing suppression across a measurement gap. Docs in `commands/steps/keepalive.md` and the script header say episode, not lifetime. Change set: `scripts/park-nudge.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_nudge.py`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.250.6

- fix: **the park nudge reads the watching-state duration instead of transcript silence, so a session that re-arms its watcher is finally nudged.** `park-nudge.py` selected on `quiet_min` — mtime age — and a watcher re-arm writes to the transcript, so the clock reset while the wait continued. Measured 2026-11-06: a session carried **49 `🟡 WAITING` closers** across **3 h 34 m** with no terminal closer after, was re-armed **20+ times**, and read `quiet 12m` — comfortably inside the 15-minute allowance, so it was never nudged and `~/.claude/state/park-nudge/ledger.json` stayed `{}`. The selection now reads **`watching_min`**, the span from the session's first `🟡 WAITING` closer, published by `session-activity.py` (vault-local, not part of this plugin — the two halves land separately). A row with no `watching_min` is skipped rather than treated as 0. The emitted line keeps its `quiet <N>m` field name for parser compatibility, but N is now the state duration; the ledger's `last_quiet` key carries it too. Suppression is unchanged in shape — one nudge per watching episode, keyed on the session id, never on the duration. Change set: `scripts/park-nudge.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_nudge.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/park-nudge-activity.json`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.250.5

- fix: **a successful spawn now invalidates the worker-count cache, so a failed liveness read after a spawn refuses instead of releasing the next spawn on a pre-spawn number.** The cache is written only by a successful live count, so a spawn that landed after the last count left a cached value that predated it; the next spawn whose live read failed decided on that value, and every further spawn in the same 60 s window was allowed on it — a burst during a store outage ran past the very ceiling that exists to bound it. `invalidateCountCache` (`server/worker-sessions.mjs`) deletes the file, and `spawn_agent` calls it on every non-error result (`server/supervisor.mjs`), covering the tab, headless, cluster and resume paths in one place. The fallback is unchanged where it was added for: a transient store failure with no spawn immediately before it still decides on a fresh count, labelled on stderr. Change set: `server/worker-sessions.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/worker-sessions.test.mjs`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.250.4

- fix: **a failed delivery's durable record now names its producer.** `--failed` accepts `--pid` and `--row-ts` — the sender's process id and the timestamp of the ledger row it wrote in the same run — and `read_pending` carries both through every rewrite. Without them a hand-written `liveness-pending.json` reproduces every field of a real failed send exactly, so "the record was written by the tick" could only be asserted, never shown. No behaviour change to the retry, the ack or the detection limb.

## v0.250.3

- fix: **a lapse report that cannot reach its transport is retried instead of being marked reported and forgotten.** `--check` wrote `notified[s] = now` at DETECTION time, so a send that failed left the entry current and every later poll was silent — the operator learned of the lapse only by noticing it, which is the one thing the goal's criterion excludes. Measured 2026-11-07 over `~/.claude/state/manager-liveness-loop/ledger.txt`: **43** `FAILED:` deliveries, none retried, 35 of them TeamVault credential timeouts and only 4 the powered-off endpoint the goal page recorded — so this was never a one-off. **Detection is untouched**: `classify()`, `observed_period()` and `SLACK` are unchanged and the limit is still `period + SLACK` (one observed period plus 180 s). What changes is the bookkeeping: an undelivered lapse lives in `liveness-pending.json`, is re-emitted on every poll — that re-emission IS the retry — and moves to `liveness-notified.json` only when the new `--delivered <slug>` verb acks it, which the sender calls only after the transport returned success. `--failed <slug> --error <text>` records the attempt count and the transport error, so the durable record says why it could not be sent. The ack stores the CROSSING time rather than the ack time, so a manager that re-armed while its report was owed still starts a NEW lapse on its next `STALE`. A lapse whose subject recovered before delivery reads `RECOVERED-PENDING` rather than `STALE` — a recovery does not cancel an unreported lapse. `RECOVERED` and the legacy-list upgrade path are unchanged.

## v0.250.2

- fix: **the fleet round now runs `fleet-legs.py --check` at its tail, so the `legs:` verdict item 14 specifies actually reaches a render.** `commands/steps/output-shape.md` item 14 names `scripts/fleet-legs.py --check` *"the mechanical guard"* and specifies its renders, but **nothing invoked it** — no router row, no leg, no tail — so the guard was correct and its verdict was never read. Measured 2026-11-07 on `~/.claude/state/fleet-handback/2026-11-06T23-46Z.digest`: its `LEGS` section still held the sweep's seed, carried no `unactioned:` line, and `--check` exited **1** naming all three keys. `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md` gains a section that runs the check and branches on the **exit code**, never on the output, carrying that code to the `Legs` render at router row 20. ⚠️ **Every `--check` message is written to stderr** while only `legs ok: …` goes to stdout, so the section requires capturing both — a read of stdout alone hands item 14 an empty `<reason>`. The format strings keep their single home in item 14 and are not restated at the call site. Change set: `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.250.1

- fix: **the manager allowlist no longer carries `uniq` or `diff`.** `server/manager-allowlist.json` is the file that decides which prompts a manager answers *on its own* instead of escalating, and its own note states the rule the worker policy was narrowed to in `v0.249.0` / `v0.250.0`: a whole-token prefix is listed only when **every** extension of it is read-only. Two entries broke it — `uniq IN OUT` writes `OUT`, an arbitrary-path write that bypasses the policy's `Write(cwd)` confinement, and `diff -l` / `--paginate` runs `pr(1)`. The worker tier lost both already; the manager tier was never touched, so `uniq /dev/null /etc/authorized_keys` and `diff --paginate a b` were auto-answered with no operator in the loop — strictly worse than the worker case, where an escalation at least parks the request with a human. Both entries are **removed** rather than special-cased: a prefix rule cannot exclude a positional or a flag. `docs/permission-allowlist.md` is updated in the same change (its *every extension* note now names all four failures), `server/manager-allowlist.test.mjs` pins both directions — `uniq /tmp/in /tmp/out` and `diff -l a b` refuse while `ls -la /tmp`, `cat f`, `grep -rn x .` and `jq -r .x f` still allow — and `make check-manager-allowlist` still reports the doc and the JSON agreeing.

## v0.250.0

- fix: **the bundled Bash allow list no longer carries `uniq`.** `uniq IN OUT` writes `OUT`, an arbitrary-path write that bypasses the `Write(cwd)` restriction, and a prefix rule cannot exclude a positional. `uniq /dev/null /etc/authorized_keys` read `allow` before this change. `server/policy-bundled.test.mjs` pins it alongside the other refusals.

## v0.249.0

- fix: **the bundled Bash allow list no longer carries `sort`, `tree`, `rg`, `file` or `diff`.** The list's own rule is that a prefix appears only when EVERY extension of it is read-only, and those five break it: `sort -o FILE`, `sort --output=FILE` and `sort --compress-program=PROG`, plus `tree -o FILE`, write a file or run a program, `rg --pre=PROG` and `diff -l` / `--paginate` (which runs `pr(1)`) run a program, and `file -C -m PATH` / `--compile` writes `PATH.mgc` — an arbitrary-path write that bypasses the `Write(cwd)` restriction. A prefix rule cannot exclude a flag, so the only honest fix is not to list the command — exactly the rule `server/manager-allowlist.test.mjs` already pins for the manager allowlist. Caught after the allow list merged: `sort -o /etc/authorized_keys f`, `rg --pre "bash -c id" pat f`, `file -C -m /tmp/magic` and `diff --paginate a b` all read `allow`. New `server/policy-bundled.test.mjs` pins the shipped file, including every refusal the change was scoped to keep (`sed`, `find`, `git`, `python3`, `xargs`, `awk`, `env`, for-loops) and the `cd`-chain boundary.

## v0.248.1

- fix: **the round's own-parks leg actually runs — row 5d joins the delegate rule's acting carve-out.** `commands/fleet-drive.md` sends *"anything not the sweep"* to a sub-agent and excepts the acting rows by name; the carve-out listed 5b and 5c only. Row 5d shipped in `v0.244.0` and the carve-out in `v0.244.1`, so the row was added to the router after the list of exceptions had been written and nobody noticed: a delegated sub-agent holds no `mcp__supervisor__answer_permission`, so the step reached its first precondition and skipped — into a sub-agent's transcript, which is not the round's. Measured 2026-11-07: the Fleet Manager's transcript holds nine `own-parks:` lines, every one `nothing parked`, all between `2026-11-06T18:54:17Z` and `18:58:37Z`, and **none in the ~16 hours after** while its loop armed every 15 minutes (`fleet--fleet-manager.arms`, cadence 900, last arm `2026-11-07T11:04:26`) and it spawned ten workers that morning. The step had never answered a park. Change set: the carve-out now names 5b, 5c and 5d (10,234 bytes, inside the 10,240 budget); `commands/steps/own-parks.md` states at its own home that a read-only execution is not a run of it and why; `scripts/check-acting-legs.py` carries 5d in `LEGS` and its `CARVEOUT` phrase, so deleting the row from the carve-out or the acting statement from the step fails the guard — `test_own_parks_without_its_statement_fails` is the shipped defect in a fixture.

## v0.248.0

- feat: **`server/policy.json` gains anchored Bash allow rules, so read-only commands stop parking for the operator.** The file carried **no Bash allow rule at all**, so `decide()` escalated every Bash command — `ls -la /tmp` included — and a 7-day board readout measured **7 👍 / 874 👎 (score 1 %)** with roughly 800 of the 👎 permission cards. Each new entry sets `matchType: "command"` (an anchored whole-token prefix, so `ls` covers `ls -la /tmp` but not `lsblk`) and is listed only where EVERY extension of the prefix is read-only — which is why `sed` (`-n -i`), `find` (`-delete`) and every `git` prefix (a repo's own config runs code via `core.fsmonitor`) stay absent. `cd` is listed because `policy.mjs` splits a chained command and allows it only when every segment is allowed on its own, so `cd /tmp && git status` still escalates while `cd /tmp && rm -rf /` still reaches the deny rule. Verified: **30/30** in `server/policy.test.mjs`, and `git status`, `git push origin main`, `lsblk` and `for … do … done` all still escalate.

## v0.247.5

- fix: **a manager no longer restates a relayed gate on its own closer.** `commands/steps/needs-input.md` and `commands/steps/goal-gates.md` now carry the same rule in their two homes: a relayed gate's card belongs to the session that raised it, so the manager ends on `🟡 WAITING` naming that pane with **no `👤 You:` minting verb** — a `you run:` / `review:` / `pick —` / `approve:` line mints a fresh card for a decision the board already carries, while a cardable class-C relay keeps its ONE board line and no per-gate jump link. ⚠️ **This is the relay case, which v0.206.0's `--closer` rule does not reach in practice.** That release covered the post-then-close case — a session that posts a card and then closes on the same ask. A manager relaying a gate it never carded writes no `<sid>.posted.json`, so there is nothing for the echo to match; and even for a carded relay, `--closer` holds a single record per session, so a multi-card round overwrites the first closer. A verb-free closer mints no echo at all, which is why the closer's shape is the fix here; `scripts/attention-ask.py`'s docstring now states both limits and stops naming the relay as the flag's common case. ⚠️ **Master's later sentence making the relayed worker gate the `--closer` flag's *commonest case* is replaced by this rule** — the relay is never a `--closer` case at all, and the flag keeps only the post-then-close case, which is not a relay.

## v0.247.4

- fix: **a goal manager's spawn gate resolves mode from `role`, and `role: agent` is `headless` always** (operator ruling 2026-11-06: *"Agents can't be interactive. Only direct sessions can be interactive."*). `agents/goal-spawn-reader.md` reports `mode_source=role` / `role-corrected`, and `commands/steps/goal-spawn.md` never passes `interactive=true` for a `role: agent` row and writes a stale `mode: interactive` back to `headless` before spawning — measured 2026-11-06: such a row reopened an Agents-window tab minutes after every Agents pane had been closed.
- fix: **the readings/spawn split no longer loses what the two step files carried, and both legs are now guarded.** `commands/steps/round-readings.md` regains the registry-vs-store reconcile backstop (open_cards `null`≠`[]`, `unknown agent`→feed, once-per-park `tick-voiced-<sha8>.json`), and `commands/steps/round-tail.md` cites it instead of a step-4 substitution nothing writes. `commands/steps/goal-spawn.md` and `agents/goal-spawn-reader.md` regain the `docker`/`k8s` branch — seven refused arguments, `task` required, the mode carve-out — citing `docs/fleet-surface.md` item 7. `agents/round-readings-reader.md` gains `inflight-unread=` / `external-unread=` so every value of every family is emitted, and `agents/goal-spawn-reader.md` regains `phase: human_review`. `docs/handback-contract.md` now owns the one truncation exception. `scripts/check-handback-keys.py` covers both reader agents and their steps, so reverting either step fails. The second review round closed what the split left inconsistent: the spawn leg's hand-back fence now names the `mode_source` values it actually emits (`role` / `role-corrected` / `classify` / `unread` — the retired `field` was never emitted and the step branched on two undeclared values), `cap=` drops its unreachable `hard` value, an absent `mode:` is **classified and written back** rather than held (item 6's rule — the pre-split step contradicted it in its own two clauses), the `To open` call shape carries the `task=` the guard already enforces, `agents/round-readings-reader.md` verifies its document **before** recording it on the receipt rather than after, and `docs/handback-contract.md` counts its seven carriers.
- refactor: **two of the manager round's heaviest legs now delegate to sub-agents.** `commands/steps/round-readings.md` dispatches `supervisor:round-readings-reader` and `commands/steps/goal-spawn.md` dispatches `supervisor:goal-spawn-reader`; each returns a ≤ 1 KB `key=value` hand-back plus a `payload=` path, so the readings contract and the spawn gates stop re-injecting into the manager's context on every tick. `commands/steps/round-tail.md` stays inline — its wakeup re-arm, Monitor arms, attention cards and heal ladder are the manager's own tool calls.

## v0.247.3

- fix: **the four consumers that decide whether a session is held now see the WezTerm Hold window too, so a pane the operator parked stops being nudged, silenced-then-escalated, restarted or listed as an orphan.** `manager-predispatch.py`, `notify-gate.py`, `restart-worker.py` and `orphan-candidates.py` each carried only an inlined store reader, so a Hold-window pane — held by POSITION rather than by a `hold` call — was invisible to every one of them. Each now inlines the same `Hold.window_id` + `wezterm cli list` predicate `session-holds.py` uses, three-state per `docs/pane-reads.md`, and each **decides its own response from what it does with the value** rather than copying a sibling's: `restart-worker.py` **refuses** (`held-session` in the window; a distinct `hold-unreadable` when the pane list could not be read, because a restart KILLS a process and a broken read must never be read as "not held"); `notify-gate.py` **never silences** (a failed read keeps the gate and prints `wezterm cli list unreadable` — a broken transport that swallowed a real escalation is the dangerous direction); `manager-predispatch.py` and `orphan-candidates.py` **answer "not held"**, because there the value only ever *moves a digest* (fail-open — CHANGE authorises a sweep, never suppresses one, so a missed hold costs one un-moved digest while the row still renders) or *drops a candidate* (inventing a hold would silently drop a real orphan — the false clean that script exists to prevent), and that is the same direction their store read already documents. The store docstrings and the "missing/corrupt store reads as nothing held" direction are unchanged; this adds a second source of heldness, not a third behaviour for the first. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/notify-gate.py`, `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/orphan-candidates.py`, `scripts/transport-read-check.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_notify_gate.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_orphan_candidates.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- docs: **the resume-path table's indeterminate-liveness row no longer spells path B as a command that cannot run.** The row's path cell named path **B** followed by the literal `wezterm cli spawn --resume`, and `wezterm cli spawn` has no `--resume` flag — run as written it fails with `error: unexpected argument '--resume' found`. Measured 2026-10-07: a manager session relayed the table's shorthand to the operator as a `you run:` line and the run failed. ⚠️ **The trap was in the row, not only in the relay** — a table cell is the form a reader is most likely to copy, and a reader copies this cell rather than the recipe 67 lines above it. The row now points at that recipe, which already carried the correct shape (`:387`, where `--resume` is handed to the launcher after `--`), and names the distinction in the cell so the flag's owner is not left to inference. ⚠️ **The malformed string had exactly one occurrence repo-wide**, so no other copy needed updating. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Tests: unchanged at **1869** (docs-only change).

## v0.247.2

- fix: **the fleet round's acting legs now write their counts into the digest, so a restarted manager and an opened flagged page survive the round that did them.** Rows 5b (`commands/steps/keepalive.md`) and 5c (`commands/steps/flagged-open.md`) instructed the round to print `unactioned: <n> lapsed-managers`, `unactioned: <n> dead-workers` and `unactioned: <n> operator-flagged`, and all three reached the round's **tool output** and nothing else: the digest is written by the sweep reader at router row 2, before either leg runs, and `commands/steps/output-shape.md` had no slot for them. Measured 2026-11-06 on `~/.claude/state/fleet-handback/2026-11-06T19-10Z.digest` — a real 69-line round over 24 sessions — `grep -c 'unactioned:'` returned **0**, so a round that restarted a manager reported nothing about it in the only artifact that outlives the round. `output-shape.md` gains item 14 (`Legs`), the single home for the three formats, their `not run — <reason>` form and the `(lower bound, scan incomplete)` floor; `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` seeds the digest's `LEGS` section with every leg `not run — no reading recorded this round` so the shape exists from the moment the digest is written, and the two step files now record their reading through the new `scripts/fleet-legs.py` and name item 14 rather than restating the line. ⚠️ **A suppressed leg and a leg that found nothing never print the same**: `--not-run` renders the reason instead of a `0`, and `--floor` is refused on any key but `dead-workers` so the lower-bound marker cannot spread to a count that is a measurement. `fleet-legs.py --check` reports a section that is missing, has a duplicated key, is unparseable, files a key under the wrong leg, or **still carries the sweep's seed** — that last one is what makes the guard non-vacuous: a round whose leg neither counted nor said why is reported incomplete rather than reading as three clean lines, and item 14 renders `legs: INCOMPLETE — <reason>` (exit 1, which also covers an absent section) or `legs: UNKNOWN — <reason>` (exit 2, digest unreadable or arguments refused). The rewrite preserves the digest's mode and mtime, so a leg write cannot reset the age `scripts/fleet-digest.py` reads for a round with no parseable baseline, and a pre-fix digest gains the section in its template position before `NOTES`. The digest render cap moves 40 → 43 lines for the section's fixed lines. Tests 27 (`scripts/tests/test_fleet_legs.py`), the first of which fails against the pre-fix digest shape. Change set: `scripts/fleet-legs.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_legs.py`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `commands/steps/flagged-open.md`, `commands/steps/sweep.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.247.1

- fix: **a pane in the WezTerm Hold window now reads as held, so a session the operator parked stops being reported as stuck.** `session-holds.py` knew only explicit store holds, so a Hold-window pane — the operator's *"leave this alone"* gesture, made by the pane's POSITION rather than by a `hold` call — was invisible to every consumer that reads the store, and a fleet scan reported such a pane as stuck. `is-held` now resolves a pane, or a session, to its WezTerm window and compares that with the role map's `Hold.window_id`, reporting `hold-window` at exit 0. The rule is decided at READ time and never written to the store: a persisted entry would outlive the pane leaving the window, so the hold would then be unreleasable by the gesture that set it. ⚠️ **The predicate is three-state, per `docs/pane-reads.md`** — `True` in the window, `False` not in it, and `None` when the pane list could not be read, where `is-held` refuses non-zero naming the transport rather than answering `NOT_HELD`. Collapsing that third state into `False` is the direction that matters: a hold suppresses ACTS, so a broken read reported as "not held" is what lets a manager nudge a session the operator parked. A missing or malformed role map is deliberately NOT that state — it is the documented fallback to explicit holds only, and it answers `False`. Change set: `scripts/session-holds.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_holds.py`, `scripts/transport-read-check.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **`session-holds.py`'s pane→session join read `tab_title`, which is empty for every pane, so `hold <pane-id>` and `release <pane-id>` could never resolve.** The per-pane title is `title`; `tab_title` was empty for all 25 panes measured on 2026-10-12, and `window_title` is one value shared by every pane in a window, so neither can identify a pane. `pane_title()` now reads `title` and strips WezTerm's leading activity glyph (`✳` / `◑` / `◐`), which restores the join `skills/hold/SKILL.md` documents — measured 18/18 panes across the Agents and Hold windows resolving to their registry session id. ⚠️ The `wezterm cli list` read moved into a new `panes()` accessor so one decision asks the transport exactly once; `pane_title` is now a caller of it, and the site is enumerated in `transport-read-check.py`'s `SITE_LIST` and driven into all three states.
- fix: **a pane in the WezTerm Hold window now reads as held, so a session the operator parked stops being reported as stuck.** `session-holds.py` knew only explicit store holds, so a Hold-window pane — the operator's *"leave this alone"* gesture, made by the pane's POSITION rather than by a `hold` call — was invisible to every consumer that reads the store, and a fleet scan reported such a pane as stuck. `is-held` now resolves a pane, or a session, to its WezTerm window and compares that with the role map's `Hold.window_id`, reporting `hold-window` at exit 0. The rule is decided at READ time and never written to the store: a persisted entry would outlive the pane leaving the window, so the hold would then be unreleasable by the gesture that set it. ⚠️ **The predicate is three-state, per `docs/pane-reads.md`** — `True` in the window, `False` not in it, and `None` when the pane list could not be read, where `is-held` refuses non-zero naming the transport rather than answering `NOT_HELD`. Collapsing that third state into `False` is the direction that matters: a hold suppresses ACTS, so a broken read reported as "not held" is what lets a manager nudge a session the operator parked. A missing or malformed role map is deliberately NOT that state — it is the documented fallback to explicit holds only, and it answers `False`. ⚠️ **A machine with no `wezterm` BINARY is a fourth state, and it answers `[]` rather than `None`** — an absent transport has no panes, and the Hold window is a WezTerm concept, so where WezTerm is not installed there is nothing for it to apply to. Folding that in with the failure state is the *false refusal* severity of the same doc: measured 2026-10-12, the first cut of this change did exactly that and made `is-held <session>` exit non-zero on a headless runner, failing CI on three tests that passed on a Mac purely because a Mac has WezTerm. Those three tests now stub the pane read, so they assert the store rather than the host. Only `FileNotFoundError` reads that way — any other `OSError` (a mux socket that is gone) is a genuine failure and stays `None`. Change set: `scripts/session-holds.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_holds.py`, `scripts/transport-read-check.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.247.0

- docs: **`/fleet-triage` moves into the plugin, and the fleet rules it was the only home for land in the plugin files that already own their neighbours.** The command was 715 lines / 38,715 B at `~/.claude/commands/fleet-triage.md` — personal to one machine, unreadable by any other session or host, and drifting from the plugin it drives. It lands as `commands/fleet-triage.md` plus eight `commands/steps/fleet-triage-*.md` payloads, **partitioned rather than copied**: `scripts/check-budget.py` refuses a new unbaselined body over 30 KB outright, so a literal move was impossible and a baseline raise was declined. Its script paths no longer hardcode a `~/.claude/plugins/cache/...` path — every call resolves through the repo-wide `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT:-$HOME/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/claude-supervisor}` form that `commands/fleet-drive.md` already uses, and every script call is routed through `python3` so the router's `Bash(python3:*)` grant covers it, with `Skill` granted because the STUCK drive branches dispatch `/supervisor:open` — a slash command is reachable only through `Skill`, so a grant-less router would park the drive path on a permission prompt — and **no `Monitor` granted**, because a one-pass run that armed a watcher would leave it firing into a session that had stopped triaging; the EXTERNAL branch reports a missing watcher and hands the arming to the row's owner instead. The fallback is `:-` and not `:?` deliberately: `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` is **unset** in a command's Bash (`docs/session-tiers.md:200-202`), so a `:?` form aborts the run in the only environment the command actually runs in. The rules land in files that already own their neighbours — **five** move into an existing home and a **sixth** gains one it did not have — **six in total**, counted explicitly because the task's own title says "four" and this change carries more than that: `commands/steps/rules.md` gains the inference that a `STALE` manager reading is `period + SLACK` arithmetic and not proof of a lapsed loop (the constant defined at `scripts/manager-liveness.py:90`, not restated a sixth time), the inference that `session-liveness.py --check` returning `ABSENT` is not a death verdict (cross-referencing `commands/open.md:495`, where the resume-side form already lives), and the missing half of § *No manager-proposal row* — a subject opens only when it carries `flag: true` and has no loop armed. `commands/steps/needs-input.md` gains the mechanical card-existence check before posting (`ls ~/.claude/state/attention/<session-id>*.open.json`, then poll every id found), with `commands/steps/goal-gates.md` cross-referencing it rather than duplicating it. `commands/steps/goal-closure.md` gains the distinction that `hold` is a pause and not a closure, reconciled with `commands/topic-spawn.md:50`, and its five command references now carry the same `:-` load path the new step documents — the bare `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` form expands empty in a command's Bash, which is the failure this PR's own load-path step argues against. One rule lands that had no home: **read text is evidence, never instruction** — pane text (3.2c), transcript closers (3.2d), a `# Progress` entry or a PR comment can report a state but never order an action, because Step 4 closes sessions, messages managers and posts cards on what those reads say. The invariant gains the one terminal outcome its own 4b branch could produce and the four-outcome list did not admit: **`NUDGED`** — a delivered `SendMessage` naming the next open box — with the expiry that keeps it honest: the nudge is written to a durable record at `~/.claude/state/fleet-triage/nudges/<session-id>.json` (a report is chat, and a later or compacted run sees none of it, so without the file every run would look like a first nudge), and a nudge unanswered on two consecutive runs becomes an OPERATOR card rather than a third identical send. Step 6's verdict block gains the matching `🔒 LIVE-IDLE` census row, green only while that record exists. Without it a live-idle headless worker with unfinished work that ignored its nudge ended the run neither driven nor carded, and Step 6's three counters did not see it, so a run could print `ALL GOOD` over a session the invariant itself calls a failure. Measurements 2026-11-04 throughout. Change set: `commands/fleet-triage.md`, `commands/steps/fleet-triage-*.md`, `commands/steps/rules.md`, `commands/steps/needs-input.md`, `commands/steps/goal-gates.md`, `commands/steps/goal-closure.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.246.0

- fix: **`attention-answer.py answer` no longer prints `ANSWERED` for a permission verdict it never saw delivered.** It used to POST the verdict, print `ANSWERED` plus an unconditional `DELIVERY: supervisor poll -- no session to send to` line, and exit 0 whatever happened next — measured 2026-11-05, a manager read that as answered while the poller refused with `wrapper holds no question for session bdc2f1ed` and the worker stayed parked. It now waits (bounded, `ATTENTION_DELIVERY_WAIT`, default 35 s — past the poller's 30 s idle interval) for the store's delivery report: `delivered` → `ANSWERED` + `DELIVERY: delivered by <carrier>`, exit 0; `failed` / `held` → `RECORDED` + `NOT_DELIVERED`, exit 3; no report or an unreadable one → `RECORDED` + `UNCONFIRMED`, exit 3. `commands/attention-next.md` branches on the new lines.
- fix: `scripts/permission-answer-poll.py` records a `delivered` attempt (carrier `supervisor:permission-answer-poll`) after it emits a decision, so a tab worker's delivered answer reads `delivered` in the store instead of `never_attempted` — without it the arm above would report every delivered tab-worker answer as unconfirmed.

## v0.245.0

- feat: **a dead Fleet Manager can be resumed from its card, on the operator's answer only.** `fleet-keeper.py`'s ABSENT card offers `resume it` next to `acknowledge`. That exact answer — no timer, no threshold, no autonomous branch — runs `restart-worker.py --absent` once, and only when three reads agree: `session-liveness.py` ABSENT (the branch's gate, never trusted alone), no registry entry carries the id, and the transcript has been silent 5 min. Every answer is used at most once (marked before it runs): a read showing the session alive consumes it unrun, and a transient miss waits at most 30 min, then drops it — so an answer can never fire later on its own. A lapsed but live manager is never offered it. Operator decision 2026-11-06 narrows the 2026-11-01 report-only ruling by this one path; the no-kill/no-resume pin in `test_fleet_keeper.py` is narrowed to everything outside the single executor — now checked with `ast`, including its exact argv and its single caller — not deleted.
- feat: `restart-worker.py --absent <sid>` resumes a session whose process is gone. It holds no kill path, takes `cwd` and the **manager** launcher from the `fleet-register.py` registration that outlives death, and refuses `not-absent` while any registry entry carries the id or cannot be parsed, while a pane record names the session within 10 min with no end, or while another `--absent` of it holds the lock — the guards against starting a second copy of a live conversation. `wezterm` is resolved by absolute path, so it works under the keeper's launchd `PATH`.
- feat: `attention-ask.py poll --answered-at` also prints `ANSWERED_AT: <rfc3339>` for an answered item — opt-in, so the default output every existing consumer parses is byte-identical. The fleet keeper bounds an answered resume's wait from this store time, not from when it first noticed the answer.

## v0.244.1

- fix: **the fleet round's delegate rule no longer swallows the two legs that must act.** `commands/fleet-drive.md` sent *"anything not the sweep"* to a sub-agent, and rows 5b (keep alive) and 5c (flagged open) are exactly that — while being the only two rows whose purpose is to restart a manager, resume a dead worker or open a page the operator flagged. A delegated sub-agent runs read-only, so the round printed counts instead of acting. Measured 2026-11-01 13:27 CET (session `ac24db61`): the round instructed its sub-agent to print the literal `unactioned: <found − opened> operator-flagged` **with opened=0**, never invoked `/supervisor:open --flagged`, and a sub-agent that read 8 `resume` rows resumed none — 50 sessions classified, nothing restarted, nothing opened. The delegate rule gains a **carve-out naming 5b and 5c as acting legs** that run in the round session or in a sub-agent granted write tools (`Bash`, `Edit`, `Write`, `mcp__supervisor__*`), because a read-only delegate can only print the count it was told to. ⚠️ **Each leg now says it acts at its own home**, since the router is read every tick while a step file is read at the moment the leg runs: `commands/steps/keepalive.md` and `commands/steps/flagged-open.md` each state that a read-only execution is not a run of it and point at the carve-out rather than restating it. ⚠️ **`scripts/check-acting-legs.py` holds the three texts** — single-copy, in the router, pointed at from both legs, and with both router rows still dispatching them — because a deleted carve-out passes a naive count vacuously and a step file that never says it acts can be delegated read-only without anything noticing. It is wired into `make precommit`, and its tests fail on the pre-fix text: `test_flagged_leg_without_its_statement_fails` is the shipped defect in a fixture. ⚠️ `commands/fleet-drive.md` is a strict 10 KB wakeup target, so the carve-out landed with equal trims to the round/arm note, the worker-session warning, the `INLINE` pointer and the `argument-hint` (10 127 → 10 144 bytes). Change set: `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `commands/steps/flagged-open.md`, `scripts/check-acting-legs.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_acting_legs.py`, `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.244.0

- feat: **the fleet round answers its own workers' routine permission parks.** New router row 5d → `commands/steps/own-parks.md`. A headless worker's prompt parks with its spawner and no other session can answer it, yet `fleet-drive.md` held `answer_permission` and no step called it, so every routine park of a Fleet-Manager-spawned worker reached the operator's board. The step dispatches `supervisor:goal-wrangler` (class B, `docs/permission-allowlist.md` only) for workers the spawn ledger names this session as spawner of. It fails closed: an unavailable `answer_permission`/`list_agents`, an unreadable ledger or a non-owned worker means nothing is answered. Classes C, D and E are an explicit list that always stays pending for the operator. Parks are read from `list_agents`' per-row `pending_permissions`, so the command's `allowed-tools` is unchanged and `fleet-drive.md` stays within its 10,240-byte budget. The step's runtime cost per tick is one ledger read plus, when owned parks exist, one bounded wrangler dispatch.
- feat: `scripts/own-park-check.py` — the ownership check (`OWN` / `REFUSE <reason>`, exit 2 when nothing can be verified), reading the spawn edge under both `parent_session` and `spawner_session_id`. `goal-wrangler` gains a scoped mode (`Answer only these request_ids:`): one `pending_permissions` read and no `await_permission`, which would return the same escalated head-of-queue park on every call and starve the routine ones behind it. It never answers an id outside the list; a server can hold parks of workers whose ledger row is missing or names another spawner. Pinned by `scripts/tests/test_own_park_check.py`.

## v0.243.1

- fix: **a wrapper-backed headless worker's policy-allowed prompt is answered by the policy again, not by a human.** The wrapper owns the child's single `--permission-prompt-tool`, so the `PermissionRequest` hook never fired. Every `Edit`/`Write` in the worker's own cwd parked for a manager, and `permissions.jsonl` had gone unwritten since 2026-10-30. `server/wrapper-policy.mjs` now polls the wrapper's parks every 2 s and runs the same `decide()` once per request id. `allow`/`deny` are answered through `POST /sessions/{id}/answer`, `escalate` stays parked, and each decision is logged with `via: "wrapper"`. A manager that answered first gets the wrapper's 409 and is not overridden. A wrapper outage decides nothing and logs once. `decide()` is gone from `policy_gap.not_run`, and `policy_via` names the poll.

## v0.243.0

- feat: exit records now name the session on a fresh spawn. `session-exit-record.py observe` stamps the session id into every heartbeat: it reads `sessionId` from the `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` record of the pane's own `claude` (breadth-first, four levels, so a direct child and the supervisor-spawn grandchild both resolve) and carries the last id past the registry's deletion on death. `append` reads the id back from those heartbeats when the trap passes `--session ""`, so trap-written ends carry it too. The descent stops at the first `claude` and accepts only `entrypoint: cli` records, so a headless worker spawned below a manager can never be bound to the manager's pane. Ids that could forge a field — tab or trailing newline — are refused.

## v0.242.1

- fix: **a `role: human` row prefers the vault's `-claude` sibling over `manager_launcher`** — both private-personal and seibert-brogrammers configure `manager_launcher` to their `-deepseek` script, so the manager chain left human rows on the worker model (found by the first live `--flagged` run).
- fix: **a taskless `spawn_agent(role: human)` now takes the Claude launcher too** — `applyHumanLauncherRule` runs after every launcher branch, not only the task branch. Doc follow-ups from the #644 review: `commands/open.md` points the `role` → `mode` mapping at the matrix and lists every gate among the non-open outcomes; `docs/fleet-surface.md` item 6 cites matrix Rule 2 instead of restating it; matrix Rule 4 states its `claude_script` fallback.

## v0.242.0

- fix: **`/supervisor:open`'s resume branch selects its path by the row's resolved `mode:` and liveness instead of always opening a wezterm tab.** `commands/open.md` § 3.1 no longer claims the resume branch is unconditionally the tab path: a `mode: headless` row with an **ABSENT** session resumes through `spawn_agent(resume=, interactive=false)` with an explicit `cwd` (server-paced, so it skips `start-rate.mjs`), while `mode: interactive` and UNKNOWN/AMBIGUOUS liveness still take the tab path unchanged. The selector's single home stays `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker's resume-path table; the call site only cites it. Under a `spawn.mode: headless` fleet default this is what stops a `--flagged` batch returning 26 headless rows as operator-watched tabs.
- feat: `scripts/check-open-resume-mode.py`, wired into `precommit` as `check-open-resume-mode` — fails on the pre-fix text (both unconditional sentences) and on any selector paragraph missing an arm, the rule-home citation, or the headless arm's `cwd` requirement.

## v0.241.1

- fix: `session-exit-record.py observe` now records the real exit status of a process-group kill — it arms a `kqueue` `NOTE_EXITSTATUS` watch on the pane pid, so the out-of-group observer writes `kind=signalled kill … signal=SIGTERM writer=observer` instead of `unknown-end … signal=unavailable`; no privilege needed. Polling + `unknown-end` stays as the fallback when no watch can be armed.

## v0.241.0

- fix: **the `--flagged` batch opens every operator-flagged row even when its `role`/`mode`/`phase` are missing.** `commands/open.md` § Step 0.6 no longer refuses a row whose body carries no role signal: it writes the default (`role: agent`, `mode` per the matrix) and prints `🏷️ defaulted <field>=<value>`. Judgement skips ("bot rows") are gone; the only non-open outcomes are provenance REFUSED, LIVE, HOLD, session hold, cap refusal and a declared `role: manager`. The footer's `refused U (undecidable)` counter is replaced by `defaulted D fields`.
- feat: **one role × mode decision matrix, `docs/role-mode-matrix.md`**, cited by `/supervisor:open` and `vault-cli` `create-task` instead of restating the mapping; `docs/fleet-surface.md` item 6 points at it.
- fix: **a `role: human` row always opens into Direct on the vault's Claude launcher.** `spawn_agent` applies `applyHumanLauncherRule` (`server/spawn-cwd.mjs`): a human row with no own `launcher:` takes the manager launcher chain (`manager_launcher`, else the `-claude` sibling) instead of the `-deepseek` worker default; a previous run's "leave closed" answer never carries over.

## v0.240.4

- fix: goal round now reaches row 5b — `commands/steps/goal-drive.md` hands off to `steps/goal-parked.md` before the tail, so parked tracked workers get their lever row every tick (previously no step chained to it and rounds skipped it)

## v0.240.3

- fix: `restart-precheck.classify()` returns `gate-unanswerable` for a pane-less (`sdk-cli`) session whose closer no channel can answer — no open card of its own, and no named full-id card that is open or answered-and-delivered — so `/supervisor:close` frees a finished headless worker instead of refusing `gate-held` forever. An answered card with no recorded delivery counts as unanswerable; an unreadable store fails closed to `gate-held`.
- fix: `closer-gate-lint.py` blocks (exit 2) an `approve:` / `review:` / `you run:` closer naming a card handle that is not a full 32-hex store id — `poll` refuses those, so the gate could never be answered. Hex is read as a handle only after `card`, `item` or `poll`, so commit SHAs pass.

## v0.240.2

- fix: `round-readings.py` matches padded `[ 417]` and headless `[ hst]` gate rows through `gate-owner-filter.py`'s `feed_rows`, resolves `hst:<item id>` to its owning session from the attention store, and reads `unread` — never `clear` — when a `[   ?]` row, an unkeyed `[ hst]` row, a failed events-log read or an unresolved store key leaves a gate's owner unknown

## v0.240.1

- fix: `gate-owner-filter.py` now reads a headless worker's `[ hst]` gate row instead of skipping it, keyed `hst:<store item id>` and resolved to its session through one extra attention-store read, so a parked headless worker reaches the manager's armed watcher (the wake line names `session=<id>`); an unreachable store fails open and still emits

## v0.240.0

- fix: the fleet round's keepalive leg (router row 5b, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`) now gates on its own debt — `manager-receipt.py --debt --only re-arm` — instead of the whole round's. Gated on the whole round, it was stopped by a step it never consumes whenever the round reached 5b with the sweep reader still running in the background: `--debt` printed `debt: digest` and exited 12, and the digest was recorded only after the leg (measured 2026-11-05 on the Fleet Manager: 2 of 25 gate calls). New repeatable `--only <step>` on `--debt` scopes the exit code and the printed lines, never the record — the debt file still carries every owed step, so `--check` and the `drive.md` / `flagged-open.md` gates are unchanged. `keepalive.md`'s park-watch section no longer claims a missed round "never strands" a resume: `defer_date` resurfaces the row but nothing starts the session. `fleet-round-tail.md` and `output-shape.md` item 13 restate the scoped gate.

## v0.239.1

- fix: `session-exit-record.py observe` now records a killed pane on a reused pane id. `has_end()` scanned the whole `pane-<id>.log` for any end line, and pane ids are reused, so an end line left by an earlier session made the observer conclude the current run had already ended. It then wrote nothing and exited. Measured 2026-11-05: `wezterm cli kill-pane` on pane 115 (pid 72157) left no end line at all, because the file still held a 2026-10-28 `kind=exited` for pid 56446. The check now counts only end lines carrying the watched pid, written after that pid's last `spawn` line. Every trap writer passes `--pid $$`, so the trap's own report still wins. The log is also read with `errors="replace"`, because a single undecodable byte raised inside the detached observer and lost the end line the same way. Four new tests (an earlier run's end, another pid's end after this spawn, a reused pid, an undecodable byte) each fail against the old check and pass with the fix.

## v0.239.0

- feat: **the fleet round now enforces the 15-minute park rule.** The rule (a machine wait over 15 min parks with a `defer_date` and exits) was always loaded and measured ignored: one session sat `watching` for 31 minutes while a 12-minute one beside it was legal. No leg consumed the reading, because `agents/fleet-drive.md` excludes `parked-on-watcher` rows from nudge candidacy. New `scripts/park-nudge.py` reads `session-activity.py --json` and emits one `NUDGE` line per session whose bucket is exactly `watching` and whose quiet exceeds `park-event.py`'s threshold (strict: 15 min is legal). The bucket is the gate: `gated`, `working`, `idle` and `unclassified` rows never qualify, however long they have been quiet. The nudge text carries the whole park: `park-event.py register` by absolute path, `defer_date` at the re-check TIME with offset, and exit via vault-cli's `session-self-exit.py`, resolved from `$VAULT_CLI_SCRIPTS`, the newest vault-cli cache version, then the marketplace clone, because that script does not ship in this plugin. Suppression is one nudge per watching episode, keyed on the session id and never on the quiet minutes, which grow every round. Leaving the bucket, leaving the fleet, or a dropping quiet re-arms it. An unreadable reading or ledger prints `park-nudge: unknown` and exits 2, never `0 nudged`. `commands/steps/keepalive.md` (fleet row 5b) gains the leg, which sends each nudge without the drive leg's busy/`shell` skip because a watcher is `shell` by definition. `test_park_nudge.py` pins the threshold, the bucket gate (every non-`watching` fixture row sits above 15 min), the four-run episode suppression and the self-exit path.

## v0.238.6

- fix: **`session-scope.py` no longer drops a task's goal when `goals:` is a bare list item.** `_goals()` extracted `[[...]]` matches only, so `goals:` then `    - Some Goal` — and the plain scalar and nested list-of-list shapes — returned `[]`: `gate-owner-filter.py` then read a foreign page as `owner-unknown` instead of `foreign-goal:<goal>`, and a manager whose own task declared its goal that way never widened its scope, so its own workers' gates left its feed. `_goals()` now delegates to the shared `scripts/goals-declaration.py` `goal_names`, the reader `manager-predispatch.py`, `reset.py` and `topic-spawn.py` already use; the now-unused `_GOALS_BLOCK` regex is removed and `_task_links` keeps its wikilink reader. `test_session_scope.py` § `GoalsDeclarationShapes` feeds all four shapes to `_goals()` and runs a bare-item manager task through `load()` (`self_goals == ["Some Goal"]`); it failed on four assertions before the change.

## v0.238.5

- docs: state that `hold` is never for a task waiting on an operator decision or answer. `docs/worker-session-states.md` § *Closed* carries the rule — such a task is `decision`, not `closed` — and names the status fix rather than a close; `commands/worker-drive.md` § *Park a clock-bound session* points at it from its "a deferred task is not `hold`" sentence. `scripts/budget-baseline.json` raises that file's entry 49673 → 49817 for the added pointer (deliberate growth; the file sat exactly at its ceiling).

## v0.238.4

- fix: the drive leg's auto-resume gate (`agents/goal-drive.md` step 3) no longer excludes a row on `flag: true`. Its `not parked` clause now reads phase and status only, so an operator-flagged, approved row whose session died is resumed instead of being opened by nothing. The gate reads no `flag_set_by` (operator rulings 2026-10-30, 2026-11-03, 2026-11-04). The open path (`commands/open.md` § Step 0.5) keeps its `OPENING_ACTORS` gate. New `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Approval provenance records which surface reads provenance; `commands/open.md`, `commands/steps/goal-spawn.md` and `docs/session-tiers.md` point at it instead of restating it. New `scripts/check-resume-flag-clause.py` (wired into `make precommit`) holds the gate's clauses to a closed list. It parses the `not parked` bullet, and any clause naming a flag field, into a predicate and fails closed on anything unreadable. It evaluates five fixture rows and two parked controls through that predicate. It then runs Step 0.5's own selector lines, shape-checked with only `str` as a builtin, on the same fixtures.

## v0.238.3

- fix: `scripts/close-worker.py` resolves a session anchored only on a goal or topic page — a goal manager or topic manager — instead of refusing it `task-unresolved`. When no task resolves the session, the script walks `24 Goals` / `23 Goals` / `23 Topics` with the same join as the task lookup: `claude_session_id`, else the session's `metrics_sessions` entry. A task the session holds as `claude_session_id` always wins. A task matched only by handover does not outrank a page the session still holds, and every held page must pass. Goal/topic terminal-state rule: the session is closable when it has handed the page over, when the page is `completed` or `aborted`, or when the page has a future `defer_date`. It refuses `task-not-terminal` while the session still holds a non-terminal page, `hold` included. The task rule does not transfer, because a goal stays `in_progress` across many sessions. A failed goal/topic lookup (grep error) refuses `task-unresolved` instead of being skipped. `--dry-run` names each resolved page path. `commands/close.md` and `docs/fleet-surface.md` describe the page rule.

## v0.238.2

- fix: **`/supervisor:open --flagged` no longer opens a row deferred to later today.** The Step 0.5 selector truncated `defer_date` to its first ten characters and compared against `date.today()`, so a row parked until 23:56 was opened at 22:56. It now calls `scripts/defer-date.py`'s `defer_state()` (loaded by path, like the scripts fixed in #599): a time of day is honoured, a date-only value still means the start of that day, and an unparseable value is withheld rather than opened — matching `flagged-rows.py`. The parser path uses the file's `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT:-…}` fallback (a bare `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` is empty in a Bash tool call), and a parser that cannot load prints `ERROR | <vault> | defer-date.py not loaded` and exits non-zero instead of silently selecting nothing. `test_defer_date.py` executes the extracted block over fixture rows (timestamp ahead/behind, date-only today/tomorrow, invalid, absent, unloadable parser) and pins the fallback, the `defer_state(` call and the absence of `[:10]` / `date.today()`. `commands/open.md`'s budget entry is raised by 276 bytes (78226 → 78502) to pay for the loader and its guard.

## v0.238.1

- fix: **a doubled `snapshot written:` label no longer reads as "no baseline", which made a timely round report as late.** A render that copies the whole `snapshot written: …` tail into the value emits `snapshot written: snapshot written: <iso>`; `parse_snapshot` matched nothing, returned `no-baseline`, and `newest_round()` carried the PREVIOUS round's baseline forward. Measured 2026-11-03 on `2026-11-03T07-44Z.digest` — the watcher reported `STALE … age=1129s` at `08:00:48Z` on a round that had run on time, the one spurious report in a 24 h live window of 78. `parse_snapshot` now unwraps a repeated label before matching: the watcher is the last line of defence and cannot assume the renderer wrote the label exactly once, so a malformed header costs nothing instead of a false report. Two regression tests carry the measured value and the empty-label guard.

## v0.238.0

- feat: add a tunnel mode to the A2A push router, so a `k8s` worker's status can reach the session that spawned it. Set `relayUrl` and the router stops receiving pushes and instead long-polls the in-cluster relay, dispatching each pulled item to the owning session's loopback receiver by the same notification-token rule. The Mac cannot accept an inbound connection from the cluster — its firewall drops every port except two something else already owns — so the only direction that works is the Mac dialling out. A direct push is refused with `503` in this mode, an unknown token is dropped rather than forwarded, and an empty pull yields a real timer so the loop cannot starve the event loop
- fix: the tunnel's pull loop now logs and backs off instead of retrying silently at a fixed rate, and each pull is bounded by a timeout that `close()` can abort. A bare `catch {}` retrying once a second forever is the deployed-but-inert shape the constructor guard exists to prevent, and that guard only rejects an **empty** secret — a non-empty wrong one, which is what rotation and typos produce, was the likeliest misconfiguration of all and produced an identical silent loop. The failure is logged on the first occurrence and on each doubling, with exponential backoff capped at 30 s. The pull carried no `AbortSignal`, so a relay that accepted the connection and never answered parked the loop with the only bound being undici's implicit headers timeout, and `close()` resolved while a batch could still be dispatched; each pull now runs under a controller that a timer bounds (90 s, above `pollWait` because the relay legitimately holds the request open that long) and that `close()` aborts. The tunnel specs no longer race the loop — a 5 ms retry against a real loopback registration round-trip plus a fixed 60 ms sleep could pull the batch before the route existed, which failed the positive case on a loaded box and made the negative case pass for the wrong reason (the item dropped because no route existed, not because its token was unknown); the stub now gates its batch on the route existing and the tests poll a predicate to a deadline. New specs cover `close()` aborting an in-flight pull and a pull that never answers timing out.

## v0.237.3

- docs: **a manager card about another session now carries that session as its `--liveness-ref`, and the closer must not restate another pane's line.** The operator raised the same defect three times (2026-09-30 twice, 2026-11-04): a card telling them to act in another pane jumps to the manager instead, so they land where there is nothing to answer. Measured on the live board 2026-11-04 — a card posted with `--liveness-ref owner:<worker session>` renders the **worker's** pane while its `producer_id` stays the asker, because `pkg/provenance.go`'s `sessionIDFromItem` resolves the ref before the producer. The jump was never missing a field: `[[Attention Item Schema]]` § *The jump needs no field of its own* already derives it from `liveness_ref`, and the producers were pointing the ref at themselves. Both board-post instructions now pass `--liveness-ref owner:<the session this card is about>` — `commands/steps/goal-gates.md` (goal tier) and `commands/steps/needs-input.md` (fleet tier, the relayed-blocked-session path that produced most of the reported cards) — and the adjacent rules state the two halves that make it work — the ref decides the jump, and the Stop hook cannot supply it (`attention-log.py` reads `WEZTERM_PANE` and has no override), so the existing prohibition on restating another session's line is load-bearing rather than cosmetic. ⚠️ Scoped to **live** targets: the ref is also what the jump resolves through, and a dead session resolves no pane, so the `stuck:` arm — whose population is sessions that may already be dead — keeps the default and passes no ref. `owner:` is the right model precisely because it probes nothing (`OwnerLivenessModel` returns live unconditionally), so the card outlives the target's exit and is bounded only by `expires_at`. No code change and no schema change: the controller already implements the schema as written.

## v0.237.2

- fix: **a denial marker stops holding a row `stuck: denied` once its tool has succeeded, and expires after 30 minutes.** Measured 2026-11-03: session 6040cad5's `<sid>.denial.json` was stamped at 18:03:58 for a denied `crane delete`; the operator restored accept edits, the delete was re-run successfully, and the worker finished 32 Complete / 8 Running / 0 Failed — yet every round still read `stuck: denied`, so the Fleet Manager had a live, healthy worker it could not distinguish from a blocked one. `commands/worker-drive.md:327` already asked the worker to delete the marker once the step succeeded; nothing enforced or observed that, and the instruction was not followed. `scripts/round-readings.py` now decides the carrier through `denial_still_blocks`: the marker stops holding when the same tool has a successful `tool_result` in the session transcript (`<projects>/<sid>.jsonl`) later than the marker's `ts` — joined from `tool_result.tool_use_id` to the paired `tool_use.name`, with `is_error` absent-or-false as the success test — or when the marker is older than `DENIAL_TTL` (30 min). A success of a *different* tool does not clear it, a failed retry does not clear it, an unreadable transcript keeps it, and a stale denial falls through to the frozen carrier rather than short-circuiting to `none`. Every marker a row carries is evaluated, so an expired one on an earlier session id cannot mask a live one behind it. `commands/worker-drive.md:327` is corrected in the same pass — it told workers "nothing expires it", which the TTL makes false; the delete-on-success instruction is unchanged and the ceiling is described as a backstop rather than a licence. Change set: `scripts/round-readings.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py`, `commands/worker-drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.237.1

- fix: **`attention-ask.py` can reach an HTTPS attention store on a python.org Python.** `ssl.get_default_verify_paths()` returns `None` for both `cafile` and `capath` on that build, so `urllib` rejected every certificate with `[SSL: CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED] unable to get local issuer certificate` before the request was sent — and the message reads as a bad server certificate, not a missing trust store. The store's chain was fine throughout: `curl` reached it and `openssl s_client` showed a valid `*.prod.nuke.benjamin-borbe.de` certificate. It went unnoticed because every store this script had used was `http://localhost`, where no TLS is involved, so the first HTTPS store was also the first to fail. `_ssl_context()` now resolves a bundle in the caller's order of intent — `$SSL_CERT_FILE`, then `certifi` when installed, then the platform bundle (`/etc/ssl/cert.pem`, `/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt`, `/etc/pki/tls/certs/ca-bundle.crt`) — and is passed to both `urlopen` call sites. It returns `None` when the interpreter's own default paths already carry a bundle, so a machine that was working keeps exactly the request it sent before; it also returns `None` when no bundle exists anywhere, because there is nothing to trust and failing loudly beats silently trusting nothing. A candidate is accepted only when it yields a context that actually **loaded roots**: an existing path that parses to zero certificates, an unreadable one, or a stale `$SSL_CERT_FILE` naming a file that is gone all fall through to the next source rather than being returned. Existence alone is not enough — a certless bundle builds a context with no trust anchors, so the original `CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED` persists and the fix is a silent no-op that reads exactly like the bug. The interpreter's **own** default gets the same test, and that is the asymmetry the first draft missed: `get_default_verify_paths()` reports the compiled-in OpenSSL paths rather than paths that were checked, and a python.org macOS build names a `cert.pem` under its framework that does not exist until `Install Certificates.command` has run — so trusting the report alone would return the broken default on exactly the machine this fix targets. The default is therefore probed by building it and counting its roots, and only a default that really trusts something is accepted. The fix lives in the script rather than in a documented env var, since the next caller will not know to set one. Verified against the live prod store: `poll` of an absent id now returns the store's own `401` instead of a TLS error, while the same request through a bare `urlopen` still fails the negative control. Measured 2026-11-03.

## v0.237.0

- feat: add the A2A push router, the one process that owns the stable address a cluster relay forwards to. A fixed port admits exactly **one** supervisor server per machine, so the first to bind held it forever and every other session's `k8s` spawns reported `push_registration: not-attempted` with `listen EADDRINUSE` — silently, because the channel is additive by design (measured 2026-11-03: nineteen servers carried the config, eighteen were in that state). The router separates the stable endpoint from per-session isolation: each session keeps its receiver on loopback and registers its token with the router via `SUPERVISOR_A2A_PUSH_ROUTER_URL`, and the router forwards each push to the owning session by the `a2a-notification-token` header — never by the body, because a body-supplied `worker_id` would let one worker claim another's identity. Registration is loopback-only while pushes may arrive from anywhere the network allows, so a LAN caller can neither claim a token nor intercept another worker's status; an unknown token is refused at the router rather than forwarded, which keeps an unauthenticated caller from opening arbitrary loopback connections. A forwarding failure returns 502 rather than a silent 200, so a dropped push cannot read as delivered. The router therefore binds **all interfaces** rather than the LAN address alone — binding a specific LAN address makes loopback unreachable, and a same-machine session registering at that address is seen with the LAN source, so the loopback gate would refuse its own machine. Routes carry a TTL and are dropped on `ECONNREFUSED`, because this is the one long-lived process on the machine and a stale token→port route would otherwise forward a live worker's status to whatever later reuses that port. A router configured alongside the older `SUPERVISOR_A2A_PUSH_HOST`/`_PORT` supersedes that pair rather than merging with it, so the receiver stays on loopback at an ephemeral port.

## v0.236.3

- fix: **a `goal-drive` or `topic-drive` round now writes its manager's liveness record every round, so a live manager no longer reads `STALE` after one period.** The arm lived only in the one-shot loop commands (`goal-loop` step 3, `topic-loop`), and a round re-arms no wakeup by design — so a manager driven by rounds kept `ScheduleWakeup` armed while its `.arms` record aged out, and the fleet sweep classified it as lapsed. Three managers read `STALE` on 2026-11-03 with their loops demonstrably live; the only thing refreshing them was `topic-keepalive.py` restarting a stale session, which re-runs the one-shot arm. New router row **0a** (`commands/steps/round-liveness.md`) writes the record in both rounds, **before the pre-dispatch gate** — the gate's `exit 0` ends a no-change by-hand round, so a record below it would be missing on exactly the rounds that go stale.
