It skips a `STOPPED` subject (`--arm` clears the `.stopped` marker, so arming a stood-down manager would silently un-stand it down), takes the clamped `--interval` from the wakeup prompt (`--arm` requires it > 0), and passes `--branch` explicitly. `goal-drive.md`'s *"This is the round, not the arm"* and `--interval` lines are amended in the same change: the `ScheduleWakeup` arm stays one-shot, the liveness record is per-round.

## v0.236.2

- fix: `manager-attention-watch.py` announces a dead worker's gate instead of only logging it. The `UNREGISTERED` `HELD` record now prints `HELD  <sid8>  <label>  ::  <reason>; no CLEARED emitted` on the arm's stdout — the same `Monitor` channel that carries `NEW GATE` and `CLEARED` — so a manager stops waiting on a card that no longer exists. The other `HELD` cause (a session the watcher lost sight of, because it stopped being tracked) stays log-only, matched exactly via `UNTRACKED_DETAIL`; announcing it would fire on every gated session that leaves the tracked set.

## v0.236.1

- refactor: **the fleet drive leg's body sheds 2,195 bytes of incident rationale into `docs/fleet-drive-rationale.md`, restoring real headroom under the 30,720-byte budget.** `agents/fleet-drive.md` measured 32,353 bytes and was pinned there by its own baseline entry, so every later change to the drive leg either grew a file already 1,633 bytes over budget or tripped the stale-baseline rule. The rules stay in the body; the measurements and the reasoning behind them move to the new page behind a pointer, and `scripts/budget-baseline.json` is **tightened** to the new size rather than raised. The body now measures 30,158 bytes — 562 bytes of headroom — and the ratchet records the lower maximum instead of pre-authorising the old one.

## v0.236.0

- feat: standing resource guard for long-running local services — `scripts/service-guard.py`, run every fleet round as the fifth reading of router row 15 (`commands/steps/service-guard.md`). It reads each guarded service's `phys_footprint` (never RSS, which on macOS under-reads by more than an order of magnitude) and `ps` CPU against the bar in `~/.claude/state/service-guard.json`, and with `--apply` reopens the owning task row when a breach lands on a terminal one, so a service that drifts back after its fix is caught by the round instead of by the operator. `UNKNOWN` is a third answer that exits non-zero, never rounded to `OK`; the guard reopens and never restarts.

## v0.235.1

- perf: the two history pollers ask the attention store to narrow server-side — `manager-attention-watch.py` reads `?dedup_key_prefix=park:` (the only keys its loop reads) and `answered-watch.py` reads `?producer_id=<session>` — instead of the whole history. Measured 2026-11-03 on the Mac board: eight manager watchers each read `?limit=50000` every 60 s and the endpoint was 41 % of the store's allocation. A store that predates the parameters ignores them and both readers still filter client-side, so either deploy order is safe.

## v0.235.0

- feat: **`close-worker.py` closes a finished headless worker instead of refusing it — the same guards, and SIGTERM where the pane kill used to be.** `entrypoint: sdk-cli` was a refusal (`headless-target`, *"it has no pane to close"*), so the fleet manager had no supported verb for a finished SDK worker and the operator killed each one by hand — eleven `! kill <pid>` runs on 2026-11-02. A pane is where a close is *delivered*, never what authorises it: the registry carries a real pid for an SDK worker, so the pane-less branch now skips only the pane join and signals that pid instead. **Only `sdk-cli` takes that path** — it is the one pane-less value measured in the fleet, and an unmeasured entrypoint still falls through to the pane join, which is fail-safe (`❌ error:`, never a close) rather than aiming a SIGTERM at a pid on a guess. Every guard still runs first, in the script's existing order (`held-session` outranks everything, then `busy-target`, `dirty-worktree`, `gate-held`, `task-unresolved`, `task-not-terminal`), and the `EXIT_DEADLINE` wait plus the stale-entry cleanup are shared by both branches, so a close that cannot confirm the pid gone still exits 1 and says so. Two guards were tightened for the population this reaches: for a pane-less target, `busy-target` also refuses on an open tool marker younger than 20 minutes (`~/.claude/state/attention/<sid>.tool.json`), because a session parked on the permission channel keeps `status: idle` and writes no transcript closer for `gate-held` to find — and that check is deliberately scoped to `sdk-cli`, since a tab session's `status` already moves and a second check would only delay every tab close; and the SIGTERM is preceded by a re-read of the registry entry, so a pid recycled during the guards' tens of seconds cannot be signalled by mistake. ⚠️ **`headless-target` is gone from the token list**, which is a contract change for any caller branching on it — notably `fleet-triage`'s manual `kill` / `kill -9` block, which now falls through to a real close; `commands/close.md` carries the eight remaining tokens and both shapes of the success line. ⚠️ **The task gate is what still separates a *finished* headless worker from a *parked* one** — an exited SDK worker waits for a continuation, so `task-not-terminal` refuses it exactly as before (`docs/fleet-surface.md` § A headless worker exits at turn end).

## v0.234.0

- feat: let a `k8s` spawn register its status callback against a caller-supplied URL, so a cluster worker is no longer handed `host.docker.internal` — a name CoreDNS does not resolve inside a pod, and whose LAN substitute the worker's own egress NetworkPolicy drops. `beginCreate({ callbackUrl })` overrides the per-spawn URL, the registry keeps it per token so a follow-up turn re-registers against the URL its own worker was created with rather than this process's default, and `SUPERVISOR_A2A_K8S_CALLBACK_URL` names the in-cluster relay the operator deploys; unset leaves the previous behaviour, visibly rather than silently. The receiver also takes a fixed `port` (`SUPERVISOR_A2A_PUSH_PORT`) and a bind `host` (`SUPERVISOR_A2A_PUSH_HOST`), because a relay's upstream is deployed configuration and an ephemeral loopback port cannot be dialled from another host; `0` and `127.0.0.1` stay the defaults, so the docker path is unchanged.

## v0.233.4

- fix: **`/supervisor:open`'s `--flagged` batch no longer approves rows it is about to hold — the approval now runs after the readiness dispatch, so a held row stays at `phase: todo` instead of entering the approved-not-started queue.** An approval is an admission: `approved_at` is the field `scripts/approved-not-started.py` counts, and the batch previously wrote it for **every** selected row before readiness decided whether that row would ever open — so each run that held K rows added K to a queue that exists precisely to be drained, and nothing on this side ever removed them. On the batch path the dispatch now runs first and the approve pass covers only rows whose readiness outcome is to open; a held row returns to the operator's approval inbox and is approved on the run that actually opens it. ⚠️ **The single-task path keeps the old ordering deliberately** — Gate 1 (`phase: todo` refuses) runs after this step and depends on the approval having landed, so moving it there would refuse every single-row open. `commands/open.md` § Step 1.5's rule *"a row that fails the first is never probed by the second"* now names the batch approve pass as a deliberate exception, and the run prints a `not ready <task>` line per row left unapproved so the ordering is auditable from the output.

## v0.233.3

- fix: the `goals:` declaration read no longer drops a shape. `scripts/manager-predispatch.py` read `goals:` through a `[[...]]`-only extractor, so a task declaring its goal as a bare block item (`goals:` then `    - Name`, no brackets) — or as a plain or nested scalar — contributed nothing to `resolve_tracked` and silently left the tracked set: no row, no warning, no failing assertion. Measured 2026-11-02 on the live `private-personal` vault, the caller's scan read 61 names against the gate's 60, differing by exactly the one task in the bare-list shape. The normaliser now lives once in the new `scripts/goals-declaration.py`, shared by `manager-predispatch.py`, `reset.py` and `topic-spawn.py` — three readers that had disagreed while each looked self-consistent on its own. The shared reader also closes two traps the split copies had each got half-right: a quoted flow-list item may carry a comma of its own (`goals: ['[[Name, With Comma]]']` is one name, and splitting the list first shatters it), and a blank line between `goals:` and its items does not end the block. `blocked_by` deliberately keeps its existing reader — its "declares a blocker that did not parse as a name" branch is a different contract.
- fix: `manager-coverage-gate.py --changed` segments its value against the known owner names rather than splitting on commas first. A task name containing a comma — *"Digest Watcher Judges File Arrival, So a Late-Landing Digest Trips a False Report on a Timely Round"* — was shattered into two fragments that matched no owner, so `resolve` returned `None` and the gate failed open to every owner: measured on a live 61-owner set, a one-owner change dispatched all 61 at full cost. A comma inside a name is now one name, and a name the set cannot place still fails open whole-set, unchanged.

## v0.233.2

- test: pin the below-box action-line order against the drawn (tree) rows

## v0.233.1

- test: pin the exit-record coverage check at the board boundary, strip control characters from its stderr lines, and stop re-reading the pane map and the whole store per render

## v0.233.0

- feat: `attention-ask.py` sends a bearer token when `$ATTENTION_STORE_TOKEN` is set, for stores that require authentication. Unset — the default — keeps the existing unauthenticated behaviour unchanged. Without this, posting to a token-gated store fails with a 401 that reads as "the card was never posted", which is how an approval collected on one store and read from another goes unnoticed. Scope is deliberately `attention-ask.py` only: the other scripts that read `ATTENTION_STORE_URL` (including `attention-answer.py`) are **not** covered, so against an auth-enforcing store this authenticates the post and nothing else.

## v0.232.0

- feat: `fleet-board.py` flags a live manager pane running without the exit recorder — a `⚠️ UNRECORDED:` action line below the box (and `exit_record` in `--json`) for each manager whose `session-exit` record carries no `kind=spawn … writer=trap` line with a still-alive pid, plus an `unknown` count for panes `wezterm cli list` cannot resolve. New `scripts/exit-record-coverage.py` owns the rule; the store was previously read by no sweep, so a hand-opened manager's death left nothing to attribute.

## v0.231.2

- fix: **a `role: human` session now takes its chip from the vault family, so a Seibert tab no longer comes up in the private colour.** Measured 2026-11-02: four `seibert-brogrammers` rows (BRO-22486/22487/22489/22491) opened as panes 349/350/352/353 and every one came up cyan. `resolveRole` reads only the role, and the map carries a single `Direct` entry with `chip: cyan`, so nothing on that path could see the vault. `server/role-map.mjs` gains `FAMILY_CHIPS = { seibert: 'green', private: 'cyan' }` and `chipForRole()`, which refines the role's chip by vault family for `human` only: `manager` and `agent` are untouched, and an unlisted family (`assistant-*`) keeps the map's own chip rather than silently picking one of the two. ⚠️ **The rule could not live in the WezTerm config**, where `window_specs` publishes `purpose → {chip, window_id}`: a purpose is 1:1 with a window, and WezTerm knows windows rather than vaults — the vault exists only at spawn time — so a publish-side split would need two purposes competing for one window id and the JS would still have to pick between them. The refinement runs after `resolveWorkerTarget`, because the family must come from the RESOLVED vault (the raw `vault` argument is absent on every cwd-only spawn), while `resolveRole` keeps its place above the answer-path probe so a bad role is still refused before that probe's ten `spawnSync('ps')` calls. The stale `human → cyan/Direct` role tool description and the matching `docs/fleet-surface.md` mapping are corrected, and a source-shape test pins that the spawn path feeds `chipForRole` the RESOLVED `workerTarget.vault` rather than the raw argument. The family table is read with `Object.hasOwn`, so a family named after an `Object.prototype` member falls back instead of returning an inherited function into a `/color` argument. ⚠️ **The rule is a hand-kept pair, not one source:** `commands/open.md` § 3.0 carries the same table for the shell path (the resume branch and the no-supervisor-MCP fallback), because a bash caller cannot import this module — and that path still resolves `human → cyan` from the map alone, so a resumed `role: human` session in a `seibert-*` vault remains cyan until it is mirrored. Pinned by all six role × family combinations plus family-parsing, fallback and prototype-chain cases in `server/role-map.test.mjs`.

## v0.231.1

- fix: **a digest round that ran on a degraded roster join is aged by its own file mtime, not by an older baseline — so a working fleet manager no longer reads as a lapsed one.** A round whose roster join collapsed writes `snapshot written: skipped (roster join incomplete: 5/52 resolved)`, which `parse_snapshot` could not parse, so `newest_round()` carried the newest parseable baseline forward. The round's own work was invisible and the staleness clock ran against a baseline from a different round. Measured 2026-11-01: `~/.claude/state/fleet-handback/2026-11-01T110630.digest` (mtime `10:08:14Z`) was aged against `2026-10-31T19:47:39Z`, and `ledger.txt:16312` recorded `STALE digest=2026-10-31T19:47:39Z age=51862s limit=1080s quality=carried-forward` at `10:12:01Z` while that round had just run. The new `join-degraded` quality covers both roster-join renders (`incomplete`, and `failed; previous snapshot left intact`) and is deliberately **not** a blanket "the newest skipped round is fresh": `skipped (roster not supplied; round failed)` did not run and still carries forward, and a genuinely old degraded round still ages out and still reports. Classification lives in `parse_snapshot` (value only), the aging in `newest_round()` where the path is. ⚠️ **The keeper needs no change and gets none.** `fleet-keepalive.py`'s `last_reading()` parses only `age`, `limit` and `digest`, so `quality` never reaches it — the fix that reaches the keeper is the age, which now stays inside `LIMIT`. Verified end to end: the replay of the real digest prints `quality=join-degraded digest=2026-11-01T10:08:14Z`, and the keeper chain (temp `FLEET_DIGEST_DIR` + `SUPERVISOR_FLEET_KEEPER_DIR`) answers `ok` / no restart, where the unfixed `8fc1889b` control answers `carried-forward` / `stale`. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_fleet_digest.py` **32** (five added, each failing on `8fc1889b`): both roster-join forms classify, `roster not supplied` does not, a degraded newest round is aged by its mtime, a non-join skipped round still carries forward, three degraded rounds spanning > `LIMIT` stay `ok` through `fleet-keepalive.py`, and an old degraded round still reports `STALE`/exit 10. Change set: `scripts/fleet-digest.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_digest.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.231.0

- feat: **the retired session-field fallback is gone, now that both vaults are migrated.** Every reader that resolved `claude_session_id` first and the retired key second — `session-scope.py` (including its `_MANAGER_STAMP` regex), `crashed-managers.py`, `topic-keepalive.py`, `topic-spawn.py`, `worker-keepalive.py` and both `flagged-rows.py` sites — now reads `claude_session_id` alone, and the contract prose in `commands/open.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `commands/steps/topic-sweep.md`, `agents/topic-sweep-reader.md` and `docs/fleet-surface.md` states one field instead of two. This is the second half of the tolerance window the previous release opened: the migration of 54 goal and topic pages across `private-personal` and `seibert-brogrammers` landed first, verified per page against a pre-migration baseline (54/54 values preserved, zero retired keys, zero duplicate keys), so no page remains that only the fallback could read. ⚠️ **The fallback was doing real work until the migration, and removing it early would have been silent:** a page carrying only the retired key would have resolved to no manager, which readers treat as "free to start one" — a second manager on a subject that already had a live one. ⚠️ **One side effect of the unified field is recorded here rather than left implicit:** `scripts/fleet-sessions.py::_walk_task_stamps()` scans `24 Goals`/`23 Goals` for `claude_session_id`, so a goal manager's session now maps to its goal title and `scripts/fleet-board.py` fills `task_title` and computes `unblocks` (`nudge`/`reap`/`close`) for manager rows that previously had neither. Grouping is unchanged — managers still classify by name and colour — and the effect is benign, but it is a behaviour change no test asserts.

## v0.230.1
- fix: **the changelog fold is now caught by a pre-push hook, and the CI precheck warns instead of failing.** A blocking CI check must fail the run, and a failed run mails the operator; this repo cuts a release on every merge, so a branch folds within minutes of going stale and the precheck fired on nearly every push — measured 2026-11-02: 15 genuine folds and 15 failure mails in 29 h, every verdict correct. `scripts/hooks/pre-push` runs the same guard against `origin/master` and blocks the push; `make hooks` installs it via `core.hooksPath`, once per clone, covering every worktree of that clone. ⚠️ **It hangs off pre-push and not off `precommit`** — the guard reads the CHANGELOG from a commit (`git show <ref>:CHANGELOG.md`), so at `precommit` time it evaluates the previous commit, which is the one that cannot fold; only after the commit does `HEAD` carry the bullet under test. The script is read from the coding marketplace clone, so `bborbe/coding` keeps the single copy of the logic; a missing guard fails the hook rather than silently skipping, an unreachable `origin/master` is announced before checking against the last known copy, and a push of `master` itself is skipped. The pull-request workflow keeps the prediction visible as an `::warning` annotation, without a mail

## v0.230.0

- fix: **a round's `.digest` now lands at the instant it stamps, so the watcher stops reporting a lapse on a round that ran on time.** `scripts/fleet-digest.py` judges freshness from the newest `.digest` on disk, and the sweep reader writes that file at the end of its own turn — after `fleet-snapshot.py` has already printed `snapshot written:`. A tick falling in that window sees the previous round and reports it stale. Measured 2026-10-22: `snapshot written: 15:43:58Z`, the file on disk at `15:46:28Z`, and the `15:44:30Z` tick printed `STALE … age=1083s limit=1080s` on a round whose successor gap was 1051 s. One spurious report in the nine the 43-hour ledger holds. `fleet-snapshot.py` gained `--digest-path` (or `FLEET_SNAPSHOT_DIGEST_PATH`): when given, it writes a one-line marker naming `swept_at` to that path, atomically, in the same call that persists the snapshot; the sweep reader's full render later replaces the same path and carries the identical stamp. `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` step 9 passes it, and a refused snapshot (empty or malformed payload) writes no marker, so a round that persisted nothing still cannot look fresh. ⚠️ **The alternative — a watcher-side grace — was rejected on measurement, not preference.** Suppressing the `15:44:30Z` report also has to suppress the `15:45:41Z` tick at `age=1154s`, and the ledger's other eight reports include genuine gaps at `1085 / 1094 / 1095 / 1096 / 1102 / 1139 s`, so any grace wide enough to swallow the false positive silences six true ones; judging from the round's filename is worse still, because the filename start is *earlier* than its snapshot line. Pinned by nine tests in `scripts/tests/test_fleet_snapshot.py`: the marker exists at stamp time and parses as the round's baseline, the env form works, no path writes nothing, a refused snapshot writes nothing, the recorded case reads `OK` while the same tick without the successor still reads `STALE` (the control that proves the criterion can go red), the second `age=1083s` report (`2026-10-22T10:29:36Z`, a genuine 1214 s gap) still fires, and a real gap still reports within one poll of the limit. Change set: `scripts/fleet-snapshot.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_snapshot.py`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.229.0

- feat: **a worker whose only open items wait on a machine event for longer than the park threshold parks instead of holding a fleet slot, and two workers on the same event cost one watch.** Measured 2026-11-01: 37 live sessions against a cap of 30, of which 9 sat on background watchers with zero model calls for 20–30 minutes — one bound for ~21 h. The park rule existed but lost to the always-loaded closer rule, which legalises a resident watcher with no duration cap; nothing tied the two together, so the permissive one won. **That global rule lives in the operator's `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` and is NOT changed here** — this change reaches only sessions that run `/supervisor:worker-drive` or load `docs/worker-session-states.md`, and its threshold is now a single constant so the global wording change is a one-liner follow-up. `scripts/park-event.py` is now the single home of the threshold (`PARK_THRESHOLD_SECONDS`, printed by `park-event.py threshold`) and of the per-event watch store: `register` writes `park_event` and the re-check instant onto the task, and joins a watch a peer already holds for that event rather than creating one — the earliest re-check wins, so joining never delays the peer. Read-modify-write is serialised per key (`fcntl` lock) and the temp file is unique per writer, because two sessions parking on one event at the same moment is the expected case here, not an exotic one. A missing watch is distinct from an unreadable one: absence is the normal first-register case, while a corrupt record holds live memberships and is refused rather than replaced — and `due` prints `CORRUPT <file>` rather than skipping it, because a read failure must never manufacture an absence. A register with no session id, and a release of a non-member, are both refused — each previously succeeded while doing nothing. `register --task` records the task name on the watch so `due` names something `/supervisor:open` can actually open, and `--repark` moves the instant forward and increments a counter persisted on the record, because the parked session has exited by the time the round re-checks and nothing else survives to count re-parks. The fleet round reads it through `park-event.py due` in `commands/steps/keepalive.md` Step 5b — the fast path, never the only path, since the task's own `defer_date` still surfaces the row. `commands/worker-drive.md` § *Park a clock-bound session* reads the threshold from the script instead of restating it, and **the value is 15 minutes — a worker that used to stay resident for up to 2 h now parks after 15 min, an 8× tightening of the policy**, not merely a change of location, and `docs/worker-session-states.md` states that `external` is a state for a wait, not a licence to hold a slot. Pinned by 21 tests in `scripts/tests/test_park_event.py` (threshold single-home, date-only and no-offset refusals, shared watch with earliest-wake in both directions, join on a record missing its instant, slug-collision separation, corrupt-record refusal and its surfacing by `due`, non-member release, unknown-event exit code, concurrent registers keeping every member, re-park moving the instant for every member task without self-membership, `due` naming the task, list, due before/after, release to empty, task-key write, refused-write reporting). Change set: `scripts/park-event.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_event.py`, `commands/worker-drive.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `docs/worker-session-states.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.228.4

- fix: **the pod arm's `poll` no longer prints `OPEN` for a record that has already left the queue.** `scripts/pod-attention.py`'s `render_poll` printed `OPEN` for any non-`permission` item whose `classify` was `NOTHING` and whose rendered answer content was empty — the same un-keyed branch `attention-ask.py`'s `cmd_poll` carried until 2026-10-28. A record reaped by the dead-session sweep, the compact-crossing clear, a forwarded `SessionEnd` or `close_session_approval` posts an empty body with no `answered_by`, so a reaped `message` or `ack` card read exactly like a live gate. Measured 2026-11-02 on the deployed 0.227.3 arm: a real reaped `message` record (`state: closed`, no `answered_at`, no `answered_by`) printed `OPEN`. The branch is now keyed on `state == "open"`, mirroring the ask arm; a reaped close falls through to `NOT_OPERATOR_ANSWERED:`. ⚠️ That does **not** restore a reap/acknowledgement distinction in this arm, and an earlier draft of this entry wrongly claimed it did: `render_poll` prints no `ANSWERED_BY:` line, so an acknowledgement (`closed` + `answered_by`, no `answered_at`) classifies `UNATTRIBUTED` and renders byte-identically to a reap — both print `NOT_OPERATOR_ANSWERED: (no answer content recorded; item is closed)`. What the guard buys is narrower: a record that has left `open` stops claiming to be a live gate. Enumerated before fixing: of 453 store records the branch is reached by `message` (90 open / 78 closed) and `ack` (5 open / 1 closed); `permission` never reaches it. Pinned by five tests in `test_pod_attention.py` — a live `message` gate still reads `OPEN`, while a reaped `message` close, a reaped `ack` close, an unreadable `state` and an acknowledgement close all read `NOT_OPERATOR_ANSWERED:` (the last pinning the limitation above, not a distinction). Mutation-checked by reverting the guard: **4 red, the live control green**. Change set: `scripts/pod-attention.py`, `scripts/tests/test_pod_attention.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.228.3

- fix: no router table can route a manager-proposal step — `scripts/check-manager-proposal-row.py` fails when any `commands/*.md` router row names `commands/steps/propose-manager.md` in its `File` cell, or runs `propose-managers.py --post` from its `Step`/`When` cells; wired into `make precommit` as `check-manager-proposal-row`. The row's removal (PR #542) was unguarded: `check-step-when.py` validates rows → step files only, so a step file with no row is never checked, and the inline shape is invisible to it because it skips rows naming no step file. Rows are read, never the file body, so a prose mention stays green. The header is matched by containment, so an extra column or a renamed neighbour cannot hide the table, and recognising zero router rows — or finding no router command at all — fails rather than printing `ok`. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_check_manager_proposal_row.py`.

## v0.228.2

- fix: **a `role: human` session's gate no longer reaches its manager's feed.** `gate-owner-filter.py --feed` reported gates raised by every session the watcher had spawned, whatever the session's `role` — and a `role: human` row is the operator's own workspace, opened into the Direct window by `/supervisor:open --flagged` (`commands/open.md` § Step 0.6), so its gate is answered in that tab and no manager acts on it. The row travelled `verdict()`'s `own-worker` branch, which returns `EMIT` before any role is read, so the exclusion belongs **above** it. Measured 2026-10-27 on Fleet Manager `ac24db61`: pane 2165 (`Check Prometheus Alerts - 2026-10-27`, `role: human`) fired the feed **three times** across three consecutive 30-minute windows, each firing a full manager turn spent re-establishing that the gate was the operator's own and relaying nothing. `is_human()` reads the gated session's own ledger record and matches ONLY an explicit `role: "human"` — the opposite default from `is_manager()`, deliberately: a pre-field record, a `null` role and a session with no record all keep every existing verdict, because excluding on an absence is the fleet-wide silence the other hops fail open to avoid. The verdict is `EXCLUDE`, not `DROP`, for the reason § GOAL SCOPE gives — the row stays visible in `--explain` / `--json` as `role-human` and only the bare feed omits it — and `counts_line()` reports it in its own `excluded(role-human)` bucket rather than folding it into `dropped(...)` or the vault's two exclusions. The match is deliberately not scoped to this watcher's own spawns: `role` is a global ledger fact, so a human row spawned by another manager is excluded too. Verified against the live case (pane 2165, `--self ac24db61-1dad-4c89-b1fe-18b454067584`): `emit own-worker` and a bare `2165` before the change, `exclude role-human` with `excluded(role-human): 1` and an empty feed after it, while a `role: agent` row the same watcher spawned still reads `emit own-worker` and prints its pane. ⚠️ **The exclusion is not a scope input, and three statements said it was.** It runs above the scope block and regardless of `--no-scope`, so `--no-scope`'s help ("no row is ever excluded") and the `SCOPE  unresolved:` stderr line ("no row is excluded; every gate emits exactly as before") were false whenever a role-human gate was present — the PR's own end-to-end test runs `--no-scope` and asserts `excluded(role-human): 1`. Both now say the scope excludes nothing and the role rule still applies, and `commands/steps/{attention-watcher,round-tail}.md` gained the same distinction where they described the exclusion family as vault-only. Change set: `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.228.1

- fix: **`/supervisor:open` reads a subject's session id unconditionally, so a goal or topic whose manager exited resumes instead of spawning a duplicate.** The page read was gated on *"when the registry matches nothing"*, and Step 2B/2C item 4's enumeration named that same empty match as a spawn outcome — so the enumeration won and a stale pointer spawned a second manager (measured 2026-10-27, a page id resolving `ABSENT` while item 3 forbids reaching item 4 on that verdict). Both branches now read the page field — `claude_session_id`, or the retired `manager_session_id` as a fallback — before the registry result, state that an empty match never *proves* no manager ever existed, and scope `None` to *registry empty **and** no page id* — so a subject that never had a manager still spawns. Pinned by `scripts/check-open-manager-resolution.py`, whose denial pins carry a negative control against the pre-change wording, wired into `check:` and `precommit:`. Change set: `commands/open.md`, `scripts/check-open-manager-resolution.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_open_manager_resolution.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.228.0

- feat: goals and topics record their manager's session in `claude_session_id` only, retiring `manager_session_id`. The two resolve steps (`commands/steps/goal-resolve.md`, `commands/steps/topic-resolve.md`) now write `claude_session_id` — the field vault-ui's `sessionButtonHtml()` keys the Resume button on and the only one it reads, which is why every goal and topic that carried the retired key showed Start instead of Resume. Readers stay tolerant for one release and resolve `claude_session_id` **first**, the retired key second: the writer sets the surviving key without clearing the old one, so a re-armed legacy page carries both and reading the retired key first would hand back the stale id — `session-scope.py`, `crashed-managers.py`, `topic-keepalive.py`, `topic-spawn.py` and `worker-keepalive.py` all follow that order. `flagged-rows.py` is flipped in this release too: its `subject_liveness()` — which feeds `owner_live` — read the retired key first while its own row builder already read the surviving one, so the two disagreed on exactly the mixed page this writer creates; both now read `claude_session_id` first.

## v0.227.4

- fix: supervisor scripts honour `defer_date`'s time of day and offset instead of truncating to the date — `scripts/defer-date.py` is the one parser, read by `open-queue.py`, `flagged-rows.py`, `next-focus.py`, `orphan-candidates.py`, `worker-keepalive.py`, `manager-quiet.py` and `close-worker.py`; a date-only value (and vault-cli's `T00:00:00Z` rewrite) still means the start of that local day, an unparseable value is never read as due, and a defer later today now counts as future for the close. Parsing is stricter than the old slice: a value it half-read (`2026-11-02 # note`, `2026-11-02 00:00:00 +01:00`) is now reported and withheld rather than guessed at. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_defer_date.py`.

## v0.227.3

- fix: **`progress-window.py` finds a vault task file when `--anchor` is relative and the session's cwd is a repo worktree.** A relative anchor resolved against the process cwd, so `/supervisor:worker-drive` — which runs from whatever cwd the session holds, a repo worktree whenever the session works in one — reported `UNKNOWN: anchor unreadable: <task>.md (No such file or directory)` and **never took the measurement the script exists to take**. Measured across five consecutive drive runs on one session, every one UNKNOWN, while the anchor task was open and being edited the whole time. `resolve_anchor` now falls back to a unique basename match under `OBSIDIAN_ROOT` (default `~/Documents/Obsidian`, overridable for tests) when the direct path does not exist and the argument is relative; a unique hit wins, two hits leave the anchor unresolved rather than guessing, and an absolute path is returned unchanged. The report's `anchor` field now carries the **resolved** path instead of the raw argument, and the failure message names the absolute-path requirement instead of printing a bare `No such file or directory`. Pinned by three tests in `test_progress_window.py` (relative resolves, absolute unchanged, unknown basename unresolved); tests 48 → 51. Change set: `scripts/progress-window.py`, `scripts/tests/test_progress_window.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.227.2

- fix: **the keep-alive's guards now fail a test when they are removed, and its two probes are bounded alike.** Three non-blocking findings from the #565 review, two of them holes in the tests that PR added. ⚠️ **`timeout=PROBE_TIMEOUT` could be deleted with every test still green.** The test stub was `def run(argv, *a, **kw)` and recorded only `argv`, so nothing asserted the bound — the hung-child guard was covered by no test at all, and deleting it silently restored the wedge it exists to prevent. `SessionVerdictTest` now records the forwarded kwargs and `test_the_probe_is_bounded_by_probe_timeout` pins the keyword. ⚠️ **`VERDICT_BY_EXIT` could drift from the instrument it mirrors.** The table is a deliberate duplicate — both scripts run standalone out of the plugin cache, so there is no shared module to import at run time — and nothing compared the two, so `session-liveness.py` could renumber a verdict while this file kept mapping the old numbers, turning a live session into a resume (or a dead one into a cannot-tell) with no red test. `VerdictTableTest` imports the instrument by path and asserts the table equals its `LIVE` / `ABSENT` / `UNKNOWN` / `AMBIGUOUS` / `MALFORMED` constants, plus a distinctness check so a collision cannot pass the dict comparison. ⚠️ **`PROBE_TIMEOUT`'s comment claimed a tick-wide guarantee that only one of the tick's two probes honoured.** `cadence()` called `manager-liveness.py` with no `timeout=` and no `except` guard, so a hung or unrunnable child blocked the keep-alive forever — worse than the abort the guard prevents, because no verdict is ever printed. It is now bounded by the same `PROBE_TIMEOUT` and guarded by the same `except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError)`, so the comment is true of both probes rather than narrowed to one. ⚠️ **A probe that cannot answer is now `UNREADABLE`, not `INVALID`.** `INVALID` is `manager-liveness.py`'s own verdict for a record that EXISTS and cannot be read, and `main` restarts a LIVE session whose loop is `INVALID` — folding a crashed or timed-out probe into it restarted a live manager on no evidence. This mirrors `scripts/crashed-managers.py`, which records the same distinction for its own `PROBE_FAILED`. ⚠️ **The exit-1 residual is closed, not merely documented.** `ABSENT` (exit 1) is the one verdict that authorises a resume, and exit 1 is also every Python traceback's code — so a crash in `main()` outside `check()` read as a confident death and would have resumed a manager that is already running (`check()` itself already degrades to `UNKNOWN`). `session-liveness.py`'s `--check` path prints `ABSENT — <message>` to stderr on every genuine ABSENT, so the exit code is now corroborated by that word (matched per line, on a word boundary, so `ABSENTEE` and a prose mention do not count); an uncorroborated exit 1 is `UNKNOWN`. The same residual on the stdout scan's side did not exist, which is why it went unseen. Tests 26 → 39. Each guard is mutation-checked: dropping either `timeout=`, drifting the table, or removing the corroboration turns at least one test red. Change set: `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_keepalive.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.227.1

- fix: **an abandonment dated in the future no longer excuses a step, and three statements the debt gate's own review found inaccurate are corrected.** `_skips` honoured an abandonment when `ts >= int(started)` with no upper bound, so a record dated forward — a clock jump when `abandon()` stamped it, or a hand-edited line — would excuse the step on every round until wall-clock caught up, defeating the round-scoping that exists to prevent a permanent green. The window is now bounded at both ends — `int(started)` at the low end and `now + 1` at the high, the same one-second tolerance on each side — pinned by `test_a_future_dated_abandonment_is_ignored`, which reddens when the upper bound is removed. ⚠️ **The three doc corrections are the same class: a mechanism stated wrongly is worse than one not stated.** (1) `drive.md`, `keepalive.md` and `flagged-open.md` said step 19's `--check` is what rewrites the debt, which is false — the gate writes it too — and drew a consequence that does not follow ("a round that stopped at this gate would leave it open forever"); the actionable rule (never end the round here) is kept, the stated mechanism is corrected. (2) `fleet-round-tail.md` said an abandonment is honoured "only for the round it was paid in", which had no upper bound; it now names both ends. (3) The same file's `Does NOT catch` bullet counted five write paths and omitted `output-shape.md` item 13, which runs `--debt` on every tick at router row 20 — the writers are six, and a round that skips its sweep but still renders its output does record its own skip. Tests 61 → 64. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `commands/steps/{drive,keepalive,flagged-open,fleet-round-tail}.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.227.0

- fix: `restart-worker.py` resolves `vault-cli` via `PATH`, `$GOPATH/bin` and known install locations instead of a bare name, and a missing `vault-cli` now exits 3 with an `environment-fault` line instead of printing as a launcher refusal (exit 1)

## v0.226.0

- feat: **a fleet round that skips its sweep can no longer report itself as a whole one, and the skip is carried into the next round as a debt.** Measured 2026-10-31 on supervisor 0.222.0: over twelve post-deploy rounds the fleet round armed twelve times and wrote a digest seven, and of the five that did not only one (11:50) ever ran the tail — the other four (10:42, 11:00, 13:11, 13:27) never called `--check` at all, and the 13:27 round rendered a whole-round closer ending `⏰ Next: the 13:43 fleet round` with no digest behind it. `scripts/manager-receipt.py` now prints `INCOMPLETE ROUND — skipped: <step> — <why>` instead of `skipped: <step> — <why>` — a POSITIVE marker that cannot exist at the previous revision, because the pre-change skipped rounds had already dropped `Snapshot written` from their render, so an absence-based signal would have passed on exactly the rounds this exists to catch — and writes the skipped steps to `<slug>.debt` beside the receipt — fleet tier only, because the goal/topic tier shares `--check` but nothing there chains `--debt`, so writing the file for it would leave state no command reads. ⚠️ **The debt lives outside the receipt on purpose:** `--start` clears `fields`, so a debt stored there would be wiped by the very round that must pay it, and the skip would die with the round that caused it. `--debt` prints `debt: <step>` and exits **12** while a step is owed, **0** when clear — and **2** when it cannot read or write the debt file, a third state the render must branch on rather than read as an empty one — so `--debt && <leg>` is a gate rather than another sentence a model may skip; the three non-sweep legs (drive, keepalive, flagged-open) chain it. ⚠️ **`--debt` re-evaluates the round in progress rather than reading the file alone** — the gate sits at router rows 5/5b/5c and the tail's `--check` at row 19, so a file-only read would judge every round on the *previous* round's verdict: blocking a round that had already swept, and never blocking the round that actually skipped, the exact inversion of what the gate is for. It recomputes the skip set from the current receipt with the same function `--check` uses, rewrites the file, and falls back to reading the file only when the round cannot be judged at all (no receipt, lapsed window) — so the file still fires on a round that never ran its tail, the 13:11 and 13:27 shape, and a round that runs its legs but not its tail now records its own skip. `--abandon --step <step>` is the single recovery verb: it drops one step from the debt and records it timestamped in `<slug>.abandoned`, honoured by `--check` only for the round it was paid in, so an old abandonment cannot excuse the step forever. ⚠️ **The arm is never blocked** — `fleet-keeper.py` reads the digest ledger and would resurrect a refused round within a minute, turning one skipped step into a crash loop; the guard sits where the round reads, not where it arms. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `commands/steps/flagged-open.md`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.225.2

- docs: **`docs/session-tiers.md` no longer says the fleet keeper restarts the Fleet Manager.** Since v0.225.1 the keeper is report-only. Rule 1 ("a crashed or lapsed manager … is restarted automatically") now names the fleet tier as its one exception: a fleet lapse is reported on a card and never restarted, with the measured reason (healthy rounds run median 18 / p90 38 / max 51 min against an 18-min `stale` threshold). The rule-4 paragraph now says the keeper opens and resumes nothing. Flagged by the pr-reviewer bot on #588 and kept out of that PR so the approved head would not move. Change set: `docs/session-tiers.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.225.1

- fix: **the fleet keeper is now report-only: it never kills, resumes or spawns a session.** Operator decision 2026-11-01 (card `efc771fb`), after the keeper killed the Fleet Manager it was meant to keep alive. Measured that day from `keeper.log` and the pane exit records: at 09:00Z and 09:05Z it killed the manager and could not resume it (`wezterm` was not on its launchd `PATH`); at 09:47Z it SIGTERMed a healthy manager it had resumed at 09:41Z. ⚠️ **The root cause is the trigger, not a constant.** Across 10 measured healthy rounds, arm→digest ran median 18 min, p90 38 min and max 51 min, while `fleet-keepalive.py` reads `stale` past 18 min, so about half of healthy rounds cross it. No settle window fixes that: one under ~40 min kills healthy managers, and one above it makes any restart cap unreachable. `scripts/fleet-keeper.py` therefore drops the whole act leg (the `restart-worker.py` call, the re-arm, the resume cap) and instead posts **one** attention card once the keepalive has read `stale` continuously for 40 min (about 58 min of digest age, above every healthy round measured). It reposts only after the fleet recovers and lapses again. It keeps the `ABSENT` card, the `/supervisor:stop` suppression and the heartbeat; an unreadable stop state still reports, with the caveat on the card itself; a lapse record that cannot be written or cleared posts its own card rather than silently suppressing a real lapse (an uncleared record would make the next lapse read as already reported); an undecidable liveness probe is carded at once; and still uses `fleet-keepalive.py`'s verdict as its only staleness input (word and exit code must agree). ⚠️ **Every card now names an explicit producer (`--producer-id fleet-keeper --producer-kind cron`).** `attention-ask.py` defaults the producer to `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` and refuses when it is unset, and a launchd job runs outside any session, so as first written the keeper could post no card at all: a real lapse would have been reported to nobody. Every keeper test mocked `notify`, which is how it went unseen. `NotifyArgvTest` now runs the real `attention-ask.py` with the session id stripped, as launchd strips it, and proves the post passes the producer gate, with a positive control showing the same post without a producer is refused. ⚠️ **Every condition card is posted once per episode, not once per tick.** The store collapses a re-push only while the item is open and its producer live, which this file cannot see. And `suppressAnsweredTwins` closes an open card whose identity (producer + key + payload) matches an answered one, so a constant key would let the operator's `acknowledge` on the first absent, unknown or liveness card silently swallow every later one. Each episode's key therefore carries its start time, and episodes end on `ok` and on a deliberate stop. The `absent` episode is per manager, so a new manager dying before any `ok` gets its own card. A lapse card, and every condition card, is re-raised once per day of its episode (days of the episode, not calendar days, so consecutive cards overlap rather than leave a gap), because cards expire after 24 h and an expired card behind a set `reported` marker would leave a day-long lapse silent. The two state-broken cards (unwritable, unclearable) cannot be episodic, since a broken state directory cannot hold the record. They are bucketed by UTC day instead, with a fresh identity each day so an acknowledgement never silences an ongoing fault. They do not flood within a day: the keeper's cards declare `owner:` liveness, which `attention-controller`'s `isProducerLiveWith` treats as always live, so the store collapses a re-push onto the open card. The launchd plist's `PATH` is now **system directories only** (`/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`). The report-only keeper calls nothing but its sibling scripts and `ps`, so it no longer needs `vault-cli` or `wezterm`, and user-writable directories ahead of `/usr/bin` would let a dropped binary shadow a system tool. The restart-era keeper's `PATH` was the source of both earlier failures: it was missing `vault-cli` (715 ticks, all refused) and then missing `wezterm` (it killed and could not resume). Tests: `scripts/tests/test_fleet_keeper.py` rewritten to the report-only contract (38 tests). It covers 40 min reported once, 39 min then `ok` not reported, one card per lapse, a fresh card after recovery, and a source grep with a positive control asserting there is no kill or resume path. The threshold, the dedup and the no-kill grep are each mutation-checked. `restart-worker.py --reason lapse` is left in place for its other callers. Change set: `scripts/fleet-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_keeper.py`, `launchd/com.bborbe.fleet-keeper.plist`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.225.0

- fix: **the resolver now refuses an agent-set task flag, which it had never emitted at all.** `refused` carried only `manager-row`, so `agent-set-flag` — a token the row contract names — was unreachable: a task whose `flag_set_by` is not `operator` or `legacy` (including an absent field) emitted `refused: []` and read as openable, the exact row `commands/open.md` § Step 0.5 excludes from the batch. The rule is per-kind, and the difference is live: a **task** refuses that provenance, while a **goal or topic** accepts any, because `scripts/topic-spawn.py`'s `flagged()` never tests it — applying the task rule to goals would refuse every goal the fleet flagged. `OPENING_ACTORS` and `REFUSAL_REASONS` are now exported, the schema's `refused.items` carries the enum instead of a bare string, and the tests assert the enum against `REFUSAL_REASONS` and `OPENING_ACTORS` against `commands/open.md`'s own declaration (read from the marketplace clone where installed). ⚠️ **A second defect fell out of writing the probe:** `blocked_by_of` stripped quotes from the list form of `blocked_by` but not from the inline form, so `blocked_by: '[[X]]'` emitted the quoted string while the same blocker written as a list emitted the bare name — two spellings of one blocker, and the consumer matching on the name would miss one. Tests 108 → 119 (`PerKindRefusal`, `BlockedBy`). Change set: `scripts/flagged-rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows_schema.py`, `scripts/tests/schema/flagged-rows.schema.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **a failed liveness registry no longer reads as "no live managers", and a `⚙ `-prefixed registry label no longer reads as an absent one.** Both produce the same wrong branch — `open-manager` against a manager that is live — which is a duplicate-spawn hazard, so each direction is pinned by its own test. ⚠️ `live_sessions()` returned `{}` on a non-zero `session-liveness.py --list` exit, and `{}` is a fact ("this fleet has no live sessions") while a non-zero exit is the absence of an answer: `--list` exits 2 when the identity registry is unreadable or the coverage measurement is UNKNOWN. With `managers` empty, `subject_live()` returned False for every subject, `owner_liveness()` derived `chain_live is False`, and `resolve_branch()` emitted `open-manager` for every goal and topic row. `live_sessions()` now returns `None`, `collect()` reports it as `{"registry": "unreadable", "effect": "owner_live null; open-manager withheld"}` in the payload's `fallbacks`, and `subject_live()` answers `None` — which every caller reads as "keep the subject as owner", so the unanswerable probe cannot open a second manager. ⚠️ `live_session_names()` matched the raw registry name and a ` Manager`-suffixed form but never stripped the leading status glyph, while both sibling readers do (`who-needs-me.py:strip_status_glyph`, `fleet-board.py:_norm_session_name`). Measured live 2026-11-01: the registry carries `⚙ No Agent Record May Sit in a Phase No Config Accepts`, `⚙ agent_6` and `⚙ Triage Twelve Hypothesis`, so a glyph-prefixed manager matched no subject page and its row emitted `open-manager`. `strip_status_glyph` is now mirrored from `who-needs-me.py` and applied to both forms, and `fleet_sid` compares through it too — its exact `name == "Fleet Manager"` match read a glyph-prefixed fleet manager as absent, making `owner_live` false. ⚠️ **Two smaller divergences from the authority, same class:** `_LIVE_LINE` matched only the `pid <n>` field form, so an endpoint-only session (`age <n>s`, no pid — cluster and mcp-timer transports) was silently dropped and its subject read `open-manager`; and the task flag predicate compared the literal lowercase token `true`, while `commands/open.md` § Step 0.5 reads `t.get('flag') is True` off a PARSED file — so `flag: True`, `flag: yes` and `flag: on` are YAML booleans the consumer accepts and the resolver dropped. The predicate now accepts the full YAML 1.1 true set unquoted and still rejects every quoted scalar, which is a string and never a boolean. `pane_of()` gained a per-sid cache (it spawns an interpreter and import-loads a 2300-line module per LIVE row, and rows share session ids exactly as `row_liveness` does), and `vault_configs()` reports a malformed `vault-cli config list` as a config error on exit 2 instead of an uncaught `JSONDecodeError` traceback. Tests 97 → 108 (`RegistryProbe`, `TaskFlagScalar`, `PaneCache`). Change set: `scripts/flagged-rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **the flagged-row resolver's `role_source`, `launcher`, `mode`, `pane` and `owner_live` now come from their real authorities, and the row contract is committed as a schema.** Writing the schema is what found all five. ⚠️ **`role_source: template` was unreachable for two independent reasons, so SC5's "the template wins over a disagreeing on-disk `role:`" was dead code**: `template_role` searched for the `# Source` footer inside the FRONTMATTER block, where it can never appear (the footer is in the body), and it anchored `frontmatter:` at column 0 while the real schedule YAML indents it under `spec.template` (`start-day.yaml` line 58 is `    frontmatter:`). Both fixed; the block now ends at the first line indented no deeper than its key. A page that NAMES a template the resolver cannot read is now reported in the payload's `normalised` list instead of silently degrading to `on-disk` — the same silent-rung shape, one step earlier — while a readable template that declares no `role:` (`topic-backup-saturday.yaml`, `shutdown-k3s.yaml`) is deliberately not reported, since reporting it would cry wolf on every template with no opinion. `launcher` was `launcher or None`, so every task without a `launcher:` frontmatter emitted null while `launcher_source` still claimed `worker-default` had won — a row that says "the default decided this" while naming no launcher, which the schema now rejects outright. It is resolved per `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 8 (the task's `launcher:` → `worker_launcher` → `claude_script`) and § Which launcher a manager opens on for goal, topic and `role: manager` rows (the page's `manager_launcher:` → `manager_launcher` → the `-claude` sibling → `claude_script`), reporting `task` / `worker-default` / `vault` / `subject` / `manager-default` / `claude-sibling` — the two arms report different sources on purpose, and a row whose launcher resolves nowhere is now skipped and counted rather than emitted hollow. `mode` defaulted to `headless` for every page without the field, which contradicts `commands/open.md` line 259 — such a row opens per the fleet's `spawn.mode`, so it is resolved from `~/.config/claude-supervisor/config.json` (default `interactive`), and a value outside `server/spawn-mode.mjs`'s `SPAWN_MODES` (`non_interactive` is live on eight pages) is normalised to the fleet default and REPORTED in the payload's `normalised` list rather than copied through. `owner_live` was `None` on every row for two more reasons: `live_session_names()` read `parts[2]` of a `session-liveness.py --list` line, which is the literal word `pid` rather than the session name, so the manager set was empty; and the registry probe and the `--check` probe were collapsed into one, which makes `owner_live: false` unreachable. The owner is now SELECTED by registry liveness (`manager_on_chain`'s own signal, per the authority this cites) and REPORTED by `--check` on the owner page's `manager_session_id` — two probes, deliberately independent, so a rotated session id reads `false` and a degraded Fleet Manager does too. `pane_of` read `pane_for`'s RETURN value, which is an exit code — `pane_for` prints the pane — so every live session resolved to no pane and folded into `skip` instead of `jump`, the exact hand-derived value the resolver exists to replace. ⚠️ **`scripts/tests/schema/flagged-rows.schema.json` is a contract with two directions, and the second is what makes the first mean anything**: every row the resolver produces validates, AND a deliberately broken row fails, one named mutation per case (`launcher: null`, `owner: null`, an unknown `kind` / `branch` / `liveness` / `launcher_source`, a string `window_id`, a missing field, an extra field). Each enum is asserted against the module that produces it — `LAUNCHER_SOURCES`, `ROLE_WINDOW`, `ROLE_CHIP`, `ROLES`, `SPAWN_MODES`, `LIVENESS_EXIT`, `KIND_ORDER` — so the schema and the code cannot drift apart, and the validator raises on any keyword it does not implement rather than passing a widened schema silently. Change set: `scripts/flagged-rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows_schema.py`, `scripts/tests/schema/flagged-rows.schema.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **`scripts/flagged-rows.py` emits one JSON row per eligible flagged page across every vault, carrying the full spawn facts.** `/supervisor:open --flagged` derives role, window, chip, name and liveness by hand at spawn time, and every failure in the 2026-10-31 batch was one of those hand-derived values — a `role: human` row opened as a pink agent, a hardcoded `window_id` after a WezTerm restart renumbered the windows, a dropped `-n` name, a resume attempted against a `LIVE` session. The resolver is a sibling of `open-queue.py` that reuses its predicates and emits 19 fields, each copied from its own authority. ⚠️ **Eligibility is the consumer's own gate, not "every page carrying a flag"** — `flag` AND `status: in_progress` AND `defer_date` absent-or-due; measured 2026-11-01, `private-personal` holds 776 flagged pages of which 45 are eligible, so an unfiltered resolver would spawn work on closed rows. ⚠️ **The flag predicate differs by kind and the difference is live**: a task binds to `commands/open.md` § Step 0.5's strict `flag is True`, while a goal or topic binds to `topic-spawn.py`'s `FLAG_TRUTHY` — eight of `private-personal`'s nine flagged goals spell it `flag: "true"`, which a strict reader drops. ⚠️ **Vault discovery reads the vault-cli config, never a sibling guess**: four vaults keep their goals in `23 Goals/`, the rest in `24 Goals/`, and a probe fallback is reported rather than taken silently. The `branch` set is an ordered partition covering all five liveness verdicts, with `unknown` outranking everything because only `ABSENT` may authorise a resume. Change set: `scripts/flagged-rows.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_rows.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.224.5

- fix: **a headless worker no longer dies five minutes after a completed turn, and no longer leaves its ledger row stuck at `running`.** `server/wrapper-stream.mjs` fetched the wrapper's `/events` stream with no dispatcher, so Node's undici applied its default `bodyTimeout` of `300e3` (`undici/lib/dispatcher/client.js:316`). An SSE body is idle by design for exactly the states this adapter serves — a child between turns, and a child parked on a question while a manager answers it — so five minutes of silence destroyed the body and undici raised `TypeError: terminated`, which reached `runAgentLoop`'s `catch` and settled a healthy worker as `error`. Measured 2026-11-01 on the deployed `0.224.2`, three runs of three: the throw landed **300.85 s / 301.27 s / 301.18 s** after the turn's `success` result, and the child was still `idle` afterwards every time — the wrapper terminated nothing. The event stream now runs on an `Agent({ bodyTimeout: 0 })`. ⚠️ **That dispatcher only works with `undici`'s own `fetch`** — passing an npm-`undici` `Agent` to Node's built-in `fetch` fails every request with `TypeError: fetch failed` / `UND_ERR_INVALID_ARG`, so `streamWrappedSession` now defaults to `undiciFetch` rather than the global `fetch` its sibling helpers use. That was caught by a live run, not by the suite, which is the reason the live run exists. The accepted trade-off is stated rather than implied: `headersTimeout` keeps its default and a refused or failed connect still throws immediately, but a body that HANGS — a wrapper that sends headers and then wedges — now has no upper bound, which is traded deliberately against a false termination every five minutes. ⚠️ **The `catch` was wrong in two ways at once, and fixing one alone would have left the other reachable.** It classified EVERY throw as a worker error, so a stream that died after the turn's `result` had been captured recorded a finished worker as a dead one; and it wrote **no ledger row at all**, so the durable record kept asserting `running` forever — the reading `server/ledger.mjs` names as one that must never be trusted, and one that outlives the server which wrote it. It now settles `done` when a non-error `result` was captured **and the throw is not a `StreamLaggedError`**, and files that result; everything else settles `error` with the error string, writing a terminal row on **both** paths. ⚠️ **The `StreamLaggedError` exclusion is load-bearing.** That error is raised precisely when the stream stopped while the child was still generating, and `wrapper-stream.mjs` exists to keep a lag distinguishable from an exit — a `result` can arrive MID-turn, so its presence is not proof the turn closed. Settling a lag `done` would resurrect the "a lag looks like a finished worker" defect the adapter was built to prevent, which is worse than the `error` it replaces. ⚠️ **A worker whose turn has closed with nothing parked is now stopped deliberately.** A wrapper child never exits on its own: it is spawned with `--input-format stream-json` and holds its stdin pipe, so after a turn it waits for the next message forever — measured the same day, **21 of 59** wrapper sessions were non-`exited` and `idle`, the oldest 22 hours, because the only thing that ever ended one was the five-minute timeout, which ended it wrongly. `runAgentLoop` gained an injected `onTurnComplete` hook that fires after each `result` is captured (a hook that throws is logged and must not settle the worker), and the supervisor wires it to stop the session only when the wrapper reports `state: idle` with `pending_turns: 0` and no parked question, **and** the attention store reports no open card. The decision is a pure module, `server/idle-worker.mjs`, because `supervisor.mjs` connects a stdio server at load and cannot be imported — an untested predicate here would leave the behaviour this change exists to deliver asserted nowhere while the change looked covered. It returns a named reason on every branch. ⚠️ **The keeps that explain a worker nobody ever reaps are logged — `wrapper-record-incomplete`, `unreadable-cards`, `open-card`, and every successful stop** — while `not-idle` and `turn-in-flight` are the ordinary mid-turn path and would log on every `result`. `wrapper-record-incomplete` is its own reason because an older wrapper omitting either field would otherwise make every worker a permanent keep, and the idle-worker leak would quietly survive the fix. ⚠️ **A deliberately-absent attention store is not an unreadable one.** With `SUPERVISOR_ATTENTION_STORE=off`, `config.attentionStoreUrl` is null and `fetchOpenCards` returns null without issuing a request — which reads as `unreadable-cards` and would keep every worker forever, in exactly the no-attention-stack deployment the config recommends for that case. There can be no card to orphan there, so the supervisor passes `[]` when the store is deliberately off and still passes the real `null` when a configured store FAILS, which keeps the fail-closed property where it matters. `pending_turns` is the signal that separates a closed turn from the mid-turn `result` this loop already guards against (`claude-wrapper` `pkg/supervisor.go:562` counts turns the child has not yet closed), and every keep-alive condition fails closed — an unreadable wrapper or store keeps the worker rather than ending one that could not be inspected. Tests: `server/agent-loop.test.mjs` 5 → **11**, pinning the die-after-success, die-with-no-result, die-after-`is_error` and `StreamLaggedError` branches plus the hook's ordering and its throw-containment; `server/idle-worker.test.mjs` **8**, covering every branch of the end-of-life decision including the `cards === null` store-outage case and the incomplete-record no-op. Change set: `server/wrapper-stream.mjs`, `server/agent-loop.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/idle-worker.mjs`, `server/agent-loop.test.mjs`, `server/idle-worker.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.224.4

- fix: **a worker parked on its own closer line on an `idle` session now escalates with `AGED` at 15 and 60 minutes.** `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py`'s `PARKED_REASONS` left out `idle+closer` on the premise that *"it is the shape the ownership-filtered FEED already carries"*. It does not. A closer panel is a `Stop`-hook record, and `who-needs-me.py`'s `is_open_gate` drops it from `needs-you` with `if is_rendered_panel(rec) and not include_panels: return False` (`:1247`, `is_rendered_panel` = `event == "Stop"`). The `kind` check at `:1239` does not separate them, because a closer panel carries `kind: "question"`. So the exclusion removed the shape's only escalation. Measured 2026-11-01 over 78 production `events.jsonl` files: 587 `idle+closer` parks across 313 sessions, 242 of them ≥ 15 min and 90 ≥ 60 min, and **zero** `AGED` records for the reason (all 272 `AGED` records were `registry:waiting`). The motivating case parked on a prod-write `approve:` for 69 minutes: `NEW GATE` fired and nothing followed. `idle+closer` is now in `PARKED_REASONS`. ⚠️ **Two consequences follow from that set, and both are intended.** (1) `INCIDENT_KIND_BY_REASON` now maps `idle+closer` → `question`, the same kind as `headless+closer`. The map's own rule is that every reason reaching `NEW GATE` is mapped, and `test_every_parked_reason_is_mapped` enforces it. (2) `repost_empty_parks` reads the same set, so an `idle+closer` park whose `Stop`-minted card the store has lost is now re-posted as a question-shaped `message` card. It stays suppressed while the closer's own card is open. `test_a_settled_idle_park_is_not_parked`, which pinned the exclusion, is inverted. The new `IdleCloserAgedTest` drives `main()` through 15 and 60 minutes and asserts both the stdout lines and the `events.jsonl` `detail` (`15m parked [idle+closer]`, `60m parked [idle+closer]`). Mutation-checked: dropping `idle+closer` from the tuple turns it red with only `NEW GATE` printed, the production symptom. `commands/steps/round-tail.md` carried the same false premise and is corrected. Change set: `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_attention_watch.py`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.224.3
- chore: **the changelog fold is now caught before the merge, not after.** `.github/workflows/changelog-unreleased-fold.yml` runs on `pull_request` and calls the new precheck in `bborbe/coding`, which computes the merge with `git merge-tree --write-tree` and fails the PR when a pending `## Unreleased` bullet would land under an already-released heading. The existing `changelog-fold.yml` stays as the post-merge backstop, and its comment no longer claims a pull-request check is structurally blind to the fold — that was true of a heading read, not of a computed merge. The 91 consecutive red master runs of 2026-10-30/31 were 91 detections, not 91 prevented.

## v0.224.2
- fix: **the closer lint decides on the message that ended the turn, not on an older one it could still read.** `reap-closer.py`'s `last_closer` scans backwards and returns at the first assistant message carrying a `👤 You:` line; when the harness had not finished writing the turn's final message, its line failed `json.loads`, was skipped, and the scan returned an **earlier** message's closer — measured 2026-10-31 on session `95e9b174`, where a real `approve: gh -R … pr merge 567 --merge` gate was recorded as `{"closer": "👤 You:    nothing", "reason": "not-a-gate"}` 266 ms after the message was written, so the v0.218.0 allow-list was never consulted on the closer the operator actually saw. A new `_partial_closer` recovers the closer from a partially-written assistant line (the text is already on disk; only the JSON is unclosed), and an assistant line that yields no closer now returns `None` rather than falling back to an older message. ⚠️ **A well-formed fixture cannot reproduce this at any scan bound** — `tail_lines` scans contiguously backwards from EOF, so reaching the earlier message implies reaching the later one; measured, and recorded in the task's `# Results`. The two fixtures therefore carry an unparseable final line: pre-fix they exit 0 / `not-a-gate` reading the earlier `👤 You: nothing`, post-fix exit 2 / `allow-listed` with `matched` `gh-pr-merge` and `vault-cli-session-close`. `_partial_closer` requires a `role`/`type` of `assistant`, so a partial tool result quoting the same words never becomes a gate. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py` **24** (eight fixtures added to `EXPECTED`, one two-sided salvage test, plus six guard tests — the `return None` branch, the tool-result guard, a quoted `--subject` that must not truncate a trailing `--admin`, a closer quoted in a `tool_use` input, a closer cut mid-command, and a literal backslash that must not read as a newline), `scripts/tests/test_reap_closer.py` **11** unchanged. ⚠️ **The salvage reads the JSON string body rather than scanning raw text.** Cutting the segment at the first `"` stops at an *escaped* quote, so `--subject "Release 1.2" --admin --merge` became `--subject \` and the `--admin` deny never fired — an admin merge released on the salvage path. It also reads only `type == "text"` blocks, matching the parsed path, so a closer quoted in a `tool_use` input is never recovered as a gate. ⚠️ **The assistant test accepts `message.role`, not just the top-level `type`.** A real record serializes `message` at key index 2 and the top-level `type` at index 9, so a line cut inside `message.content` — the exact shape this salvages — carries `"role": "assistant"` and **no** `"type": "assistant"` at all; keying on `type` alone left the whole salvage path inert while the tests stayed green. The fixtures were rebuilt to mirror that field order (`message` before `type`) for the same reason. ⚠️ **And it refuses a closer whose line runs into an unterminated tail:** the write can stop inside the command, and `--admi` is not `--admin`, so a half-command must read as `no-closer` rather than as a whole closer. The line end is found with a backslash-aware walk, because a literal backslash in the text (`C:\new`) carries the same `\n` pair as a real newline escape, and a plain split truncated the closer before a trailing `--admin`. Change set: `scripts/reap-closer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/18-partial-write-gh-pr-merge.jsonl`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/19-partial-write-session-close.jsonl`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/20-unreadable-assistant-no-closer.jsonl`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/21-partial-tool-result-quoting-a-closer.jsonl`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/22-partial-quoted-subject-before-admin.jsonl`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/23-partial-tool-use-quoting-a-closer.jsonl`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/24-partial-cut-inside-the-closer.jsonl`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/25-partial-literal-backslash-before-admin.jsonl`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the round receipt's staleness window is `manager-liveness.py`'s window, not a second one.** `scripts/manager-receipt.py` carried its own basis — `2 x` the UPPER median (`gaps[len//2]`) plus a 60 s slack — while its docstring asserted the two checks were aligned, and they could not be: the multiple differed (`2x` vs `1x`), the statistic differed (upper median vs true `median()`, which differs on every even gap count), and the slack differed (60 vs 180). Measured 2026-10-21 over the retained `.arms` history, the two limits differed on **46 of 46** subjects, and the band between them let a manager liveness had already called stale keep its round's fields judged current. The receipt now imports `observed_period()` and `SLACK` from `manager-liveness.py` and computes `period + SLACK`, so the limits are equal because there is one definition. **A second, silent defect fell out of the same read:** the receipt's slug is the bare base, but liveness has written `goal--<base>` / `topic--<base>` since the goal/topic split, so **4 of the 9** live receipts found no history at all and fell back to the reported interval while a full `.arms` record sat under the prefixed name; the record name is now resolved the same way. The lapsed line prints the rule it used (`period=<P>s(observed,1x+180)`), so the deployed output names the basis rather than leaving it to be inferred from the number. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `commands/steps/re-arm.md`, `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md`, `commands/goal-loop.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.224.1

- fix: **the fleet keeper no longer restarts a fleet the operator stood down on purpose.** `/supervisor:stop` writes `<slug>.stopped` precisely so a watcher reads the silence that follows as a stop, not a lapse (`docs/fleet-surface.md` § Session end). But the digest goes stale either way, and `fleet-keeper.py` read only the digest — so roughly one digest limit after every deliberate stop it would have killed and resumed the Fleet Manager the operator had just stood down. It now reads `manager-liveness.py --state` after a `stale` verdict and leaves a `STOPPED` fleet alone (verdict `stopped`, no card — a deliberate stop is not something to report). ⚠️ **The read SUPPRESSES and never TRIGGERS:** only the `STOPPED` token is acted on, and `manager-liveness.py`'s own `OK`/`STALE` is ignored, because its staleness is heartbeat-based and reads `OK` throughout a lapsed round — the keeper's staleness input stays `fleet-keepalive.py`'s verdict alone. An unreadable stop state kills nothing and is carded, since whether the operator stopped the fleet is exactly what a kill must not guess at. Tests 45 → 54, including a round trip through the real `manager-liveness.py --stop` writer; both directions mutation-checked (ignoring the marker, and letting `STALE` count as a stop, each turn exactly their own test red). Change set: `scripts/fleet-keeper.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_keeper.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.224.0

- feat: **the session-exit store can now answer "how many deaths were unexplained today" without a human assembling a pipeline.** `scripts/session-exit-record.py` gains `daily`: end-kind counts per calendar day plus the unknown-end share, with `--json`, `--days N`, and a `--line` form a monitor or a `grep` reads. ⚠️ **Only end kinds are counted** — `spawn` and `heartbeat` are markers, and folding them into the denominator makes the fraction track the fleet's size while still reading as "how many deaths were unexplained". A pane with a `spawn` and no end line is a session still running, so it is absent from every count rather than counted as zero. The metric exists because the store recorded a jump from 2 unexplained deaths on 2026-10-27 to 37 on 2026-10-28 and nobody saw it for four days: seeing it meant remembering to run a shell pipeline over 200+ files, and a metric that only exists while someone is looking is not a metric. ⚠️ **`--days N` zero-fills the window, and that is the difference between a metric and a report.** Emitting only the days that carry records makes a day with no deaths and a day the writer did not run look identical — both simply absent — which is precisely the confusion this store exists to remove. A window now always prints N consecutive days, a quiet one as `ends=0`. `launchd/com.bborbe.session-exit-daily.plist` runs `daily --line --days 1` at 23:50 daily, so the log grows by exactly one line per day: a day with no deaths prints `ends=0` rather than vanishing, and a *missing* line then means the agent did not run and nothing else. ⚠️ **A wider window would have destroyed that signal** — a 14-day re-print leaves the previous 13 days sitting in the log on every run, so a stopped agent stays invisible for a fortnight; that is why the invocation is one day, not fourteen. ⚠️ **Days are UTC**, because every record's `ts` is UTC and the bucket is its first ten characters; the 23:50 local run therefore reports a *provisional* latest row until the UTC day rolls over, which the plist states in place rather than leaving to be rediscovered. A line whose first field is not a date is skipped instead of being bucketed under a junk key, and a non-UTF-8 byte degrades one file rather than aborting the unattended run. `RunAtLoad` fires one on install, so the metric carries a baseline from the moment it is wired. Change set: `scripts/session-exit-record.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_exit_record.py`, `launchd/com.bborbe.session-exit-daily.plist`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.223.1

- fix: **a task deferred to today is no longer held as if the date were still in the future — in the auto-resume gate, its runbook mirror, and the fleet sweep's orphan reader.** `agents/goal-drive.md` clause (10) read *"`defer_date` is absent, or names a date that has already passed"*, so a row deferred to a given date was held **on** that date and lost it, although the vault's own rule is that `defer_date` keeps a task `in_progress` and makes it available **on** that date. Measured 2026-10-24: `Executor Spawns Agent Jobs Into the Sandbox Namespace` carried `defer_date: "2026-10-24"`, its session read `ABSENT` from `session-liveness.py --check`, and it was held across that day's manager rounds (the owning goal page records the hold at 18:58). ⚠️ **The prose clause was only half the defect.** `scripts/orphan-candidates.py`'s park filter compared `defer_date >= today`, so a due-today row was dropped from the fleet sweep's orphan roster *before* the gate ever saw it — measured as `[]` from the live vault, against the same reader over the same row with `defer_date` removed printing the candidate and over a tomorrow-dated copy printing nothing. Both now compare `>`, matching `.claude/scripts/sweep-gate.py`'s `is_deferred()` (`day > date.today()`), which was already correct and is deliberately untouched: editing it would put the two renderers out of step. ⚠️ **The off-by-one was tested in, not merely uncaught** — `scripts/tests/test_orphan_candidates.py` carried `test_same_day_defer_date_is_parked` asserting the wrong verdict, plus an end-to-end fixture deferred to *today* and asserted to be skipped. Both are inverted, and the new pair (the same fixture one day apart, verdict flipping) is what makes the comparison provably *read* rather than merely present.

- fix: **the round receipt recorded the park threshold as 1x the tick interval, so the sweep reader reconciled two different numbers on every round.** `scripts/round-readings.py` wrote `park_threshold_seconds: <interval>` — 900 at a 15-minute tick — while the rule is **2x** the clamped cadence (the runbook's wording, and what `.claude/scripts/sweep-gate.py`'s `park_threshold_seconds()` derives).
