These are two different cells: the **frozen** verdict is 1x the interval, the **park** cell is 2x, and the field was being written from the wrong one. Now `2 * interval`. `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py` pins the pair — 900 → 1800 and 300 → 600 — so a constant cannot pass for the rule, and both cases fail against the old value with `900 != 1800`.

## v0.223.0

- feat: **the fleet tier can act on its own verdict — a lapsed fleet round is now resumed instead of merely announced.** v0.221.0 shipped `fleet-keepalive.py`, which reads the digest ledger and answers `ok` / `stale` (exit 10) / `unknown` (exit 2) — and then stops, printing the words *"Resume the fleet manager"* while resuming nothing. So a lapse was detected, reported once, and `SUPPRESSED reason=already-reported` for every minute after, until a human looked at a screen: measured 2026-10-30, **six lapses in one day**, the last still open at **6h17m** (`age=22642s limit=1080s`). `scripts/fleet-keeper.py` is the missing leg. It consumes `fleet-keepalive.py`'s verdict as the **only** trigger — the verdict word and the exit code must agree, and a disagreement is `unknown`, never a restart — with no second staleness rule, because `manager-liveness.py --state` reads `OK` throughout a lapsed round by design (the heartbeat is written by the model-free gate loop, and a heartbeat proves the timer works, not that the round did) — resolves the fleet manager from its `worker-manager` registration, and resumes the **recorded session id** through `restart-worker.py`. ⚠️ **`ABSENT` is never restarted:** a dead fleet manager may have died for a reason a restart repeats and there is no tier above it to escalate to, so it is reported on an attention card instead; `UNKNOWN`/`AMBIGUOUS`/`MALFORMED` are cannot-tell and are never death. ⚠️ **It restarts, never opens** — there is exactly one fleet manager, so two sessions claiming the registration is its own refusal rather than a pick. ⚠️ **A resume is not a recovery until the loop is re-armed.** A killed session's scheduled wakeups die with its process, and `restart-worker.py` resumes it with `/color <chip>` as its only prompt — so without a re-arm the resumed manager sits idle, the digest stays stale, and the keeper would re-kill a healthy manager every tick until the cap tripped. After the resume the keeper sends `/supervisor:fleet-loop` into the pane it just spawned, on `commands/open.md` § 3.2's recipe, and cards an unconfirmed re-arm. Readiness needs `Session color set to:` in the pane's **last** lines — a resume re-renders its transcript, which already holds that line from every earlier open — **and** the resumed process reading `idle`; the resumed process is told apart from the killed one by pid, read before the kill, because the killed process's record can still read `busy` — and when the registry does not hold exactly one pid to read, **nothing is killed**, since a kill whose re-arm cannot be confirmed only feeds the loop. Text and submit go as two separate sends, and the re-arm counts only once the new pid goes `busy` after an `idle` read taken after `/color`. A dead **newest** registration is reported, never routed around to an older one still open in some tab. ⚠️ **The cap arms when the kill-and-resume RAN, not when it is proven to have landed** — deliberately unlike `topic-keepalive.py`, which resumes an already-dead session and so kills nothing when it fails; here every resume kills first, so a resume that does not land must count or the kill loop has no bound. The bound and the store are still `topic-keepalive.py`'s, imported rather than re-derived (3 per 30 min), and a 5-minute settle window keeps a manager that is still booting from being read as a fresh lapse. Stale registrations are tolerated: nothing deletes one, so claimants whose session reads `ABSENT` are dropped before a pick is called ambiguous. The keepalive's verdict word and exit code must agree, and every non-transient refusal or unconfirmed step is carded rather than reported as `busy`. ⚠️ **`restart-worker.py` gained `--reason lapse`, and it is load-bearing:** precondition 4 refuses a restart when nothing would load differently, which is correct for a code-staleness restart and wrong for a lapse — a lapsed round writes no file, so the unmodified precondition would have refused every legitimate restart and the keeper could never have acted. Every other refusal (held, busy, headless, unresolvable launcher) still applies. It also now prints a `killing-pid <pid>` line immediately before `os.kill`, so a caller can tell a refusal that came before the kill from a failure that came after it without matching refusal prose. The keeper's own liveness is a separate instrument — `~/.claude/state/fleet-keeper/heartbeat`, rewritten before anything can fail — so a dead keeper cannot read as a healthy fleet. Armed by a launchd agent (`StartInterval` 60, a fresh process per tick) because an in-session `Monitor` dies with its session and a detached loop is the failure mode being fixed. The keeper's `wezterm` helper is enumerated in `scripts/transport-read-check.py`; it keeps the three transport states apart by returning `(rc, stdout)`, so an unreadable pane can never satisfy the readiness wait. Change set: `scripts/fleet-keeper.py`, `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_keeper.py`, `scripts/transport-read-check.py`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `launchd/com.bborbe.fleet-keeper.plist`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.222.0

- feat: **the fleet round's tail checks that it armed and wrote its digest, and a stalled digest re-reports instead of going quiet.** The fleet round is a router map with no mechanical check that any row ran, so a round can arm its wakeup, take the readings and render like a full one while the digest was never written — measured 2026-10-30, twelve `manager-liveness.py --arm` calls across a window in which no digest landed for 6h38m, with nothing on that round's own output saying so. `scripts/manager-receipt.py` gains the fleet tier: `--branch fleet` resolves the `fleet--fleet-manager` slug `manager-liveness.py` writes, and two steps join its vocabulary — `re-arm` (an arm epoch **inside** the round window, since a ring survives from earlier rounds and `manager-liveness.py:345` empties it after a stand-down) and `digest` (the sweep reader's rendered payload, by its own `DIGEST … sessions … snapshot written:` opening line). They live in `FLEET_STEPS` rather than `STEPS` because a goal or topic round owes no digest and a shared tuple would report `skipped: digest` on every healthy one. `commands/fleet-drive.md` gains a round-tail row, `commands/steps/re-arm.md` and `commands/steps/sweep.md` record the two artifacts, and `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md` runs the check. ⚠️ **That check catches a round that ran and skipped a row, never a round that never ran** — a hand-assembled substitute reads no router table — so `scripts/fleet-digest.py --check` also stops deduping on the digest's epoch alone: a flat age still reports once, a **rising** age re-fires once per further `LIMIT`, because the epoch never moves during a lapse that never recovers and every tick after the first printed `SUPPRESSED` while the age climbed 1,374 s → 23,064 s. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/fleet-digest.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_digest.py`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/{re-arm,sweep,rules,fleet-round-tail}.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.221.4

- fix: **a fleet session parked on a machine is no longer read as stalled.** `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` step 8 gains three signals above the `stalled` rule — a `⏰ Ends:` declaration on the session's newest `Stop` record (`parked-on-watcher`), an open permission/AskUserQuestion gate (`waiting-on-human`), and a transcript written inside `LIVE_WINDOW` (`progressing`) — read for **every** row rather than `busy`/`shell` only, because an `idle` session parked on a watcher would otherwise fall through to `parked` and be **nudged**: the false positive reaches the operator twice, once as a false `stalled` and once as a nudge at a worker that is correctly waiting. ⚠️ **All three are shipped store reads, not new ones.** The `⏰ Ends:` slot is `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` input 13's own read and `agents/manager-drive.md:134` forbids *"a fourth definition of 'idle'"*; the gate comes from the feed step 1 already reads; and the in-flight signal is **transcript freshness**, because `manager-drive.md:130` records the `*.tool.json` marker as *"effectively dead today — nothing writes the marker any more"* — a marker-based row would have rested on evidence nothing produces. ⚠️ **`stall_count` now resets on any claiming signal.** Without that the counter is a second door: a session parked for several sweeps is `busy`/`shell` with an unchanged mtime every time, so it accumulates a count and renders `stalled` on the one round its own read fails. `agents/fleet-drive.md` step 1 refuses `parked-on-watcher` and `waiting-on-human` as candidates — they share `parked`'s prefix and nothing else, so the match is exact and an unrecognised class is not `parked`. **Both model-free renderers learn the same read** through a new shared `scripts/declared-wait.py`, following the `session-liveness.py` precedent rather than adding a private copy in each: `manager-predispatch.py`'s `liveness_of_sid` **and its `roster_owner` name-fallback sibling** both park a declared wait so `idle_stuck` exempts it, and `fleet-board.py` suppresses the `Unblocks: nudge` value — **the board's live route**, since its `problem` bucket is fed by the same dead marker and repairing that alone would change nothing. ⚠️ The new classes are added to the **out-of-repo** vault runbook (`65 Runbooks/Fleet Manager Session.md` § Sweep output's reconciliation table) — external to this repo, so deliberately absent from the `Change set:` list below and not verifiable from the diff — because a class that lands in the runbook and not in the renderer is the drift that table exists to catch; ⚠️ **`commands/fleet-loop.md` no longer carries a second copy:** a later merge moved that file's class list and its rationale into the same runbook, so the runbook is now the only home for the class vocabulary and this change follows it there rather than reintroducing an in-repo copy. ⚠️ **The replay harness is repaired so it can actually fail.** Three defects made it a no-op: `_rendered_class` read `fields[1]`, which is the `<age>` column, so every row was graded against its age and a correct reader reported MISMATCH; `materialise()` seeded no prior snapshot, so the `stalled` control row was first-seen (`stall_count` 0) and fell to `unclassified`; and the export block pointed `ATTENTION_STORE_URL` at a dead port, which made `session-liveness.py --list` answer UNKNOWN so every roster row resolved `[unresolved]` and rendered no CLASSIFICATION row at all. The class is read from `fields[2]`, the snapshot is seeded through a new `FLEET_SNAPSHOT_PATH` override on `fleet-snapshot.py` (the one reader in the store family without an env knob), and the roster join is supplied explicitly — stated as a boundary, because that join also reads the live identity registry and cannot be isolated from outside the harness. The expected classes no longer ride in the block handed to the reader. ⚠️ **Both agent bodies are recorded in `scripts/budget-baseline.json`.** The 30 KB context-budget gate refuses a new oversized body, and both files cross it: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` sat at **30717** bytes on master (three bytes under the cap) and reads **40443** after this change, `agents/fleet-drive.md` 30707 → **32369** — so no addition of any size fits without deleting reviewed prose. Recording them is the baseline's own stated mechanism (its `_note` is seeded from the post-change tree for edited files), and the ratchet then holds both at these sizes rather than letting them regrow. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_declared_wait.py` (new, **14**), `test_fleet_board.py` **95 → 101** (+6), `test_manager_predispatch.py` **188 → 192** (+4) — **+24 owned by this change**, each delta measured against `origin/master`. The whole suite reads **3821** on this branch; that is higher than the +24 because the branch has taken six `origin/master` merges since it was cut and each brought its own tests, so the suite figure is a measurement of this branch rather than a sum of this change's deltas. Change set: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `scripts/declared-wait.py`, `scripts/fleet-board.py`, `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_declared_wait.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_board.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/fleet-park-replay.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/fleet-park-signals.json`, `scripts/fleet-snapshot.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.221.3

- fix: **the closure reader's copied checkbox regex can no longer drift from vault-cli's silently.** `_VERB_BOX` mirrors `vault-cli task complete`'s gate by hand, and nothing tested or checked the copy: the reader's own `_BOX` diverges from it by design (`+` bullets, any marker char, wider spacing, no text required after the box) and none of those shapes was pinned, so an edit to either regex could move the verdict away from the verb with every test still green. Two guards now hold it. `scripts/check-checkbox-regex-drift.py`, wired into `make precommit`, fetches `vault-cli`'s `pkg/storage/base.go`, extracts `storage.CheckboxRegex` and fails with both literals printed when it differs — failing closed and naming the fetch error when upstream cannot be read, since an unreachable source is not evidence of agreement. `DivergenceClassTest` pins every divergence class on **both** sides, each test asserting `_BOX`'s count and `_VERB_BOX`'s; the mutation check confirms it pins something — adding `+` to `_VERB_BOX` and removing `+` from `_BOX` each turn the suite red. `commands/steps/goal-closure.md` now states that the `Whole page` line prints on every `--kind task` run with `0 open` as the passing reading, which its `OPEN` row had implied otherwise. The guard carries its own tests (`scripts/tests/test_check_checkbox_regex_drift.py`, fetch injected so the suite stays offline) pinning all six outcomes — match, drift with both literals printed, unreadable upstream, upstream without the literal, reader without the mirror, and a differently-named assignment — because a guard that argues against untested copies cannot be the one untested piece. Its extraction regexes are anchored under `re.M` so `MY_VERB_BOX = …` cannot satisfy them, and `curl` runs with `-q` plus `--proto`/`--proto-redir` pinned to HTTPS so neither a `~/.curlrc` nor an `-L` downgrade can undo the fetch's TLS. `UPSTREAM` deliberately tracks `master` rather than a tag: a tag would compare against a released vault-cli the installed binary may not be, which is the staleness the check exists to catch. Change set: `scripts/check-checkbox-regex-drift.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_checkbox_regex_drift.py`, `scripts/tests/test_goal_closure_read.py`, `commands/steps/goal-closure.md`, `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.221.2

- fix: **a task the closure reader calls `CLOSE` is now one `vault-cli task complete` will actually close.** The reader measured `# Success Criteria` + `# Definition of Done` only, but the task verb refuses on any open box anywhere on the page (`countCheckboxStates` over `storage.CheckboxRegex`, no fence stripping). Measured 2026-10-30: a goal round read one task `CLOSE`, the verb refused `incomplete subtasks: 1 pending, 0 in-progress` (an open `# Tasks` box), and every later round would have repeated the attempt and the refusal. `--kind task` now also counts the verb's own gate page-wide, prints it as `Whole page (task complete's gate): N open`, and reads `OPEN` while it is non-zero. Goals are untouched — `vault-cli goal complete` checks linked task status, never boxes. Change set: `scripts/goal-closure-read.py`, `scripts/tests/test_goal_closure_read.py`, `commands/steps/goal-closure.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.221.1

- fix: **a dead tab worker no longer blocks resuming its own session.** A tab worker's row is `status: 'interactive'` from spawn and no agent-loop ever runs for it, so nothing settles it to `done`/`error` — and `pruneRehydratedAgents` only touches `rehydrated` rows. Read as a plain live holder, that row blocked a resume of its own session in the spawning server for that server's **whole life**, live or long closed: measured 2026-10-24, two sessions unresumable with no pid, no registry entry and a 42-minute-stale transcript, while every verb that could clear the flag refused *because of* it. The guard's decision now lives in `resume-guard.mjs` as `resumeRefusal({ agentRecords, sessionId, live })` and takes `live` — the one fact that separates the two cases the map cannot: a **tab** is a real `claude` process and is in the registry for as long as it lives, so `live: false` means it is gone and the row is released; a **headless** row is an in-process `query()` the registry cannot always see, so it keeps holding (the case the guard exists for), and `live: null` releases nothing (fail-closed, unchanged). ⚠️ **The release requires EVERY holder for the session to be a dead tab, not just the first one** — and that is not defensive. Releasing a dead tab is what makes its session resumable, and a resume mints a new row while the old `interactive` row is never deleted (`agents` is keyed by `id`), so the map ends up holding `[interactive (stale), running (live)]` for one sessionId, stale-first. Deciding on the first holder alone would find the dead tab, release, and miss the live writer the previous resume created — the two-writers corruption this module exists to prevent. ⚠️ **The safety property is what the release could have broken**, so both directions are asserted in `resume-guard.test.mjs`: widening the release to `live === false` alone turns the headless case red, and removing the release turns the dead-tab case red — each verified by mutation, not by reading the assertions. ⚠️ `registry-rehydrate.test.mjs` pinned the guard's *source shape* (`pruneRehydratedAgents()` immediately followed by an inline `findLiveHolder`), which this move necessarily changes; that assertion is updated to pin the same two properties at the new shape (prune first, every row unfiltered) while the behaviour is tested directly instead. Change set: `server/resume-guard.mjs`, `server/resume-guard.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/registry-rehydrate.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.221.0

- feat: **the fleet tier gets the keeper every other tier already had — and it reads the digest, not the heartbeat.** `worker-keepalive.py` covers workers and `topic-keepalive.py` covers goal and topic managers; the fleet tier had nothing, so a dead fleet round was restarted by no one. Measured 2026-10-30: the round did not complete between 12:38 and 15:09 — **2h30m** — with **116 approved-not-started rows, 100 of them unmanaged, 16 of 30 worker slots free, and output at 363 tok/s against a 1000 target**. ⚠️ **The liveness instrument read `OK` throughout**, because `manager-liveness.py` answers from a heartbeat the model-free gate loop writes every 60s — a launchd job that keeps ticking whether or not a round ever happens. A heartbeat proves the timer works; only the digest proves the round did, so `scripts/fleet-keepalive.py` reads the digest ledger's **last** line and nothing else. Verdicts are `ok` / `stale` / `unknown` with exit 0 / 10 / 2, matching the code `scripts/fleet-digest.py --check` already uses for a lapsed round; `unknown` is fail-closed, because `ok` is what stops a keeper from acting. ⚠️ **An earlier reading in this session reported the fleet manager as dead and asked for a restart — that was wrong**, and the correction is why the signal moved: the pane was alive and idle at a prompt, and the registry's `status: shell` was the misleading input. The keeper deliberately does not probe liveness at all. ⚠️ **The second half of the fix is capability, not detection: `agents/fleet-drive.md` carried `tools: Read, Bash, Write` and `agents/topic-drive.md` carried `tools: Read, Bash` — neither had `Task`, so neither manager could delegate anything and every piece of work it picked up occupied the session that should have been sweeping.** That is the mechanism behind the outage rather than a coincidence: the manager was working an unrelated item in its own session because it had no other place to put it. Both agents gain `Task`, and each states the rule plainly — the sweep is the product, everything else is delegated. Change set: `scripts/fleet-keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_keepalive.py`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `agents/topic-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **a flag now opens what it marks, whoever set it — and the topic tier finally reads one.** Two operator rulings on 2026-10-30, in one change. First: *"in the end it should not matter how set the flag … if flag, should be open if claude session limit allow it"* — so `flag_set_by` stops being a gate and becomes a note. `topic-spawn.py`'s `operator_flagged()` becomes `flagged()` (flag truthy only — `true`/`yes`/`on`), the same predicate moves in `topic-keepalive.py`, and `open-queue.py`'s rank drops the setter check. ⚠️ **The consequence is deliberate and stated in the code**: an agent may flag its own page and get something opened for it, limited only by the cap. That is the trade the ruling makes — reading the setter again would silently reinstate the gate it removed. Second: **a flag on a topic page was read by nothing.** `commands/open.md`'s topic branch gated on `phase: todo` alone, so rule 4's *"flag are fine"* had an executable leg for goal managers (`goal-unflagged`, v0.218.2) and none one tier up — flagging a topic neither opened a manager nor blocked one. `topic-spawn.py` gains `--topic-open`, answering exactly that question with two tokens: `topic-unflagged` (the page carries no flag) and `manager-cap` (the fleet ceiling is reached — the only bound once the flag is present), printing `open-ok: <topic>` on success. `commands/open.md` § Step 2C **item 4** calls it, so the gate is executable rather than prose. ⚠️ **The gate sits on the spawn branch and nowhere earlier** — items 2 (JUMP) and 3 (RESUME) are not opens, and the first cut of this change put the check at step 0b, ahead of manager resolution, which refused a live or resumable manager on an unflagged topic and contradicted the same block's own "restart is not open" line. ⚠️ **Scope is stated rather than implied, and the boundary is the WRITE, not the tier:** `docs/session-tiers.md` § rule 2 now names the three legs that read a flag alone — `--goal` for goal managers, `--topic-open` for topic managers, and `open-queue.py` (the fleet round's open leg) for tasks — because a flag no leg reads is inert, which is the defect this removes. ⚠️ **`/supervisor:open --flagged` is deliberately NOT one of them**, and the doc now says so: that batch's first move is `vault-cli task approve`, which writes `approved_by: operator` onto the row, so reading the flag alone there would let an agent mint an operator approval. It keeps its `flag_set_by: operator|legacy` gate; the sibling surfaces that carried the unqualified "an agent-set flag opens nothing" (`commands/steps/goal-spawn.md`, `commands/steps/rules.md`, `agents/goal-sweep-reader.md`) are scoped to `--flagged` so they stop contradicting the bullet beside them. Change set: `scripts/topic-spawn.py`, `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `scripts/open-queue.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_spawn.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_open_queue.py`, `commands/open.md`, `commands/topic-spawn.md`, `commands/topic-drive.md`, `commands/steps/goal-spawn.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.220.5

- fix: **a dead goal manager is restarted whether or not its goal is flagged, so rule 1 holds on the topic tier again.** `docs/session-tiers.md` § The manager boundary said the topic manager starts a goal manager *"only for a goal … that the operator flagged"* and named `/supervisor:open --goal` as that verb — but `/supervisor:open --goal` is **also the resume verb**, so the bullet read as gating a restart on the flag. Its own next sentence, *"Rule 4 — restart is not open: an unflagged goal is reported and held, never opened"*, named the right distinction and then applied the open's gate to everything. ⚠️ **Measured 2026-10-30 on the `Development Pipeline Throughput` round:** three goal managers dead with recorded ids — `A2A k8s Workers` (10 d), `Operator Oversees` (31 h), `Throughput Is Measured` (8 d) — all reported as *"resume held … all unflagged"* and none restarted; `A2A k8s Workers` read `STALE age=886 999s` before the round and identically after it. `commands/topic-drive.md` § Keep alive defines `resume` as *run `/supervisor:open --goal`* with **no** flag condition, and `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`'s `resume` arm carries none — so the round was applying a gate its own step file does not have. ⚠️ **The split is stated in both homes** — the bullet here and `commands/topic-loop.md`'s matching line, which restates it — because a rule with two homes drifts, and this one had already drifted into a state where rule 1 was inert for every unflagged goal. The flag gates the **open** (no recorded `manager_session_id`, `topic-spawn.py`'s `goal-unflagged`); a goal that already carries an id and whose session is `ABSENT` or whose loop is `STALE` is a **restart**, resumes the same session, and decides nothing about who supervises. Change set: `docs/session-tiers.md`, `commands/topic-loop.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.220.4

- fix: **`session_verdict` reads the liveness instrument's exit code, so `ABSENT` is reachable and the keep-alive can actually resume something.** `scripts/session-liveness.py`'s documented contract is the exit code — `LIVE` (0) / `ABSENT` (1) / `UNKNOWN` (2) / `AMBIGUOUS` (3) / `MALFORMED` (4) — and it prints **only `LIVE` to stdout**, putting every other verdict on stderr. `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`'s `session_verdict` scanned `stdout` for the verdict word instead, so every non-`LIVE` answer fell through to `UNKNOWN`. ⚠️ **`ABSENT` is the only verdict that licenses a resume, so no keep-alive could ever act** — the goal tier that had shipped for months, the topic tier added in `v0.220.0`, and the worker tier that reuses the helper were all inert, and each reported `cannot-tell` for exactly the dead session it exists to recover. This is the defect class the fleet's own rule names — *a documented mandate is not an executed mechanism* — and it sat one layer below where anyone was looking. Measured 2026-10-30 on a killed topic manager: stdout `''`, stderr `'ABSENT — <id>'`, rc 1, verdict `UNKNOWN`; with the fix the same call returns `ABSENT` and the leg prints its `open` line. ⚠️ **An exit code outside the documented set still maps to `UNKNOWN`** — cannot-tell is never death, and that guard is unchanged. ⚠️ **The probe is guarded as well** — a missing interpreter or a hung child returns `UNKNOWN` instead of raising out of the function and aborting the whole tick with a traceback, matching `scripts/crashed-managers.py`'s `session_state()`. Pinned by eight tests that stub the subprocess: the code mapping, the measured empty-stdout shape, an exit code that disagrees with `stdout` (the exact shape the old reader answered `LIVE` to), the argv, and an unrunnable or hung probe. Change set: `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_keepalive.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.220.3

- test: **the resume context gate is exercised by the spawn path's own call, so "no worker is created" is a tested property rather than an inspection.** `spawnAgent`'s resume arm did three things inline — `transcriptPathFor`, `readContextStats`, `resumeContextRefusal` — and a test could only reach the last of them, so the guard's own criterion ("asserts the refusal line is emitted and no `spawn_agent` call is made") rested on reading the code. The three are now one function, `resumeAdmission`, and `context-guard.test.mjs` drives it with fixture transcripts through `readFile`: an above-bound session returns `admit: false` with the reason the caller returns, a below-bound one returns `admit: true`, an unreadable transcript returns `admit: true` (unmeasured is not refused), and a totality test pins `admit === (error === null)` across four shapes — because the caller's branch is `if (!admission.admit) return { error: admission.error }`, and an `admit: false` carrying no error would be a refusal with no reason. The one-line `return` placed before any spawn work remains the "no worker" guarantee, and it is now the only thing standing between the tested gate and a spawn. No behaviour change. Change set: `server/context-guard.mjs`, `server/context-guard.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.220.2

- fix: **the vault scope no longer silences a pane the manager adopted, and a shared goal-page basename no longer replaces or wipes the manager's own set — the two remaining ways the durable input could exclude a gate the manager exists to see.** Both are the fleet-silence direction `session-scope.py`'s own docstring names as the dangerous one, and both were found in the #556 review. ⚠️ **The scope block ran ABOVE the claims path with no `adopted_by_self` guard**, so a pane the manager had explicitly undertaken to route (a live claim) was `EXCLUDE`d instead of reading `own-claim` — contradicting both the hop-3b comment (*"silencing the gate for the one manager that undertook to route it"*) and the scope comment's own promise. `adopted_by_self` is now computed once, above the scope block, and reused by hop 3b, so the three "never silence this" branches (`own-worker`, `self`, `own-claim`) each guard the block that could silence them. ⚠️ **`goals[title] = set(_task_links(text))` OVERWROTE on a basename collision**, and `24 Goals/…` is a basename every `private-*` / `seibert-*` vault reuses; the last vault's `# Tasks` replaced the set `own_titles` is built from, so an own task linked only from the first vault's page was excluded as foreign, and a duplicate page with no `# Tasks` heading wiped the set entirely. It now merges with `setdefault(...).update(...)`, matching `declaring` (which merges) and `managers` (which appends). ⚠️ **The scope CLI seam was unpinned end to end.** Every scope test drove `_FakeScope`, so nothing exercised the real `spec_from_file_location("session_scope", …)` + `load(self_id, vault_paths)` import, the `--scope-vault` and `--no-scope` flags, or the filter's own `SCOPE  unresolved:` line — a rename or signature drift would have passed the suite. `test_gate_owner_filter.ScopeCli` now drives `main()` against the fixture vault with the real pane→session mapping, and pins the filter's distinguishing suffix (`— no row is excluded`) because `session-scope.py` emits a same-prefixed line that an existing test already covers. ⚠️ **The `vault-cli config list` failure branches were untested**, which is exactly the fail-open path the docstring promises: `test_session_scope.VaultDirs` covers the non-zero exit, unparseable JSON and non-list payload, each asserting its OWN reason string so a build returning a different branch's text fails, plus `_probe_root` widening to a stub `SUPERVISOR_VAULTS_ROOT` tree. ⚠️ **`ScopeExclusion`'s docstring said the third verdict "may only turn an EMIT into an EXCLUDE"** while its own test pinned the scope superseding a spawn-edge DROP; reworded to "non-exclude", and narrowed again by this change since an adopted pane is now exempt. Change set: `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/session-scope.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_scope.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.220.1

- fix: **the resume context guard stops refusing healthy sessions — 258 of 3 653 down to 41, with every recorded failure still caught.** v0.216.4's guard refused a resume whenever the restored payload sat within one working set of the point where the session had compacted. Measured 2026-10-30 over every tracked task carrying a `claude_session_id`, that fired on **258** sessions, and the sample showed why it was wrong: `preTokens` is where the harness *compacted*, not where it ran out of window, so a session 10 k from that point simply compacts and carries on — the harness working as designed. Two changes, each with its own evidence. ⚠️ **The ceiling is now measured per model.** A single global maximum mixed windows whenever the router switched models mid-conversation: `7231d34d` compacted at 274 k under `deepseek-v4-flash-max`, then ran its last 315 turns on `claude-opus-5-5` at 690 k, so a 274 k ceiling called it −416 k of room and refused a perfectly healthy resume. A boundary is now attributed to the model in force when it fired, and only boundaries under the session's LAST model can bind. ⚠️ **A refusal now also requires the thrash count** (`DEFAULT_THRASH_COMPACTIONS`, 3): proximity alone no longer refuses. The five recorded failures compacted 35, 24, 10, 3 and 3 times; the healthy session the per-model ceiling rescues compacted twice — so the discriminator is fitted to a 2-versus-3 gap, and that weakness is stated in the constant's own comment rather than hidden. A session that never compacted under the model it ended on is judged by the count, and the refusal says so (`under another model`) instead of implying a measured window it does not have. Verified against the four real transcripts: `21c0b4ff` (22 409 tokens of room), `fc025fc5` (3 669), `ab98608f` (no deepseek boundary, 3 compactions) and `42e1db23` (−337 622) all still refused; `7231d34d` now allowed. Change set: `server/context-guard.mjs`, `server/context-guard.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.220.0

- feat: **The fleet round resumes a worker whose session died, replacing the "orphan → escalate, never nudge" disposition.** `agents/fleet-drive.md` filed every absent session as `blocked` with cause `orphan` and escalated it, so a dead worker was the operator's problem every round while the goal tier already auto-resumed the same population (`agents/goal-drive.md`'s orphan gate). Rule 1 is *a crashed or lapsed manager or worker is restarted automatically* (`docs/session-tiers.md` § The four tiers) — a worker that already owns its task decides nothing new by being resumed. ⚠️ **`scripts/worker-keepalive.py` decides, it never resumes** — it takes one task, reads `status`, the open boxes and `claude_session_id`, probes the id with `session-liveness.py`, and prints `jump` / `resume` / `crash-loop` / `cannot-tell`; the round executes the resume with `/supervisor:open --task`. It reuses `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`'s helpers, cap and store shape (`worker--<slug>.json`), so the two keep-alives cannot drift. ⚠️ **Three worker mechanisms, three disjoint populations, and this is the third** — `scripts/restart-worker.py` restarts a **live, idle** session whose loaded code is stale and refuses `busy-target`/`headless-target`; `scripts/stuck-heal.py` heals a **stuck** headless worker whose permission channel died while the session lives; this leg resumes one whose session is **gone**, which has no pid left to kill and no channel left to reopen. ⚠️ **A task with no recorded id is reported, never opened here** — opening it is rule 3's approved-work path, not this leg's. ⚠️ **`agents/fleet-drive.md` has 3 bytes of headroom against the 30720 body budget** (`wc -c` at this head), so the disposition is one clause and the rationale lives in `commands/steps/keepalive.md` § step 5b's worker half. Change set: `scripts/worker-keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_keepalive.py`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **The fleet round opens the rows the operator flagged, and `flag` now means one thing across the tree.** `/supervisor:open --flagged` had a full spec and no caller, so a flagged row opened only when the operator typed the command by hand. ⚠️ **The field's second meaning and the spawn gate's exclusion shipped separately in `v0.218.2`** (PR #559) — this entry covers only the missing caller, and the two together are what make `flag` mean one thing across the tree. ⚠️ **The caller is `commands/fleet-drive.md` row 5c → `commands/steps/flagged-open.md`**, which **invokes the command** rather than re-implementing its selector: `commands/open.md` owns the vault scan, the due-date rule, the `OPENING_ACTORS` gate and the `REFUSED` line, and a second copy would drift from it. ⚠️ **The three rule files that said the loop must never take this path are corrected in the same change** — `commands/steps/rules.md:25` and `agents/goal-drive.md:293` now name row 5c as the leg that runs it, so the shipped caller and the written rule cannot disagree. ⚠️ **`flag_set_by` is untouched and still refuses an agent-set *and* an absent value** — the leg executes the operator's mark, it does not widen who may write one. `commands/fleet-drive.md` was at its 10240-byte tick budget, so the two new rows were paid for by dropping a sentence that restated its own preceding clause. Change set: `commands/steps/flagged-open.md`, `commands/steps/rules.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `agents/goal-drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **The fleet round keeps topic managers alive, closing the one rung of the manager tree that had no keep-alive at all.** `scripts/topic-keepalive.py` served the goal tier only — it hardcoded `--branch goal`, read `24 Goals` and wrote `goal--<slug>.json` — so a lapsed topic manager stayed lapsed and nothing reported it, while the tier below it restarted automatically. The script now takes `--branch goal|topic` (default `goal`, `--goal` kept as an alias so every existing caller is unchanged), reads `23 Topics` on the topic branch, and writes a `topic--<slug>.json` cap of its own — a topic resume must not spend the goal tier's budget. `commands/fleet-drive.md` gains router row 5b → `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, which enumerates the topic pages carrying a `manager_session_id` and acts on the verdict exactly as the goal tier does. ⚠️ **This is a restart, not a proposal.** The row resumes a topic manager that already has a subject and a recorded id; it never starts one where none existed, which is the right `fleet-drive.md` removed on 2026-10-29 and rule 4 of `docs/session-tiers.md`. ⚠️ **`STOPPED` is left alone** — `topic-resolve.md` § 4c writes that marker for a deliberate stand-down on a non-`execution` subject, and restarting one would turn a refused arm into a restart loop that still runs a round every tick. ⚠️ **The cap counts only resumes that took**, via `--record-resume`, so a resume that never landed does not arm the cap against its own retry. `commands/fleet-drive.md` was at its 10240-byte tick budget, so the row is one line and the rationale lives in the step file; the superseded "(operator ruling)" clause in its manager-proposal note was dropped to pay for it, the provenance being in this entry and in `docs/session-tiers.md`. Change set: `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_keepalive.py`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `commands/topic-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **`docs/session-tiers.md` states the automation boundary as four rules, and three restatements of the superseded "no lower-tier manager owns the subject" clause are corrected with it.** The operator's 2026-10-30 ruling was recorded in the vault and in `commands/fleet-drive.md`'s router note but never in the doc every manager reads, so the doc still granted the fleet manager the right to open a goal manager that the fleet round had removed on 2026-10-29 — the tree and the legs disagreed, and the doc was the stale half. ⚠️ **The distinction the rules hang on is "restart is not open":** a restart resumes a session that already has a subject and a recorded id, while an open creates supervision where none existed, so restarting a broken manager decides nothing new and opening one decides who supervises. Four rules follow — a crashed or lapsed manager or worker is restarted automatically; a flagged page is opened by the tier above it when `flag_set_by` is `operator` or `legacy` (absent refused, per `commands/open.md`); an approved worker is opened up to `spawn.maxConcurrent`; and **a manager is never opened fresh on the operator's behalf**. ⚠️ **Rule 4 binds every tier**, which is why the tier table's `May start` cells now read "a goal manager only when that goal is flagged" rather than "when no lower-tier manager owns the subject". ⚠️ **`flag` is also stated as carrying one meaning tree-wide** — it marks a page the operator wants opened, and a leg that reads it as "parked / waiting-on-human" and excludes it inverts the operator's mark; the parked class needs its own marker. The two restatements in `docs/fleet-surface.md` and `commands/read-guides.md` are edited in the same change because a copied rule is exactly what drifts, and `read-guides.md` is the surface that tells every session what its tier may open. Change set: `docs/session-tiers.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `commands/read-guides.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **the closure rule now honours the vault's DoD grandfathering cutoff, so a grandfathered goal can close again.** `v0.217.0` shipped a closure predicate requiring a `# Definition of Done` on every goal, while the vault decided on 2026-10-07 that goals created before `DOD_REQUIRED_AS_OF` (`2026-06-26`) are grandfathered and carry none. The two rules could not both hold, and the closure rule was the one that could never agree with the rest of the vault: a grandfathered goal was closable by every other reader and unclosable by the one that actually closes it. Measured 2026-10-30 with the deployed reader over all `in_progress` pages — **0 CLOSE, 31 OPEN, 14 UNMEASURABLE**, nothing at either tier able to close; **12 of 39** `in_progress` goals carry no `# Definition of Done` heading at all (verified by heading grep, so it is absence and not a parser miss), of which **8 are grandfathered** and **4 are post-cutoff violations**. ⚠️ **`scripts/goal-closure-read.py` gains the cutoff as two changes, not one:** `verdict_of` received no date, so `read_created()` parses `created:` from the same frontmatter the status read already opens, and `dod_exempt(kind, created)` — the single named home for the policy — is threaded in as `verdict_of(status, sections, dod_may_be_absent)`. Lexicographic comparison against the ISO-8601 date the vault writes needs no parsing and no timezone, and a `created` on the cutoff date itself reads as post-cutoff. ⚠️ **The exception is deliberately narrow, and each boundary is a test:** **absence only** — a DoD heading that exists but holds no box stays `UNMEASURABLE` at every cutoff, because grandfathering accepts that the section was never written, not that it was written and left blank; and **goals only** — a task's DoD requirement was never part of the decision, and a topic closes on `# Completion Gate`, which has no cutoff at all, so widening it to every kind would close pages on evidence the vault never agreed to accept. A goal with open Success Criteria never reads `CLOSE` at any age. ⚠️ **The reader's absent-but-exempt line is distinct from `MISSING` on purpose** — `Definition of Done: ABSENT — grandfathered (created <date> < 2026-06-26)` — so a close it now permits is never mistaken for a section it failed to find. ⚠️ **The cutoff is stated at its call site** in `commands/steps/goal-closure.md`, naming the constant, the date and why the branch exists, so the next reader of the closure step does not re-derive it; `DOD_REQUIRED_AS_OF` now appears in this repo, which is the probe the task file carries. The date is restated in four places — the script constant, `commands/steps/goal-closure.md`, this file, and the vault's `agents/goal-auditor.md` § 12 — because a plugin script cannot import from a vault agent; change them together. ⚠️ **`created` is compared only when it carries a zero-padded ISO-8601 prefix.** The comparison is lexicographic, which is exact for `YYYY-MM-DD` and for a timestamp on that prefix — but `15.01.2026` and a bare `2026` both sort BELOW the cutoff and would have exempted a page that is not grandfathered. A false CLOSE is the expensive direction, so an unrecognised shape is refused rather than sorted. ⚠️ **Exemption needs absence, and absence is decided by a loose search plus a parse guard, both failing closed.** `read_created` returns `None` for an absent field *and* for a page whose frontmatter never parsed, so without a parse check a BOM-prefixed or unterminated-frontmatter page would read as "no `created`" and close; and `section_of` matches only an exact H1, so an `## Definition of Done` or a `# Definition of Done (optional)` holding an unticked box would read as absent. Exemption therefore requires the frontmatter to have parsed **and** no line of the page to begin with the section name, once leading whitespace, blockquote markers, `#`s and emphasis are stripped — which covers ATX at any level, indentation or suffix, setext, blockquote and bold headings alike, because it does not parse heading syntax at all. Three rounds of a regex over heading shapes each missed a spelling (h2, then a suffix, then indentation, then setext, then a suffixed setext) and each miss was a false CLOSE; a line-normalising check cannot drift from the parser it shadows. A mid-sentence mention is not matched, so prose about the section does not over-refuse a goal that genuinely has none. `normalise` also strips a leading BOM, the sibling of the CRLF fold that already exists for the same reason. Change set: `scripts/goal-closure-read.py`, `scripts/tests/test_goal_closure_read.py`, `commands/steps/goal-closure.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- docs: **the surfaces that describe the operator-flag gate now agree with the executable one.** The bot review of the rule-4 PR called these non-blocking; they are the same defect class that PR exists to remove — a documented mandate that is not the executed mechanism — so they land here. `commands/topic-loop.md` still said *"start what the ranking admits"* and *"No goal manager → start one, under the ranking"* with no flag condition, leaving the deployed tree carrying two contradictory statements of the topic tier's start rule; it now states rule 4 and points at the executable gate. `agents/topic-drive.md` Step 2 rendered every non-`start` row as `N slots full` or `phase todo`, so an unflagged goal with a free slot got a lever the operator could not pull — it now renders the fourth `unflagged` slot, and the output block carries the `open` / `open-unflagged` verdict lines. `commands/topic-spawn.md` described `--rank` as emitting `start`/`held` only and `<n>` as every startable row; it now documents the `unflagged` mark and that `<n>` counts only the flagged rows. ⚠️ **The gate's premise is now measured rather than assumed.** Its cited evidence (`docs/task-writing.md` § The approval flag, `commands/open.md` § Step 0.5) is task-scoped and no artifact showed a goal page carrying the pair; measured 2026-10-30 in the primary vault, **0 of 355 goal pages carry `flag: true` and 0 carry `flag_set_by` at all** — so the gate's live effect is total, and that is the operator's ruling working as stated rather than a gap in it. `scripts/topic-spawn.py`'s docstring gains the `goal-terminal` and `goal-unflagged` tokens it was omitting, `commands/topic-spawn.md`'s frontmatter description gains the same two so it stops disagreeing with the verdict table below it, and three tests pin the mixed-topic `--rank` header count, the printed `unflagged` slot, and `flag: yes` through the rank path. ⚠️ **The `goal-terminal` bullet first written here claimed `hold` was terminal, and the code says otherwise** — `TERMINAL_STATUSES` is `("completed", "aborted")`, so a held goal in a startable phase still passes the gate. The bullet now states the real set and records that whether a held goal should be openable is an open question this gate does not decide, rather than documenting behaviour the code does not implement — the same defect class the whole change exists to remove. `commands/topic-drive.md`'s frontmatter description carried the identical unqualified "start what the ranking admits" phrase, so it moves with `commands/topic-loop.md`, and `agents/topic-drive.md`'s output contract is restructured so *"exactly one line per declared goal"* is structural rather than a precedence list: Step 2 no longer emits rows of its own, it appends `· rank <i>, <slot>` to the line Step 1 already produced, so the cap (`held`) and the flag (`unflagged`) each show in exactly one place and two independent passes cannot overlap. ⚠️ **That restructure is the third attempt and the first that holds.** A precedence list came first, and each review round then found another overlapping pair (`To open` against `Held: … N slots full`, `Live` against `Held: … no operator flag`); a blanket "Step 1 wins" rule in between made Step 2's rows unreachable and silently dropped the over-cap signal. A contract that relies on enumerating every collision is a contract that will keep having them. A positive-control test pins that a `status: hold` goal still passes the gate, so the corrected docstring cannot drift back, and `commands/topic-loop.md` gains the `argument-hint` its own frontmatter comment requires. ⚠️ **Two over-corrections the review caught are fixed here as well.** The precedence paragraph first claimed Step 2 was wholly subsumed by Step 1 — but Step 1 lines *every* declared goal, so that made `To start` and `Held: … N slots full` unreachable and silently dropped the over-cap signal a `held` row carries. And the phrase this bullet says it removed still survived in `CLAUDE.md`'s layout map, so it moves too — alongside the two refusal lists that named only three tokens (`docs/session-tiers.md` § A topic manager, `commands/topic-drive.md` § Start). Change set: `CLAUDE.md`, `commands/topic-loop.md`, `commands/topic-drive.md`, `commands/topic-spawn.md`, `agents/topic-drive.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `scripts/topic-spawn.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_spawn.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.219.0

- feat: **the fleet round now opens the approved queue itself, ranked, up to the free slots — closing the last hop of a measure-then-delegate design whose delegates were all absent.** Measured 2026-10-30 09:45: **133 approved rows waiting, 8 of 8 owning managers reading `STALE` / `UNARMED` / `STOPPED`, 17 free worker slots.** The round had read that queue every tick since 2026-10-01 and stated the design outright — *"The line reports; it never opens"* — while the opening was delegated to managers that were not there. New router row 16 (`commands/steps/open.md`) runs `scripts/open-queue.py`, which ranks the queue **operator-flag first, then `priority`, then oldest `approved_at`**, applies the eligibility guards, and admits the top-N the free slots allow; the round then opens those rows **in the printed order** through `/supervisor:open`, whose own gates (approval, the readiness ladder, the cap) are unchanged — the leg ranks, that command decides admissibility. ⚠️ **The population is delegated, not re-derived.** `open-queue.py` calls `approved-not-started.py` rather than re-reading `approved_at`/`status`/`claude_session_id`: two readers of one predicate is the defect `session-liveness.py`'s header records, so the live-session rule stays in one home and this script adds only the guards (`phase: todo`, a `blocked_by`, a future `defer_date`, an unreadable file), the rank, and the cap. ⚠️ **The rank is the claim, not the count.** A round that opens `free` arbitrary rows and prints `skipped: capacity` satisfies a count and violates the 2026-10-01 ruling (*"approving and starting all of them is not a good idea"*), so the skipped rows are counted by reason and the opened set prints in rank order. ⚠️ **Operator-flag means `flag: true` AND `flag_set_by: operator`.** A bare `flag: true` is not an approval and does not rank — the same rule `commands/open.md` § Step 0.5 applies from the selector's side. ⚠️ **Zero free slots has its own literal line** (`no free slots — selected 0`), `unknown` is a third answer that opens nothing, and a **`target unlimited`** config (`spawn.maxConcurrent: 0`) also opens nothing — a removed cap is not a licence to open the whole queue, so the leg names the config state instead of inventing a per-round number. ⚠️ **`commands/steps/output-shape.md` gains item 3** so the block actually renders; without it a round following the canonical render spec dropped the leg's only observability. ⚠️ **The block reports a SELECTION, not an outcome** (`selected N`): the round opens afterwards through `/supervisor:open`, which may refuse a row at its own gates, so a head reading `opened` would claim a result the leg never measured. ⚠️ **`blocked_by` is read in BOTH frontmatter shapes** — the inline scalar and the multi-line block list this vault actually writes — because a scalar-only read returns empty for the block form and the guard then never fires, which is the measured defect `next-focus.py` records for its own hold-goal class; here the consequence would be a spawn. ⚠️ **The round passes `--vault`** alongside `--task`, since task titles are not namespaced across vaults and the population and the action must name one. ⚠️ **Five correctness defects the review caught, all fixed here:** `field()`/`has_blocked_by()` used `\s*` after the key, which matches `\n` — an empty `phase:` or `defer_date:` swallowed the line below and read the NEXT key as its value; the age tie-break compared raw `approved_at` strings across differing UTC offsets instead of the normalised `age_seconds`; `blocked_by: []` read as a blocker; and a missing `--tasks-dir` printed only to stderr, so the round had no `open leg: unknown` to print. ⚠️ **One invocation, two fields.** `--json` carries the rendered block (`render`) alongside the quoted actions, as `next-focus.py` does — a step needing both from two runs would print one tick's block and run another tick's rows. ⚠️ **The leg never spawns, arms or resumes a manager** (operator ruling 2026-10-30: *"we dont want to auto open topic-manager, goal-manager ... but flag are fine"*). `commands/steps/readings.md`'s three *"the line reports; it never opens/arms"* statements are amended to name which leg acts — the arms clause keeps its meaning and gains the manager-vs-worker distinction, since opening a worker arms no manager. Router rows 16–18 shifted to 17–19. Change set: `scripts/open-queue.py`, `scripts/tests/test_open_queue.py`, `commands/steps/open.md`, `commands/steps/readings.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `commands/steps/voice-notify.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.218.2

- fix: **the topic tier stops opening goal managers, and `goal-spawn` stops excluding flagged rows — the two places the deployed tree still contradicted the operator's 2026-10-30 automation ruling.** *"we dont want to auto open topic-manager, goal-manager ... but flag are fine"*, and *"if a topic manager or goal manager is crashing, then we want to auto restart it"*. The organising distinction is that **restart is not open**: a restart resumes a session that already has a subject and a recorded id, while an open creates supervision where none existed. Three edits follow it. **`commands/topic-drive.md` § Start** is now gated on the flag — a goal is startable for that row only when `flag: true` carries `flag_set_by: operator` or `legacy`, and an unflagged goal with a free slot is *reported with its rank*, never started, because an unmanaged goal still gets its **workers** opened by the fleet round's open leg so the work moves without a manager. **Its keep-alive `resume` arm** splits into two cases: a recorded `manager_session_id` means the session is gone but named, so resuming it is allowed; no recorded id at all is an **open**, which rule 4 forbids unless the goal is flagged. **`commands/steps/goal-spawn.md`** drops the clause that excluded `flag: true` as *"a parked/waiting-on-human task"* — `bborbe/vault-cli` `docs/task-writing.md` § The approval flag defines it as a task the operator **wants picked up**, so the exclusion inverted the operator's meaning; measured 2026-10-30, of 684 flagged tasks in the primary vault **0** were `human_review` or `hold` and **632** were already `completed`, so the clause never expressed the parked class and the two fields beside it do. ⚠️ **The gate is executable, not documented:** `scripts/topic-spawn.py` gains `operator_flagged()` and a fourth refusal token, `goal-unflagged`, checked in **both** `rank()` and the `--goal` startability gate — so a flagged goal must carry `flag_set_by: operator` or `legacy` (193 rows predate the field), and an absent setter is refused because a writer that did not name itself is not evidence the operator asked. Five tests cover the refusal, the non-operator setter, the absent setter, the legacy pass, and the rank exclusion. ⚠️ **The review found the keep-alive split was prose-only, and the fix carries it into the executable surface.** `scripts/topic-keepalive.py` printed `resume` for BOTH the recorded-id-but-dead case and the no-recorded-id case, and `agents/topic-drive.md` mapped every `resume` to `/supervisor:open --goal` — a verb with no flag gate — so an unflagged goal manager was still opened there. The verdict now splits: `open` when the goal is flagged, `open-unflagged` when it is not, and `resume` keeps only its true case (a recorded id whose session is `ABSENT`). ⚠️ **An unflagged goal is now RANKED rather than dropped.** `rank()` dropping it made "render it with its rank" unexecutable, since `--rank` then emitted no row to render: it keeps the row carrying a fourth slot, `unflagged`, which can never take one of the N slots, and the `rank:` header counts only the rows that may start. ⚠️ **Two robustness fixes the review also named.** `operator_flagged()` accepts `yes`/`on` as well as `true` — `operator_pin` beside it already did, so a goal the operator flagged with `flag: yes` was reported as held. And `scalar()` in both scripts uses `[ \t]*` instead of `\s*`: `\s` matches the newline, so an EMPTY `manager_session_id:` swallowed the line break and returned the NEXT key's line as its value, making the keep-alive resume a session that never existed. The new `goal-unflagged` token is documented in `commands/topic-spawn.md`'s verdict table alongside the pre-existing `goal-terminal`, and `commands/steps/goal-spawn.md` now states the `flag_set_by: operator|legacy` pair rather than the flag alone. Change set: `commands/topic-drive.md`, `commands/steps/goal-spawn.md`, `commands/topic-spawn.md`, `agents/topic-drive.md`, `scripts/topic-spawn.py`, `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_spawn.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_keepalive.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.218.1

- fix: **the attention arm no longer hands a manager another goal's gates, and it names the real owner instead of dropping the row.** `gate-owner-filter.py` resolved ownership from the spawn edge plus liveness only — a live peer manager, a live manager's own gate, a live claim — and every one of those inputs is a live store that fails open, so a dead spawner, an unresolved `parent_session` or an unreadable ledger all landed on `EMIT`. Measured 2026-10-29 on goal manager `49b471f1`: **five foreign gates in one afternoon, across five sessions and four goals** — panes 269/`695c311f`, 260/`0eacc5e4`, 272/`2767ba96`, 278/`0b7d28a7`, 279/`51e78933`, each resolved by hand to a task whose `goals:` names another goal, none in that goal's tracked set. The same five read `peer-manager` on a later live run, so the filter's verdict for one gate flipped with the health of a manager that did not own it — and it could never name the owner, because `verdict()` has no notion of goals or tasks at all. New `scripts/session-scope.py` supplies that notion from the VAULT, which is durable: `--self` resolves to a goal page's `manager_session_id` (or a task's `claude_session_id`), the manager's scope is that goal's `# Tasks` list widened by every task declaring the goal, and each gated session resolves to the page it is stamped on — including through a `- session_id:` metrics entry, the only stamp one of the five cases carries. ⚠️ **An exclusion is NOT a drop, and the third verdict says so.** The row stays in `--explain --json` with `foreign-goal:<goal>` (or `owner-unknown` when the owning task declares no goal) and `counts_line()` reports it in its own `excluded(foreign-goal)` / `excluded(owner-unknown)` buckets, because a row that disappears from a sweep is indistinguishable from a row that got fixed. ⚠️ **Unresolvable is never foreign:** a manager with no page, a session stamped on two pages, a session with no page, and an unreadable vault list all exclude nothing and print one `SCOPE  unresolved:` line — the same fail-open asymmetry every other hop takes, since excluding on a failed read is a fleet-wide silence produced by a missing page. ⚠️ **The scope check sits above the spawner path on purpose:** for a positively-foreign pane the spawn edge may say `peer-manager`, `dead-manager` or `unowned` for the same row, and which one it says is exactly the fact that proved unstable; the durable answer wins, and it is the only one that names the real owner. The read is arm-time (35 159 task pages, ~7 s), so a page stamped mid-arm keeps its gate until the next re-arm; a topic manager has no scope yet and therefore excludes nothing. `--no-scope` restores the old behaviour exactly. Change set: `scripts/session-scope.py`, `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_scope.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.218.0

- feat: **`closer-gate-lint.py` now allow-lists `/vault-cli:session-close` and the plain `gh pr merge`, so two measured closer families stop costing the operator a gate.** Operator decision 2026-10-30, taken per class on a 7-day reading that found four already-authorised-verb families recurring across 2+ sessions and 2+ days — `gh … pr merge` (17 sessions across 8 repo children), `compact this session` (6), `/vault-cli:session-close` (6) and `git worktree remove` (12). Session-close and green-PR merge have a documented no-ask basis in `CLAUDE.md` (`§ Async State Closer` names the closer as the defect outright; `§ After Opening a PR § Merge on Green` says "merge without asking"); the merge entry had been withheld because this file read `§ Execution Phase No Re-Ask`'s do-not-weaken list as keeping every merge gated, leaving the two rules contradicting each other. ⚠️ **`--admin` stays gated, and the exclusion is global rather than a lookahead inside the merge entry.** `match_allow` returns the FIRST match, so a per-pattern lookahead let `approve: /vault-cli:session-close then gh pr merge 60 --merge --admin` through on the session-close entry — a bypass this change introduced and the review caught. `DENY` is checked before `ALLOW` is walked, making the exclusion independent of both the entry order and which verb the closer names first; pinned by `14-gh-pr-merge-admin.jsonl` and `15-session-close-with-admin-merge.jsonl`, both asserting exit **0** / `not-allow-listed` so the operator's gate stands. ⚠️ **The two arms are opposite exit codes and the earlier text had them backwards:** allow-listed returns 2 (Stop blocked, turn continues), non-authorised returns 0 (Stop allowed, gate stands). ⚠️ **The emitted message no longer calls the step reversible.** It said "authorise for a reversible, in-scope, non-production step" *and* "keep the line if the action is irreversible" — self-contradictory on the exact closer this change releases, since a merge is irreversible; a careful model resolved it by keeping the gate, reinstating the cost the entry removes. The message now names the **precondition the rules attach** (`WHEN`), which the verb-based hook cannot check, and the escape clause keys on that precondition not holding. ⚠️ **Compaction and `git worktree remove` were measured in the same decision and deliberately left gated** — neither has a no-ask basis in `CLAUDE.md` (zero occurrences of either verb), so allow-listing either means writing a new rule first — pinned as still-gated by `16-compact-stays-gated.jsonl` and `17-worktree-remove-stays-gated.jsonl`. `hooks/hooks.json`'s duplicate verb list and `docs/closer-classes.md` test 3 move in lockstep; the latter keeps its `merge` / `session-close` entries, because it governs what the fleet sweep must treat as a gate, which the lint narrows but does not change. Change set: `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/13-vault-cli-session-close.jsonl`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/14-gh-pr-merge-admin.jsonl`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/15-session-close-with-admin-merge.jsonl`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/16-compact-stays-gated.jsonl`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/17-worktree-remove-stays-gated.jsonl`, `hooks/hooks.json`, `docs/closer-classes.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. ⚠️ `09-gh-pr-merge.jsonl` is byte-identical to master — only its expected exit code moved (0 → 2) in `test_closer_gate_lint.py`, so it is not in the change set.

## v0.217.5

- fix: **the quiet check no longer counts `status: next` as unsurfaced, so a subject full of approved rows no longer stands its own manager down.** `vault-cli task approve` writes `phase: todo -> planning` and deliberately leaves `status` at `next` — "the row stays queued, not owned" (`vault-cli` `1804f59`, 2026-10-09) — so every row approved since then carried `status: next`. `scripts/manager-quiet.py` read that status as *not surfaced*, which made a subject holding nine approved, unblocked, undeferred, ownerless rows read as **empty** to the stand-down counter: `QUIET_TICKS_TO_STAND_DOWN` ticks later the manager retired itself and drove none of them, and nothing announced the loss. Measured 2026-10-30 on goal `The Operator Inbox Is Small and Fully Owned`: `armed 1 10 0` before, `armed 10 1 0` after. ⚠️ **The approval boundary moved to `phase: todo`, the field `approve` actually writes** — admitting `next` without that guard would have surfaced the operator's whole unapproved inbox (858 rows in one vault read at `status: next` + `phase: todo`), so `UNAPPROVED_PHASE` is new work here, not a preserved property: the file contained **no `phase` read at all** before this change. ⚠️ **This makes the third renderer agree with the other two.** The `Manager Session` runbook names `agents/goal-sweep-reader.md` and `sweep-gate.py`'s `bucket()` as the pair that must agree — and they already did — while `manager-quiet.py` was an unnamed copy that did not; the predicate now mirrors `_classify`'s arm exactly (`status == "next" or (in_progress and phase == "planning")`), including the deliberate retention of unphased `next` rows that a `phase`-only predicate would drop silently. ⚠️ **`backlog` and `hold` stay quiet** — someday and paused-with-no-resume-date are genuinely not driveable, and no approve path writes them. ⚠️ **The docstring's quiet-shapes list was updated in the same edit**, since it is normative prose that stated the rule being reversed. Change set: `scripts/manager-quiet.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_quiet.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.217.4

- fix: **the drive agent's `<output_format>` now carries the re-read stamp, the drop line and the degraded line, so the re-read can actually be rendered.** The agent body said *"Return exactly this"* and its `Waiting on your keystroke` block had no slot for any of the three, while `docs/blocked-row-reread.md` made all three load-bearing — so a literal reading of the format dropped a closed `BLOCKED` row silently, the exact "section quietly shrinks" failure the rule forbids. The section header now carries `re-read <stamp|failed: <why> — rows unverified>`, and a per-row `re-read <stamp>: no open gate for <name> [pane <id>]` line records each drop. ⚠️ **Paid for in the same file, not by a baseline entry:** `agents/fleet-drive.md` sits at 30,705 of the 30,720-byte body budget, so verbose clauses were tightened to make room; `check-budget` stays green with no ratchet. ⚠️ **All four doc-mandated markers now have a slot**, the fourth being `no pane to re-read` on the numbered-row line, which had none. ⚠️ **Two review nits fixed alongside:** `docs/blocked-row-reread.md` mis-attributed `pane —` to the digest's link column — it is the pane-column failure form of the fleet-drive `ESCALATION` row, and the digest's link column fails as a bare `—`; and `commands/steps/voice-notify.md` ran `crashed-managers.py --json` then `--post`, paying the probe cost twice while nothing consumed the `--json` output, so the routine round now runs `--post` alone. A second review round then caught three cosmetic nits this change had itself introduced — the doc's *"with its live stamp"* sentence, *"beneath the section"*, and *"the resolved rows"* — all corrected here, the last by restoring *"rows whose pane resolved"*. Change set: `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `docs/blocked-row-reread.md`, `commands/steps/voice-notify.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.217.3

- fix: **a manager that is live but whose loop lapsed now raises one card — where nothing else covers it.** The round's only manager signal since the proposal row was removed is this recovery one. `scripts/crashed-managers.py` reads the `<branch>--<base>.cadence` records the round already writes, classifies each subject with `manager-liveness.py --state`, and cards the live-but-lapsed ones. ⚠️ **Three conditions, each a way the card must not fire:** `STALE` only (`STOPPED` is a stand-down, not a crash); `session-liveness.py --check` on the subject page's `manager_session_id` reads **`LIVE`** (`ABSENT` is an abandoned record; `UNKNOWN`/`AMBIGUOUS`/`MALFORMED` are unverifiable, never silently dropped); and **no keep-alive already covers it** — `topic-keepalive.py` restarts lapsed goal managers from the topic tier, uncapped, so a card there would duplicate an action already taken (operator decision 2026-10-29). ⚠️ **Coverage is the topic's CADENCE, not its session:** `/supervisor:stop` disarms the loop and leaves the session `LIVE`, so a stopped topic manager would otherwise read as covering while restarting nothing. Uncovered is therefore a topic manager, a goal manager whose topic has no live manager, or a fleet manager. ⚠️ The predicate is imported from `topic-keepalive.py`, never forked. Flat pre-split records carry no branch, so they are **counted, never carded** — 46 legacy records against 43 attributed today (89 in the directory), which is why a naive per-`STALE` rule would have flooded the board with 18. Measured live 2026-10-29: 43 attributed, **1 card** (`b9a2ea68`, `age=78742s limit=4648s`) — a second manager the motivating round named has since recovered, which is why a proof run must quote its own reading. ⚠️ **The card names the age-vs-limit**, because "live but lapsed" without the margin is not actionable. ⚠️ **Hardening in the same change:** an unreadable vault, goals dir or topic page **refuses** (exit 2) rather than reading as an abandoned record; `declares()` is scoped to the page's `## Goals` section, since real topic pages carry `## Non-goals` immediately after it and a whole-file `[[goal]]` match would suppress a genuine crash; probes are bounded (`PROBE_TIMEOUT` 30 s, `POST_TIMEOUT` 60 s); the dedup key carries the branch; the helper import is lazy and its failure is a `REFUSED`, not a traceback. ⚠️ **The session probe reads `session-liveness.py`'s exit codes, not its stdout** — `LIVE` is the only verdict on stdout, everything else goes to stderr, so a stdout-parsing caller can never see `ABSENT`. The card is an ASK. Change set: `scripts/crashed-managers.py`, `scripts/tests/test_crashed_managers.py`, `commands/steps/voice-notify.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **a blocked row is re-read against the live probe before the round renders it, so a gate that already closed stops reading as "waiting on your keystroke".** The `BLOCKED` section is a snapshot the sweep wrote before the drive leg reads it; measured 2026-10-29, three of four rows had resolved by render time and telling a real ask from a stale one cost three pane checks by hand. `commands/steps/drive.md` now runs one `who-needs-me.py --section needs-you --section board-missing --all` read per round and passes the confirmed pane ids to the drive agent, which matches each row on **pane id** — the only key both the digest's `BLOCKED` row and the probe carry, since neither has a session id — and the wall clock stamped at the moment the probe runs. ⚠️ **Both directions are load-bearing and each guards the other:** a `BLOCKED` row the probe does not list is dropped *and the drop is printed* (`re-read <stamp>: no open gate for <name> [pane <id>]`), because a section that quietly shrinks is indistinguishable from a fleet that never had a gate; and a gate the probe does list still renders with its live stamp, so deleting the section is not a way to satisfy this. ⚠️ **A degraded probe leaves every row standing** with `re-read failed: <why> — rows unverified` — an unreadable store is not a clearance, and folding it into "no open gate" is the one direction that would let a real ask vanish. ⚠️ **Exit status alone cannot detect it:** on an unreachable store `who-needs-me.py` prints `store unreachable — reading event log` and **exits 0**, so the degraded line is the signal, not the code — otherwise an empty fallback drops every `BLOCKED` row. ⚠️ **The drop is scoped to `BLOCKED`-sourced rows, because the probe can see only those:** `who-needs-me.py`'s `needs-you` carries store records of kind permission/question, so a chat-only `CLOSERS` ask — a session that ended its turn on a `pick` / `review:` / `you run:` — is never listed there, and dropping it on a miss would delete a live ask. Change set: `commands/steps/drive.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `docs/blocked-row-reread.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.217.2

- fix: **`changelog-archive.py` extends the archive instead of replacing it.** A rotation that actually cut wrote `ARCHIVE_HEADER + archived` straight over `CHANGELOG-archive.md`, so every section an earlier rotation had archived vanished from the repository — observed 2026-10-29, when a real second rotation dropped **316 released sections (v0.102.3 … v0.1.0)** and nothing else in the tree held them. ⚠️ **The suite was green throughout, and why is the point:** the only second-run test was `test_second_run_is_a_no_op`, whose second run finds nothing to cut and returns *before* the archive write — so the overwrite branch was never executed by a test. `merge_archive()` now prepends the prior archive's sections, deduped by heading so a repeated run cannot duplicate one, and separates the junction by a blank line; `test_a_second_rotation_extends_the_archive_instead_of_replacing_it` grows the changelog again with strictly newer versions so the second run really rotates, and asserts ordering, retention and the junction. The archive is also written **before** `CHANGELOG.md` is truncated, so a failed write duplicates at worst and never drops the freshly cut sections. Change set: `scripts/changelog-archive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_changelog_archive.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.217.1

- fix: **the drive round's `UNVERIFIABLE` block reaches both of the round's artifacts, and a round that loses it fails loudly instead of reading as quiet.** Measured 2026-10-29 on round `T1358`: `grep -c UNVERIFIABLE` was 0 in both the `.digest` and the `.drive` while `scripts/drive-unverifiable.py` had produced rows — the agent pasted the nudge and escalation lines, but the block had no home: neither the payload template (`agents/fleet-drive.md`) nor the round's print step (`commands/steps/drive.md`) named one. The template now carries an `UNVERIFIABLE` section, the round prints it first (always, including `(none)`), and `handle --digest` mirrors the same marker-delimited block into the round's `.digest`, so a reader of either artifact can tell "nothing found" from "the block never arrived". `scripts/check-drive-unverifiable-paste.py` is the gate — static assertions plus a `--digest`/`--drive` pair check the round runs, so a lost paste is reported in that round. Change set: `scripts/drive-unverifiable.py`, `scripts/check-drive-unverifiable-paste.py`, both new tests, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `Makefile`.

_Releases older than v0.217.0 (v0.140.1 … v0.217.0) are archived in [CHANGELOG-archive.md](CHANGELOG-archive.md)._
